The Great King of Yoginī Tantras called the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas (Śrīḍākārṇavamahāyoginītantrarājanāma)

The Great King of Yoginī Tantras called the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, known in Sanskrit as the Śrī Ḍākārṇava Mahāyoginī Tantrarāja and in Tibetan as Pal Khandro Gyatso Naljorma'i Gyü kyi Gyalpo Chenpo, is Toh 372 in the Kangyur, where it belongs to the Tantra section among the yoginī tantras associated with the Cakrasaṃvara cycle. It opens in the manner of a sūtra, with the Blessed One, the Lord of Great Heroes, at play among the yoginīs of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, and goes on to teach in verse across some fifty-one chapters. Its subjects include the maṇḍala of the heroes and yoginīs, the twenty-four sacred sites and the accomplishments they bestow, the yogic correlation of breath, time and the inner elements, offerings, feasts and pledges, and the coemergent great bliss that unites emptiness and appearance. Its colophon records that it was translated in Nepal by the Indian paṇḍita Jayasena together with the Tibetan translator Dharma Yönten, and later revised.

In the language of India: śrī ḍākārṇṇaba mahā yoginī tantra rāja nāma. In the language of Tibet: The Great King of Yoginī Tantras called the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas (Ḍākārṇava).

oṃ Homage to the Buddha, the Hero!
Homage to the Blessed One, the Lord of Speech!

Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One, the Lord of Great Heroes, was engaged in play within the bhagas of the yoginīs of the body, speech, and mind of the heroes of all the tathāgatas. There the Lord of Great Heroes declared:

"Through the meditative absorption of great bliss,
I see the net of the senses.
Through saṃsāra and through renunciation,
Nirvāṇa is generated, each in turn.
In its center is the great body,
Known by my senses.
The very nature of the hero
I shall listen to in the ocean of wisdom.

In the center of the wheel of yoginīs,
I, Vārāhī, ask:
What is the net of the senses said to be?
Where am I to explain it?
What are seeing and the great sound,
And likewise what is the eating of bliss?
What is the name of the meditative absorption? And in it,
That which has arisen being made to wane,
And saṃsāra that has truly arisen,
Where do they come to dissolve?
Blessed One, how is that named nirvāṇa
Once more fully generated?
Please speak the account of the body.
What your own senses know,
O my lord, abiding in great bliss,
Please speak it."

The Lord of Great Heroes declared:

"Vārāhī, bearer of the lotus of divine substance,
Goddess, listen with one-pointed mind.
The defining marks of the prior practice
I shall explain in brief.
The senses, the world, the supreme Dharma are taught;
In that net the mind takes its play.
Through mere pride of self
The supreme pledge arose.
Therefore great wisdom is seen,
Because it abides in the bliss of great skillful means.
Where the senses eat,
By that my name went before.
Then, as mere meditative absorption,
It was spoken by the sages, the supreme among the born.
The wisdom of perfect higher realization
Was brought about by the lord of gods of the buddhas,
Through the union that arose in them,
By many supreme ḍākinīs.
From the waning of both pleasure and pain,
It arises here once again;
Arising and emerging from the form of sound,
It is the domain of the conduct of the pledge.
Through the division of the three minds,
You, accomplished one, should come to know it.
By this, the self-arisen Blessed One
Is the hero, the binding of the net of ḍākas.
At the time of having arisen and of arising,
It is fully clear, it is luminosity.
Therefore it makes the world revolve,
And so it is fully proclaimed as saṃsāra.
Here it is like an illusion that has arisen;
It has no entity at all that dissolves.
Nirvāṇa is a distinct substance that exists,
Gone beyond the bounds of saṃsāra;
That which holds the summit of existence,
Therefore it is called nirvāṇa.
Form is a body like an illusion;
It radiates throughout its abode.
The great self, the form of wisdom,
Is clearly known by the senses:
The form that moves in the sky,
Unseen by gods, dānavas, and the rest."

"O god, with the taste of the great wisdom
Of your words I am satisfied.
Today I have a doubt:
What is the nature of the heroes?"

The Blessed One declared:

"All the heroes are intrinsic nature,
And those natures possess the qualities of dharmas.
Here the time of desire is proclaimed:
Desire arises fully from the mind.
Arising from both desire and mind,
Form is the principal.
Hard to find in the three worlds,
It truly abides at beginning, middle, and end.
Whatever is the mind of the final ones,
That mind has become of one taste.
From the union of the great maṇḍala,
Therefore that ḍāka arose.
The great-souled Vajraḍāka
Should accomplish the three planes of existence.
When at first he truly arose,
The chief of beings was Brahmā.
The abodes, and likewise how they are empty:
The protector liberates sentient beings.
Then, by other world-realms,
The small one here was urged:
'Living one, living one, gone into the expanse of awakening,
Without the thought of delivering sentient beings,
You who abide as the nature of illusion,
Please deliver the three realms without remainder!'
Having heard of these qualities,
Meditate thus on the liberator of beings.
In all the aspects of the tathāgatas,
The chief of beings is Heruka.
By first proclaiming the Glorious One,
The supports and the rest are generated.
Songs and music, gandharvas,
And whatever others there may be,
The classes of beings and their karma,
All arise fully from his stream.
These and others, endless in number,
And thirty-three thousand besides.
In the delight of the supreme secret,
The self of all forever abides.
Suchness of sixteen kinds,
And the cause of birth and of dissolution,
The suchness of the yogin and the yoginī,
Moves through the divisions of the moon.
Having always come to know the joy,
Through the moon's waxing from the first day,
In the dark half and the rest, and at the end of the bright half,
Likewise the short is to be known here."

"'Thus have I heard': my speech
Grants every secret desire.
Likewise here I have a doubt:
What is the teaching on the secret and the rest?
O my lord, please speak it."

"Acting for the benefit of sentient beings,
Great Goddess, I am pleased.
As the cause of what all sentient beings need,
I shall explain in brief;
Listen with one-pointed mind.
The secret is the limit of all the elements;
The abode of the senses is supreme;
In those perfected consciousnesses,
Delight arises from space.
The self of all, of the three realms,
Has truly entered as the form of what is pervaded.
The ever-abiding nondual Dharma
Is supreme, like an illusion or a dream.
Or else it is the sixteen selflessnesses,
And in particular compassion.
Its aspect is great wisdom,
Which has fully entered suchness.
What is to be understood here as birth
Is yoga and subsequent yoga.
Dissolution and accomplishment, these two,
And likewise the two approaches.
Yoga is explained as the drop;
The subtle yoga is the yoginī.
Through time, the half month and the month,
The Kṛta age, the third, the second, and the Kali age have passed.
Its four vajra faces
Become twice eight joys;
Endowed with sixteen digits,
It moves through the division of the fortnights.
Through the course of the short sun,
Likewise the digits are consumed.
The illusion-net of all women
Is unsurpassed nondual wisdom.
Into the great light of the mind of awakening
The great yogin assuredly goes.
Emptiness and compassion are indivisible;
Things are like flowers in the sky.
Even upon waking they fade away;
That abode is no abode at all."

"A dream arises from no object;
Nothing arises from all the senses.
Through the power of falling asleep,
The three planes of existence are to be eaten.
Thus, at the time when deep sleep has arisen,
There is no waking, no sleep, no dream.
In the times of past bliss,
Even buddhahood itself abides all the more.
The hero who is the reality of all the ḍākas
Is Vajraḍāka, supremely blissful.
If one keeps company with the objects of karma,
One falls into the Heaven of the Thirty-Three.
Stirred up by ignorance, beings surely
Go to the hells and the rest, of great terror.
Therefore, to the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha,
And to this great miracle, one should be devoted."

"How, Blessed One, my lord,
Are the names of all the ḍākinīs?
Speak them, with your mind turned toward me."
Supreme among all the ḍākinīs,
Bowing down there to the secret,
Vārāhī, yoginī of divine substance,
Asked again and again,
"How is it, in nondual words?"

The lord of the ḍākinīs declared:

"The subtle aspects are the three channels,
The cause of the three consciousnesses.
Therefore they are to be known as subtle;
The arising of the channels is the great self.
The thirty-seven yoginīs:
Each one is fully endowed with it.
All the ḍākinīs are explained there,
And others too, thus greatly wondrous.
Here in this ocean, the summary of the names,
I shall explain them all just as they are.
Moon, Milk, Oily One,
Gentle One, Desirous One, All-Giving One,
Mother, Sister, Daughter,
Sister's Daughter, Aunt,
Kinswoman, Father's Mother,
And the Wife of the Maternal Uncle,
Padminī, Śaṅkhinī, Citriṇī,
Hastinī, She of Waves of Intoxication,
She of Form, She of Sound, She of Smell,
She of Taste, She of Touch, She of Mind,
She of the Dharmadhātu, She of the Host,
She of Sleep, She of Sloth, Subduer of Others,
The Proud One, the Graceful One,
Fair-Faced One, Fierce One,
Drinker, Eater, Terrifying One,
She Who Shouts Ha Ha, She of the Charnel Ground:
These are always declared to be the delight of the Blessed One,
The thirty-seven yoginīs.
Through taking up the ritual of the activities,
Their other names too should be known.
Whatever channel is designated by whatever name,
That channel is spoken of here.
They are heroes whose nature is bliss,
Final, and supremely at peace.
The reality of Vajraḍāka, too,
Is endowed with the supremely undefiled yoga.
In aspects of color, arms, and the rest,
It has truly arisen in the wheel of existence.
Here among the sites, the secondary sites, and the rest,
Pullīra and the others are in the center.
Whoever is the lord of whichever deity,
That one is to be sealed with that very one.
Playing with oneself as the self,
One is fully endowed with all bliss.
By this, the self-arisen Blessed One,
Alone, likewise the first god,
Who grants bliss to all the ḍākinīs,
The Blessed One Vajra, spoke this.

Space has no beginning and no end;
Just as one supremely conceives it,
Just so do I know it;
Even without appearance it is fully clear.
There is no arising in it at all;
It is the supreme worldly convention.
Just as fire arises from space,
So it is with this unsurpassed fire here.
Taste is in a single entity,
Yet within it are many entities.
Through this nature of one and many,
All is taste, the all-pervading lord of taste.
By the lord of the messengers
The three planes of existence are supremely eaten.
Skillful means, wisdom, and great compassion:
There is no other lord of the gods.
It is not desire, and not freedom from desire;
Nor is it apprehended in the middle.
The yogin who knows how to examine it thus
Should enjoy all that is desirable.
Having always made it inward,
The colors arise from the mouth.
Always giving with the body,
One should abandon what arises from the mind.
Thus, play within desire, illusion's limit,
Is the supreme state.
Freedom from desire and the three joys
Are abandoned: the undefiled middle.
It is not something to be grasped;
The nature of things is not desire.
The joy of freedom from desire wanes,
And one should not delight in that either.
By abiding in the middle of those two,
In that dharma, too, joy is without object.
In the dharma of nondifferentiation,
In the vajra, take delight.
Yogins of the aspect of the deity
Make it directly manifest in an instant.
Through the yoga of a cast image and the like,
It becomes a shape that has been fashioned.
The yoga of form whose nature is emptiness
Is the defining mark of self-blessing.
The yoga devoid of self-blessing
Should be known as like fragments of husk.
This kind of supreme defining mark
Is not known by cattle-like people.
Therefore, if one meditates on this magical display,
One attains unsurpassed awakening.
Even one who is dull in this birth
Becomes a person of wisdom and poetry.
One who has this assembly of qualities
Should regard things as equal to a dream.
One goes into the tantra of yogin and yoginī;
Therefore it is worldly.
Directly perceived, oneself is the mantra body;
The three wheels are doubled.
For one, three wheels are taught;
For the second, they are held to be doubled.
The three wheels are skillful means and wisdom,
Taught as entering into union.
That is to be known as doubled;
By that the doubled wheels are explained.
The great abode of the three realms
Is meditation on the maṇḍala wheel.
Joining all the heroes in union,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
The Tathāgata, great lord of heroes,
The Blessed One Vajra, spoke this.

Where Dharma and non-Dharma set,
There hold the mind in awakening.
Accomplished ones, gods and asuras,
The senses and the objects,
The elements and the sense sources, all of them,
Are arising and ceasing.
The deluded are bound, while the wise
Are liberated through full understanding.
This mind itself, directly,
Abides throughout the three realms.
From the nonduality of myself and you, supreme one,
The chief of the assembly has arisen.
The Vaibhāṣikas who hold to the modes of conduct and so forth,
The Sautrāntikas, the Vijñānavādins, and the Mādhyamikas:
It is explained to sentient beings according to their inclinations;
It is neither true nor false.
He is endowed with twelve arms,
And likewise has four faces.
His color is the blue of the sky;
He has three eyes and stands with legs outstretched and bent,
Pressing down Hara and Gaurī together.
Others, by hundreds of thousands and thousands,
Of whatever tantra it may be,
Outer, secret, and inner in nature,
Are definitely the form of that one and that one.
The deity is the very self of Heruka.
In tantra and supremely within tantra,
The divisions are by the power of inclination;
The names should be known accordingly,
But they are mixed with Heruka.

Their colors are to be known as similar to that.
The manner of abiding is the aspect of Heruka.
It is the cause of the twelve grounds;
Through it one is like Heruka.
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
Joining all the heroes in union,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the first chapter, Entering the Ocean of Wisdom, from the Great King of Yoginī Tantras, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas.

Vārāhī, yoginī of divine substance,
Once again worshiped the Vajra:
"Blessed Lord, please teach:
From what did Vārāhī fully arise?
By what generator was she generated?
How is her nature held to be?
What is the reason for my arising?
And what is the purpose here?"

"The senses and all the objects
Fully enter the maṇḍala of the navel.
In the vajra tent at the crown of the head,
The wind descends, facing upward.
In the center of the sun of desire,
In an instant there is emanation and dissolution.
Vārāhī, who is born there,
Arose from the mind of bhaga and liṅga.
The elements whose nature is great bliss:
From them she was born first.
Her nature is the sole selflessness;
She has the form of yoga equal to space.
The reason is the deliverance of beings,
And she arose with the form of compassion as her nature.
Here two purposes are to be known:
Thoroughly accomplishing the union with the mudrā.
You are the lord of the goddesses,
Practicing all conduct, practicing much.
Having transposed the garland of mantras,
The letter ā and the rest are its pervading lords.
Or else it is endowed with the root mantra
And the armor mantras of Vārāhī;
The conduct of the mantra of each single letter
Has entered into the vow.
The birth of the supreme yoginī
Is in the supreme city of the channels:
Of the seventy-two thousand,
The central one is avadhūtī.
Thus this central channel
Abides as the pervader of all.
Therefore Vārāhī is to be known
As the supreme state of the chief lady.
At the beginning of the names, vajra and the rest:
a vajra and ka vajra,
And at the end of the mantra, those and the rest,
Or else separately, each distinct.
Whenever one recites the mantra,
At those times one is endowed with yoga.
Navel, crown, heart, and upward:
Thus there is no going and no coming.
The hero and the chief yoginī:
That self radiates outward.
The yoginīs, each with her own retinue,
Go into field after field.
Goddess, you are the lord,
In union together with me.
At the proper times and not at the proper times,
Remaining within the aperture of space,
And likewise emerging on the path of the womb,
The seed of deathlessness drips down.
There it is to be made wisdom without emission.
One should meditate with the half syllable hik.
At the time of woman and man as well,
One should make the supremely undefiled manifest.
The great vidyā that arose from it
Is written by one whose self is meditation.
First is Vairocana the protector,
Encircled by three lines, located below the navel;
Rising, it goes up as far as the throat,
And enters the three planes like a lamp.
The other mantra is like the letter that ends with sa;
From it the moon flows down from the left.
This mantra is adorned with a drop;
The mantra is the generation of the yoginī.
Again, the second seed is told:
The right, generating the sun,
Divided in two at the sides, with a drop on top.
The seed at the end of the ya class
Is the path by which the great mind of awakening descends.
This is the seed of Vārāhī,
Adorned at the end with the letter ā,
Adorned with the sixteen, the half moon, and the sun.
The seed first in the ya class,
Through union, the three planes, the quality of wind,
Is sealed with the thirteenth vowel,
Adorned on its head by the bright fortnight,
Blazing together with the drop:
It is the purity of the four joys.
The last of the pa class, the form of bliss,
Has the letter o mounted upon it,
Pressed on its head by the half moon and the sun.
Through the indestructible one beyond the earth,
It abides on a sun seat.
Taking the fourth vowel beside the ear,
Adorned on its head with the half moon and the drop,
It is the nature of the end of desire.
The wisdom seed is divided and doubled,
Born through emanation and gathering.
By abiding below and above,
The protector who holds complete enjoyment,
In the heart and in the water-born lotus,
Completes the rite of the meaning in full.
The seed of the Terrifying Lady:
Having taken the letter at the end of the pa class,
One should again recite it in the same way.
In Uḍḍiyāna, in the aperture of the city that holds the blaze,
Caṇḍālī is kindled.
With the mind of awakening the three planes are filled.
Whatever mantra has been explained thus,
Its letters do not accord in sequence.
Endowed with the names of the yoginīs,
The times of the activities are to be understood thus.
The great self of each one
Is by the number of its letters.
The great self radiates outward;
The portions of awakening are the cause of the portions.
First comes homage to Vairocana;
The dharmadhātu is the form of activity.
The fifth letter of the ta class
Is of the fifth group.
The yoginī's form rests upon space.
The seed at the end of the pa class, with the vowel o,
Is she who destroys delusion;
She emanates the host of yoginīs.
Its fourth is the seed;
She emanates the yoginī who pervades the three planes.
The third of the first class
Emanates the wheel of ḍākinīs
Who make the six destinies wane.
The seed last in the ya class
Is the nature of the mind of awakening.
The first of the fifth class
Is the seed of suchness,
Touched on its head by the vowel ī.
The fully praised letter of water
Is the nature that accomplishes everything.
Taking the one in the middle of the ca class,
Endowed with a sun seat,
It has special power at the time of the activities.
The seed of Vārāhī
Is the letter a touching the ear,
Equal in nature to the wisdom of the three planes.
The seed of fire, endowed with two vowels,
Is the nature of the karma of saṃsāra.
On the head of the letter that ends with sa, the vowel ī touches;
Always regard it as the nature of Brahmā.
Taking the second vowel,
Thus the form of the ḍākinī
Delivers sentient beings.
The letter ya stands with ra,
And again the vowel ī is joined to it;
It emanates the yoginīs of the three realms.
The first of the sixth class
Is the nature of all the yoginī mantras.
ra stands with the vowel a;
It becomes the slayer of hostile beings.
Taking up the letter ja,
Truly endowed with the vowel i,
It is fully endowed with the nature of illusion.

The seed of the teeth, at the ear,
Is distinguished by being touched by the vowel i.
Having joined the mantra of the nāgas,
The syllable tre is all the three planes.
It is the special mantra of wisdom.
The letter la endowed with o
Is the support of the worlds;
It is the dharma that brings about awakening.
The first, ka, has ya as its seat;
Death and the coemergent are without distinction.
To the last of the pa class
One should join the vowel of the letter ā;
It is the mother of the three planes by nature.
The letter ta, adorned with the letter e,
Is what holds bliss.
Again, the last of the pa class
Is the nature abiding in the center
Of the immeasurable coemergent.
The last of sa endowed with ā
Is the indestructible wisdom of Vajrasattva.
The last of the ya class with the vowel i
Is what destroys the nature of unknowing.
The mantra tye is the nature of the dharmadhātu;
It pervades as the nature of all.
Dripping from the apertures, ṣa;
The descending seed is endowed with the sun.
The mantra of the lord of sea monsters
Is the fourth seed of the ba class,
Endowed with the vowel ū and a seat;
It makes the terrors of saṃsāra wane.
With the mantra of ta one makes enemies wane.
Again, the fourth of the pa class
Proclaims the great sound.
The seed of wind touched by the letter ā:
The mantra is like a jewel raining down.
The seed of water held at the ear
Is the nature of illusion.
What is beyond sa, endowed with the letter e,
Is the cause that destroys the confusion of the three planes.
The last of the pa class has the nature of a foot;
What is beyond sa, endowed with ā,
Is the dharma of great wonder.
The last of the ya class
Is the supreme letter, the seed of the lotus.
The seed in the middle of ca,
With ra placed on its head,
And opened by the letter e,
Is the nature of gods and asuras.
Thus there are the six mantra phrases,
Emanating the host of yoginīs,
The wheel whose nature is all the activities.
The vast words of suchness,
Which have become the wish of compassion,
Thoroughly filling all yogas,
The Blessed Ḍāka spoke these.

I shall explain the yantra wheel.
Your yantra, small or vast,
Should be drawn completely round,
With thirteen lines.
O goddess, there will be twelve segments,
Adorned with thirty-six spokes.
On each spoke a letter
Should be placed clearly, in order.
The remaining twenty-four letters
All go in the hub at the center.
The channels generated by them
Are seventy-two thousand.
The retinue of each one individually
Is vasu, arrow, and moon;
What falls short of all by the remainder
Is the assembly of vasu and veda.
For one who abides there, the yantra is drawn up;
A single activity, according to one's inclination.
When the mount of consciousness
Arises in all the channels,
There, at the time of the activity,
The senses and the objects are to be slain.
What is called mentation is to be expelled,
And the mind is to be brought under control.
The elements and the sense sources, and likewise the others,
Are to be pacified and increased.
All the aggregates, and likewise the life force,
Are summoned and made hostile.
Body, speech, and likewise mind,
These are to be paralyzed.
Other activities, as is fitting,
Are one hundred and eight as well.
Or else, whatever the seed is like,
It is from one equal to its measure.
At that time, by the great self,
There is generation and destruction through the letters.

I shall explain the second yantra
Of Vārāhī, the chief lady of the vajra.
The seed at the end of the ya class
Is the nature of the seed of awakening.
The two-tongued letter endowed with the letter ra:
Fill the yoginī mantra with the letter ā.
That which abides in sa is the unmoving.
The letter na endowed with e
Is the reason for emanating all sentient beings.
The vowel abiding first is the first protector.
The two-tongued one touched on its head by the vowel e
Is the nature of the element of the three planes.
The letter ṭa endowed with e,
The first of the fifth,
Is the nature of the yoginī.
The seed abiding fourth in the ya class
Is the nature of the chief lady of all.
The seed first in the eighth class,
Adorned with the empty one,
Is the very essence of the element of semen.
The first abiding in the second
Is the skeleton that holds the drop.
The seed second in the ya class,
Endowed with the vowel i,
Is the protector abiding in the vajra heart.
The last of ta, adorned with the letter e,
Is ornamented upon the womb.
The letter ta endowed with the sun
Is the king who destroys fevers.
bha endowed with the sun, adorned with the letter a,
Abides in the hearth of the navel.
The last of the pa class is the nature of the sites.
The last of ṭa, adorned with i,
Is the nature of summoning.
bi śo ṣa ṣi are four mantras;
Through being the nature of the four joys,
They are the seed of the great treasure.
The letter ra, adorned with the letter o,
Is the nature of the fierce channel.
The second seed of śa
Is in the form of the essence.
Held by its nature, the letter ṇi
Is the very reality of the yoginī.
The letter ka endowed with the letters ra and o
Is the central channel, lalanā, and rasanā by nature.
One should take what abides between ta and pa;
Of the host of yoginīs
It is the eighth foot, the nature of accomplishment.
The last of the ta class, adorned with the letter i,
Is the nature of the channel in the middle of the skull.
One should take what is designated as ka;
By it activities are exhausted.
The letter ra is held by the letter a;
From the middle of the head the great sound drips.
The seed of sa endowed with the letter i
Is in the middle of the triangle.
The last of ta, adorned with i,
Is above and below the emanation.
Join the letters ta, ra, and ā;
The dharmadhātu is the triangle,
And therefore it generates the three planes.
Take sa, the seed together with the central one;
The letter na endowed with i is the nature.
The last of the pa class, adorned with a,
Is the essence of illusion, the channel of the lotus.
Wherever ra abides is the nature of the ear.
The last of the ṭa class endowed with i
Is the protector who holds the elements
Of all sentient beings without distinction.
Thus ten mantra phrases are proclaimed,
Having the measure of the four times.
The universal monarch of the activities
Of all the yoginīs, who enjoys them,
Whose nature is the form of time,
The Blessed God spoke this.

The gathering place of the ḍākinīs
Is known as wheel upon wheel.
From the wisdom of twenty-one thousand six hundred,
It acts in the same way.
Those who speak crookedly
Hold that it is born from the number of its atoms.
Having taken the name of the one to be accomplished,
The wise one then joins the mantra.
First, pa with ra as its seat;
It is the dharma form of the first.
On what abides fourth from it,
The ear is adorned with the letter e:
This is the mantra of the yoginīs.
What abides in the middle of the ta class grants whatever is desired.
The letter na endowed with the letter i
Makes manifold emanations.
The letter pa is together with elaboration.

The letter ra endowed with ā
Is the universal monarch of the yoginīs.
Take the seed abiding in the middle of the ca class;
Its nature is just as it is seen.
The gathering of wind, endowed with the letter e,
Is the nature of the letters a haṃ.
The last of the ya class
Is adorned on its head with the third vowel;
It is the nature of the mantra of manifold wisdom.
The seed of ja, without limit,
Is the form that comes to blaze.
The letter ya endowed with the letter e
Is the form of the view of all the vehicles.
The seed third in the ca class
Is called the form of the yoginī.
The end of pa is on its fourth;
It is the master of all mantras.
The last of the ta class, endowed with the third vowel,
Is the form that calls all sentient beings without exception.
The third of the sibilants, with ta as its seat,
Delivers beings, those that move and those that do not.
Likewise, take the letter mbha;
It is the form of the fourteenth ground.
The mantra ni enters
Into the signs of the yoginīs.
The last of the pa class touched by the letter o
Holds manifold unknowing.
The end of sa is the nature of Vajrasattva.
The letter ni refutes
Dharma and non-Dharma.
The last of the ya class
Is the yoginī, the form of space.
The letter ja with the letter ra as its seat
Is the dawning of the nature of semen.
Again, the last of wind,
Given splendor by the letter a,
Is the form of Brahmā and of the lotus.
The letter ra endowed with the letter a
Is the unchanging nature.
The end of sa, with the third vowel on its head,
Is the form of the yoginīs descending.
ma, the central one, is the form of suchness.
The end of sa, filled with a,
Is proclaimed at the mouth of the yoginī's form.
The letter ya, with the diadem of o,
Is the coemergent nature.
What abides in the middle of the ka class, adorned with the third vowel,
Is the nature without suchness.
The letter ni is without intrinsic nature.
On the first of the second,
The letter a makes it clear:
It is the nature of the desire realm.
The last of the pa class endowed with e
Is the nature of everything as illusion.
The seed of pa, with śa at the end,
And the last letter of the ya class as its seat,
Is semen and wind without distinction.
The seed of fire, adorned on its head with the vowel i,
Is accomplishment, the nature of illusion.
The seed second of ka
Is space, the form of the yoginī.
The letter at the end of that seed,
Adorned at the ear with the letter e,
Is the sole hero, Akṣobhya.
Thus the wheel is the nature of space.
Thus these nine lines
Are the letters of the host of yoginīs,
The nature of the mantra of the three realms.
Abiding in the pledge of the ḍākas,
Whose self is the union of the conduct of all mantras,
Thoroughly bestowing all accomplishments,
The Blessed God spoke this.

Through the power of the third wheel,
One performs whatever activity one wishes.
At the time of the yogins' activities,
One should bewilder everyone through illusion.
The selfless Vajrā, rising again,
Endowed with the yoga of the other wheel,
With the supremely unchanging chief,
The Selfless Lady embraces him tightly.
With the mantra of ta one joins the second.
dya abiding is the seed of fire and wind,
The great passion of great bliss.
Its second seed
Is filled with the letter ā;
It is the nature of the four great elements.
Join the letters pa, ra, and o;
The three are the nature of wind.
The letter ta endowed with the letter aṃ
Is again the seed of consciousness.
What abides in the middle of the ka class,
Fully adorned with the letter e,
Is the nature of the yoginī's mudrā.
It is to be recited again.
These words are the reality of the inseparability of means and wisdom.
The end of sa and the mantra of na
Are to be recited again;
It is the form that generates and gathers.
pa ra ā is a single seed,
The letter of the ultimate.
The end of ṭa endowed with the letter ā
Is born from the indestructible.
The last of ta destroys everything.
On ka ka and where ṇi abides,
Join the third vowel and add the drop;
It calls all the yoginīs.
Likewise kha kha,
And the third, i abiding on ṇa,
Is the cause that removes weariness.
The mantra words dhu na
Are to be recited twice;
They destroy all conceptions.
The mantra of the last of the ya class
Is adorned at the aperture of the vajra.
The seed abiding in the middle of the ca class
Abides fully on a seat of ra;
It devours delusion.
The end of sa is the indestructible, the central one.
The third of the śa class, with ha as its seat,
Adorned at the ear with the letter e,
Is the form of Citriṇī the ḍākinī.
It is the mantra that calls.
The mantra words śo ṣa ya
Are to be recited twice;
They destroy the conceit and pride of those who think themselves wise.
It is awakening, the very reality of no awakening.
Thus these twelve lines
Are the nature of the grounds and of the activities of yoga.
The pervading lord of the twelve grounds,
The Blessed God, spoke this.

Through the signs of the places and so forth of the letters,
One goes beyond to suchness.
From me all sentient beings are born,
And from sentient beings I too arise.
That self having become ever nonexistent,
The Tathāgata is like an illusion, a dream.
This yantra has great power;
The activities arise just as they are.
In the character of Vajraḍāka,
At this time everything is transformed.
In these, the fifth wheel,
Nirvāṇa too is to be proclaimed.
It continually slays the gods and all the rest,
And always liberates them all.

He declared:
The seed second of ka
Is the very host of yoginīs.
Take ṭa with the end of pa as its seat,
And adorn its diadem with aṃ;
Fill it with the letter a.
It is the nature of the six destinies.
The seed abiding in the middle of the ka class
Removes the touch of poison.
The seed of ka is the nature of emptiness.
The last of na endowed with the letter ā
Is powerful through blazing fire.
The seed of holding
Is the great pervading lord of movement.
The fourth of the ta class
Is adorned with the vowel ā;
It is the form free from existence.
The sun endowed with the letter e is the vetāla.
Take the seed of the end of ta,
Adorned on its head with the third vowel;
It slays all the three planes.
The seed of the last of the pa class
Makes the speech of the mind free of conception.
The end of sa endowed with the sound of ā
Is conception, the friend of liberation.
Two ma and two ha are to be recited;
They seize and seize the nature of delusion.
The seed of pa adorned with the letter i:
All the forests of pha wane; that itself is to be taken.
The letter śa endowed with i
Is the mantra of the lion's devouring.
The mantra of ta again gives birth to the deathless.
Fill the end of pa with the letter i,
And let the drop touch its diadem;
Death in the seal is conception.
The letter sa is adorned with two vowels for recitation;
It is what seizes first.
The seed abiding in śa is the charnel ground.
The end of ta endowed with i
Is everything without intrinsic nature.

The last of the pa class, endowed with the vowel ī,
Is the great seizer of the hateful mind.
The letter na with u as its seat dispels all thoughts.
The seed that is second of the śa class,
With the letter ā, and a drop abiding where it touches the head,
Is the mantra for accomplishing awakening,
Through the downward-clearing wind.
The two letters ta and ra,
With sa, are the mantra that causes terror.
The single seed of pa, ra and ā
Crushes the weariness of the Thirty-Three.
The last of the ya class, pressed by ri,
Is the purifier of wisdom.
The seed abiding beside ṇa,
Adorned at its ear with i,
Crushes the ignorance of the yogin.
Fill the letter sa with the letter e;
It makes the cutting off of breath decline.
The mantra na, adorned on its head with the third vowel,
Has the nature of abandoning Dharma and non-Dharma.
The letter dha joined with ya
Is the nature of the mantra of meditative concentration.
The last of the ta class is to be taken;
Appearing like a city, it is without real entity.
The seed that is second of the ya class
Is the blazing of the nature of delusion.
Divide the letter sa with the letter i:
It is the lion that crushes the buffalo.
That which also abides in the sun
Is the king of all yoginīs.
The last of the pa class, adorned with the vowel ā:
By it all sentient beings are held as an illusion.
The seed of holding, adorned with the letter a:
The wisdom of suchness is attained.
Thus, through these fourteen lines,
All sentient beings slay ignorance.
The very embodiment of the sublime mantra,
The one who emits and gathers in,
Who terrifies all the bhūtas,
The Blessed One, the deity, spoke this.
Embodying the form of a wheel,
It adorns the heart of Vārāhī;
Through the inseparability of emptiness and compassion
It is both killing and not killing.
In the middle of the ka class, the one with ra as its seat:
On the head of that third, place a drop.
It has the nature of the supreme vehicle.
The second letter of the ta class
Is endowed with the vowel i;
It makes the yoga of the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) decline.
The letter ta brings about realization.
That which abides fourth in the ta class, endowed with ā,
Holds endless awakening.
Ra in its place, adorned with the third vowel,
Explains all the yoginīs.
The mantra na adorned with the letter i
Is the path of the yoginīs.
The line of sumbhana
Should be recited twice;
It is the reality of realizing the six words.
Likewise the mantra sumbha
Has the nature of the worship of the yoginīs.
The mantra hana is recited twice;
It makes the hostile vehicle decline.
The single letter of pa, ra and ā:
The essence of each sense faculty, its own, is other.
Fill ṇa with the letters a and aṃ;
This seed is the mantra of qualities.
The letter sa is the dripping of all the channels.
The letter ba, adorned with the letter ra, and ri,
Abiding in pa, enters into space.
The place of the letter sa is the form of all.
Fill the last of the ya class with ā;
Wind is the wisdom of life.
Fill the letter na with the letter ā;
It is not conceiving of awakening as anything at all.
One should gaze at the drop on its head.
The last of the ba class is the form of the great bee.
The end of sa should be filled with ā;
It is the life of the assembly of yoginīs.
To the end of pa endowed with a
A diadem of aṃ is added as adornment;
It has the nature of a cloud of all phenomena.
The letter sa slays ignorance of the true Dharma.
The mantra cche is the bodhisattva;
Having gone, there is no birth in saṃsāra.
The seed abiding in the middle of the ta class:
Nga is appearance without an object.
Na endowed with i
Is the very binding of the entire world.
The mantra kro, by suppressing, cuts off saṃsāra.
The seed dha crushes all beings;
It renders them free of conceptual elaboration.
The letter mū, the seed,
Is the karma of the whole deluded world.
Thus these fourteen and a half words
Are the end of the wheel of the channels.
Practices such as vase breath retention (kumbhaka):
The yogin does whatever he wishes.
Through the thorough application of the six wheels,
It is the nature of whatever action is desired;
The lord, able to accomplish the union,
The Blessed One, spoke this.
From the crown protuberance and so on down to the place of birth,
From body, speech, mind, consciousness and space,
By the forward order and by reversal of that order,
Reality and the awakenings should be known.
Here it is declared as the aspect of skillful means
And also as the aspect of wisdom.
Or else, in the intervals
Between each letter, and again,
The mantra dhi is of innate nature;
It performs the subsidiary rites
Of the particular wheel.
The mantra daṃṣṭrakarāli
Has the nature of the karma of beings.
The mantra ma, the mantra hā, and the mantra mudre
Are the nature of the ear.
The mantra words śrīherukadevasyagramahiṣi
Are the nature of the nose.
The mantra sahasraśire
Is the form of the lord of voice.
The mantra syllables sahasrabāhabe
Are the form of the body.
The mantra śatasa is a single wheel.
The mantra of the ḍākinī has the nature
Of death and of birth.
From the Dharma whose aspect is nondual,
Sentient beings grasp it as two;
The one who bestows bliss upon wisdom,
The Blessed Guru, spoke this.
The śrāvakas and others, and the non-Buddhists,
All the cattle of ignorance:
In order that they may attain wisdom,
This method has been taught.
The mantra hasrānane
Touches visible form as object.
The mantra jvalitatejase:
These mantra words are the object of the mind.
The words jvalamukhi are the object of the body.
The mantra piṅgalocana
Is eye consciousness.
The mantra words bajraśarī
Are body consciousness.
The mantra bajrāsane
Touches the object of the nose.
The mantra words milite
Are nose consciousness.
The mantra words cilite
Are the object of the lord of voice.
The mantra he, recited twice, is the object of sound,
The consciousness of both sound and voice.
Thus the seven and a half mantra words
Are the form of all death and birth.
The Blessed Lord, delighting in bliss with Vārāhī,
Spoke this:
Embodying the union of bola and kakkola,
It slays objects, sense faculties and the rest.
Whatever sentient beings there are,
Born from the lotus of the object,
Those very sentient beings
Are slain by their own respective karmas.
Because it destroys objects and the rest,
That is explained as the wheel of wisdom.
The actions of the tantra and of the tantras
Are the wisdom that is the heart of accomplishment.
The end of sa, seated on the sixth vowel,
Its head adorned with crescent moon and drop,
Should be recited twice;
It is the secret form of Vajrasattva.
kha twice, dhu twice, muru twice
Are the reality of the actions;
They abide together with the life breath.

advīte mahāyoginī,
The mantra words pathitasiddhe:
That, too, is the form of the end of the body.
dre ṅhaṃ, two words; graṃ, two words;
he, two words; ha, two words.
The letters of the ten wrathful ones
Are to be understood as Yamāntaka and the others.
On the seed that is fourth of the pa class
The letter ī touches the head.
The mantra mesa comprises all the words;
It is the nature of the ḍākinī.
Thus these twelve mantra words
Deceive the twelve links of dependent origination.
The Teacher, the chief of the great pledge,
The Blessed One, spoke this.
By the karma of the twelve limbs of dependent origination
It stupefies them all together.
The letters that generate the messengers
Have the nature of the form of a wheel.
Just as the wheel is not the letters,
So the messengers are without intrinsic nature.
What is variously born from that ripening
Is perceived under its separate name;
Through the reality of Mañjuvajra and the others
The characteristic of the avadhūtī is to be made.
hāsahā, the mantra words of the single hero,
Are the seed of the six tathāgatas;
They explain endless qualities.
The seed hā, four letters;
ho once and ho twice are to be recited.
The seven letters are the elements of skin and the rest.
The mantra trailokyabināśa
Is armor against the hostile.
śatasahasrakoṭi
tathāgataparibāre.
The end of sa, ornamented with the vowel ū,
Adorned with crescent moon and drop:
Thus it gathers in all mantras.
ha ū aṃ is the nature of the mantra.
Thus there are four mantra words.
Generating the yoginīs, accomplishment comes about.
Entering into the accomplishment granted by command,
The Blessed Hero spoke this.
What is taught in the Ḍākinīsaṃvara, in the Abhidhāna,
And also in the other yoginī tantras
And in the yoga tantras
Bestows the accomplishments of the whole range of rites.
Through the thorough application of the wheel granted by command
They are accomplished, and not by any other application.
Again, the end of sa, on a seat
Abiding beside the fifth vowel,
Is adorned with crescent moon and drop.
By the rite, it is the seed of reality itself.
That which abides beside pa
Generates the assembly of yoginīs.
The mantra that makes the letter ṭa blaze:
The mantra words siṅharupe khaḥ
Destroy the chief of the obstructors.
The mantra gajarūpe gaḥ
Acts upon the obstructors of all sentient beings.
The words trailokyodare
Generate all the most supreme messengers.
The words samudramekhale, with graha twice:
Adorned with a girdle like the ocean,
That is the girdle of the mantra, and so on.
The letters hūṃ and phaṭ
Are the all-pervading lord of the ḍākinīs.
The mantra words bīrādvīte hūṃ:
From the nonduality of skillful means and wisdom
The ordinary conditioned is born.
Thus the mantra of seven lines.
Of the nature of the ground of wisdom,
The Blessed Vajra spoke this:
At the end, when sentient beings are accomplished through karma,
They go to his supreme state,
The name of the Buddha Vajradhara;
Born again, they abide on the path.
The illusory aspect is illusion;
One's own self is known individually.
hūṃ hā twice, mahāpaśumohani:
These words are the delusion of saṃsāra.
The mantra yogīśvarī tvaṃ
Is in the form of peace.
The words ḍākinīlokānāṃ bandhā
Cause the world to enter awakening.
The mantra words satyame hūṃ phaṭ
Teach the four extremes.
The words bhūtatrāsani
Crush the wisdom of the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas).
Thus by these seven mantra words
The distinctions of the rites are made.
On each petal around the lotus hub
The letters of the mantra abide;
The form of the hero's mantra abides.
The Blessed One spoke this.
Those twelve great yantras
Were spoken by my beloved.
The yantras of all the rites
Are to be performed together with the twelve.
In each of the great wheels
One must perform its respective rite.
At the twelve junctions
One should accomplish it with the name of the one to be subdued.
The end of pa and the end of sa, endowed with ā;
The end of ya together with the vowel ī;
The letter re, the end of na, and the letter ra;
The end of pa and the word siddhe;
The word pidye, the mantra śvari, that which stands beside pa, and the seed ṭa;
hū adorned with crescent moon and drop:
Repeat it and recite it again.
That which abides beside pa, and the letter ṭa,
Together with hūṃ phaṭ and svāhā:
With this mantra, at the hub, according to the rite,
Recite it together with the name of the one to be subdued.
The twelve together with the hub:
The Tathāgata spoke this.
The seed letters, twenty-four,
Are mixed with Pullīra and the other sites.
Glorious Heruka-vajra and Vārāhī:
What has not arisen will not arise.
As for the might and power of this mantra,
From the endless scriptures taught
In thousands of koṭis, the hero
Has likewise gathered the essence.
The mantras taught in other tantras
Are the means of accomplishment of this mantra.
Without recitation, without count, without letters,
Without form, and likewise without meditative concentration,
By the wisdom that recollects the deity
All accomplishments are generated.
At the time the breath enters,
All the letters are to be slain.
With the exhalations one performs every application;
This is to be known at the time of outflow.
At the time of abiding, the lord of yogins
Should not desire a buddhafield.
Thus the movement of the breath is to be known;
Childish yogins do not realize it.
The disciple whom one empowers
With the four realities,
Once fully matured in the Great Vehicle,
Should afterwards be properly taught the mantra.
First the maṇḍala of Vārāhī
Is to be drawn, and then that of Heruka.
A vajra daughter, or else another,
Is there to be brought in as the wisdom consort.
When empowering in other tantras
This method is not to be taught.
Wisdom having the nature of the knowable:
The maṇḍala is to be made in the avadhūtī.
Vārāhī, the aspect of wisdom,
Embraces the knowable, glorious Heruka.
She has three eyes and a fierce form,
Four faces with bared fangs,
And is endowed with twelve arms.
She tramples Gaurī and Bhairava.
On her body are the five mudrās and the rest;
She is radiant like the fire at the time of destruction.
In her right hands are a vajra and a curved knife,
An axe, likewise a ḍamaru,
A trident, and the skin of the Bodiless One (Kāma);
And so too with her left hands:
On the left are a bell and a skull cup,
A noose, a khaṭvāṅga, and a head.
Her hair hangs loose; she is naked,
Holding a garland of heads,
Endowed with the ornaments of erotic charm,
With a garland of skulls on her head.
She is sealed with the vajra of passion,
Or else with Heruka.

Her limbs are anointed with divine fragrances;
She wears anklets and armlets
And is draped with garlands of divine flowers.
Upon her forehead she always holds
A vajra resting on the moon at the tip of her crest.
She stands with her right leg extended.
Before the assembly of yoginīs
She sends forth garlands blazing from her body.
Heruka is like black collyrium,
His crown of matted locks in front.
He wears a skirt of tiger skin
And is fully adorned with the five mudrās.
The hero has a garland of skulls
And bears a crossed vajra and a half moon.
He has three eyes and bared fangs;
With four faces he strikes terror.
He is to be known as twelve-armed,
Adorned with fifty heads.
For worship he bears ash as well;
He has a garland of flames and bares his fangs.
With two hands he forms the mudrā of the place of birth;
The rest are as for Vajravārāhī.
If Heruka is the principal,
Then they are in the two hands of Vārāhī.
He holds an elephant skin.
Embodying skillful means and wisdom,
Undefiled yoga, excellent great bliss,
The form of existence and of nirvāṇa:
The two are to be given as the heart mantra.
As for the remaining yoginīs,
Ḍākinī and the others, in their order,
The knower of mantra should give the skull fragments and the rest
To all the heroes as well.
For each one, too, its tantra
Follows the earlier pattern.
The lalanā and rasanā channels,
Where wisdom and skillful means meet,
Have as their support the avadhūtī;
There one arrives at equal taste.
The three wheels are to be known as becoming twofold,
With Ḍākinī and the others in their order.
Heruka and Vajravārāhī
Have hundreds of thousands and thousands of arms;
Beginning from two arms and so on,
Whatever others there are, they are those.
Not gazing at the objects of the senses,
Not with hands, postures and the like,
By meditating, all elaborations are seized;
Abandon that delusion.
The self of the yoga of momentary aspect,
Whose application is the moment and the rest:
Set out the seed in the heart
And perform emanation, absorption and the rest.
Invite the maṇḍala, worship it,
Confess wrongdoing, and so on;
Emptiness and so on, up to Mount Meru;
The fence, the tent, and the rest.
On the ground portion are the vajra lines;
It is drawn in six wheels.
Following the defining marks of the clear realization as it is,
Perform all the various aspects.
All the methods and instructions
I, the Beautiful One, have explained.
There the torma is to be given,
And worship made with the five, earth and the rest.
With the letters of the blazing syllables
The recitation of the mantra is also to be done.
With the blazing, doubly increasing letters
Kindle the caṇḍālī of the life breath.
Facing upward, it burns the channels;
Likewise the wisdom ḍāka is born.
All the ḍākinīs and the others, without exception,
And consciousness and the rest, dissolve;
Burned, the rabbit-bearing moon, haṃ, drips.
All the heroes in equal union,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

From the Great King of Yoginī Tantras, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas: the nature of the generation of Vajravārāhī, of the chief goddess, of the wheel of yantras, of the meditation on the maṇḍala, and so on. This is the second chapter.

Next, the generation of Ḍākinī and the others,
How it is done, I shall fully explain.
Relying on union with the ḍākinī,
The Blessed One plays supremely.
The form of Vārāhī, though infinite,
Is the binding of the net of elaborations.
By the distinctions of bodily things
The ḍākinī has infinite forms.
Relying on the bliss of the body,
Desire becomes threefold.
There are three joys and three channels;
The fourth is held to be the innate.
The great experience of the buddha body:
From its nature the manifold radiates.
Relying on the abodes of the ḍākinīs,
It is the nature of the maṇḍala wheel.
In the center of the twelve sections
Of great Jambudvīpa,
In the eastern land, the goddess abides:
The union of time, Rohita, the makara,
Victory and the sun, the producer of joy,
And the life breath flowing as the substance of the channel.
The goddess, as the self of outer and inner,
Abides in the form of the Blessed Lady.
She has four arms and is black in color,
With three eyes and a single face.
She bares her fangs and is fierce,
Manifestly holding the five mudrās.
She rides a corpse, her hair hangs loose,
And she stands with her right leg extended.
The fierce one has a garland of skulls;
In her right hands are a ḍamaru and a curved knife,
In her left a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga.
Her body emanates and gathers in.
She is to be placed in the center of the maṇḍala
That embodies the thirty-seven.
The heart mantra of twenty-two syllables,
Joined in union with the ḍākinīs:
oṃ śrī oṃ bajra bajra he ḍā he ki ru nī ru ye kaṃ hūṃ ḍā hūṃ ki pha nī ṭa jā pha la ṭa saṃ svā ba hā ra hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā.
The goddesses of the maṇḍala
Are held to be letters bearing the vajra.
From Ḍākinī to the end with the Crusher of Thunderbolts,
They are renowned as the root of the family.
Once the abode of the ḍākinī has been accomplished,
Vārāhī will abide there.
The thirty-seven deities
Are bliss nondual with a skillful means like themselves,
To be known through the application of their names:
An instruction serving many purposes.
The tantra of the thirty-seven
Is generated from great bliss.
Because the lord is the ḍākinī,
That tantra is of the ḍākinī family.
Lord, I am yours.
How you are to be generated by me,
Having pleased the Blessed One
Who gives bliss to the ḍākinī,
Listen, and I shall explain your generation.
One's own continuum, unimpaired;
The letter ḍā, the very self of the secret aperture;
In the form of a bow, endowed with the life breath;
Truly having the form of the body;
Embodying objects and sense faculties;
The great drop of the mind of awakening;
The continuum bearing the name i:
In the great lotus of going and coming
It pervades the wheel of channels.
The letter ka and the form of ka likewise:
Luminosity, what is emanated, and the emanation.

In all the realms of enlightened activity
She goes forth greatly in peaceful form.
Though without joy, her beauty does not change;
She is ripened by the hues of the ineffable innate.
In human form, always through great bliss,
Illusory displays surely radiate forth.
Ī shows itself as all the great elements,
And as Īśvara and the rest.
Through the Ram, the Tawny One and the Good-Minded
Its basis is generated.
This is the name of the ḍākinī;
It is to be indicated according to the time of the rite.
Summoning the nāgas properly,
Melting them so as to make them bring down rain,
The great magic over the gods, all the bhūtas
And kings as well,
Subjugation, fierce rites, paralysis,
Killing, expulsion and the rest:
These are to be known from the time of the fierce rites.
She is generated in each one's own continent.
Through the equipoise of wisdom and skillful means,
The lord of yogins, embodying emptiness and compassion,
With subtle mind, and by what is extremely subtle,
Enters therein.
For the sake of sentient beings, here and there,
He acts through union with his own self.
In the great thousandfold trichiliocosm,
Where the all-pervading lord enjoys everything,
There, in the twelve sections,
Each one's own chief goddess, a yoginī,
In dissolution and in the enjoyments of experience,
Enjoys all the great miraculous powers.
The movements of the breath, twenty-one thousand six hundred,
Are to be known.
By the eighteen hundred life breaths
The transference is here declared.
The twelve become the day and night,
In the sequence of the twelve junctures of time.
Having tripled the junctures of time,
The messengers of time enjoy everything.
By fifty times, each messenger
Is to be distinctly known.
By fifty-three, precisely,
They act within a single day and night.
They are properly explained as letters,
Beginning with a and ending with kṣa.
Taught as the surplus qualities,
Tripled, together with half a hundred,
These are definitively explained as the messengers.
This is the ḍākinī seal:
Sky-goers, earth-goers and others,
And those who abide below the earth as well.
The yoginīs are of three kinds:
Field-born, mantra-born, and innate by nature.
Whoever abides predominantly through whichever,
That one is clearly seen as hers.
Relying on the distinction of family,
They abide born from the union of desire.
In those eatings of the caru,
The great beast having the form of wisdom:
What is not to be eaten is to be eaten there as well,
And what is not indicated is to be clearly indicated.
Abandon both characteristic and non-characteristic;
That is the thorough abandonment of abandoning.
Even attachment to caring for and subduing,
Whoever, by whatever, in its own reality,
Having made the Buddha and the Dharma abide,
Through the application of manifest union:
When the life wind of the avadhūtī
Is stable, Vārāhī and the other yoginīs appear;
For those whose life winds are unstable
Accomplishment does not become clear.
The goddess is to be sealed by Vairocana,
And by Akṣobhya, lord who holds the vajra.
The thirty-six mantras of the messengers,
By time, each with its own family,
As with the family of the chief goddess,
Their hands and emblems in their order;
Or else, as definitively stated,
They are to be known from the characteristics of the clear realization.
Through the union of bolla and kakkola
The goddess herself enters into melting.
In that instant she beholds
All the maṇḍalas without exception as of equal taste.
The goddess, appearing within space,
Should rouse the lord with this:
Vajra of supreme great bliss, play, play!
Bring the intention of wisdom and skillful means to perfect fulfillment.
Through your compassion, meditated upon for the worlds,
Make all the gods and asuras, without exception, into buddhas.
Holder of the vajra of awakening, give delight with great bliss.
Have you entered the meditative absorption of emptiness?
Through the play of vajra and lotus, realize it with certainty today,
You who abide together with her in the absorption of emptiness.
Lord, let the seed of the four elements drip.
Caṇḍālī, the very nature of the avadhūtī, will arise;
She burns the three worlds, and is seen nowhere at all.
In the essence of nirvāṇa she blazes and goes on blazing.
Delight the ḍākinīs through the innate nature.
Joy and the appearance of death are not seen.
Protector, Victor of mind, awaken these beings.
Liberate the realm of illusory sentient beings.
Having heard this great song,
The yogin possessed of the wheel fully awoke.
All beings, like illusions,
Beings both stable and otherwise,
Just as he saw them, he with his own wheel
Dissolved into space and became the great body.
Possessing emanations through the innate and the rest,
The great body in which the two are one,
Having the nature of the Ādibuddha,
Is the Dharma body of all.
Having obtained the yoginī tantra,
One gains confidence in the yoga tantras.
The master Cittavajra, too,
Is the domain of the Madhyamaka way.
From obtaining that reality as outwardly taught
Comes the characteristic of the great Madhyamaka.
The domain of the instructions on tenets
Is not the domain even of the learned.
Tenets are to be known from direct perception;
The instructions, too, from inference.
All is to be known from the guru's mouth;
That wise one is the domain of the buddhas.
It is not the domain of the conceited
Vijñānavādins of the childish class.
From the equal union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

From the Great King of Yoginī Tantras, the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, this is the rite that sets out the characteristics of the generation of the ḍākinīs and the reality of the action of fully enjoying bliss. This is the third chapter.

Next I shall rightly explain
How Lāmā abides.
As it was taught by the buddhas of old,
So I merely make it clear.
The Blessed One is Vajrasaṃvara;
Vārāhī is the yoga saṃvara;
Cakrasaṃvara is Ḍākinī;
And likewise the samaya saṃvara
Was rightly taught by Lāmā.
What is held to be the lotus saṃvara
Is she who has the form of Khaṇḍarohā,
The jewel saṃvara. To the all-pervading lord
Lāmā herself put the question:
"What is the vajra saṃvara and the others like?
O wondrous one, my lord,
How likewise is the union of the vajra samaya,
The lotus wheel, and likewise
The saṃvara of the jewel?"
Then, having worshiped the vajra holder,
Being pleased, he rightly spoke to me:
"Having realized the vajra cave, the wisdom without transference,
In the three vajras,
The saṃvara in the three vajras,
The generation, is the vajra saṃvara.
In all the root samayas,
To be known, the saṃvara that protects
The follower of the path; the worship of the wheel
Is held to be the samaya saṃvara.
The sixfold yoga,
The six unions in the aperture of space,
The saṃvara, all the true realities:
This is my yoga saṃvara.
The lotus is the channel of the avadhūtī;
Abiding in the four wheels,
It is the saṃvara; in those wheels
It is to be declared the lotus saṃvara.
The wheel is the path of the sugatas;
Through generation in the empowerments and the rest
It is the saṃvara; in those dharmas
It is explained to be the Cakrasaṃvara.
The jewel: by the coming that descends
Into the gem at the head of the vajra
It is the saṃvara; the drop of the life breath, too,
Is held to be the jewel saṃvara.
From what has not been explained, through these:
The seventh ḍāka is the supreme wisdom;
Kākāsyā and the others, eight,
And the guide, because of the characteristic of nonduality.
Thus, through the equal union of them all,
It is the ḍāka of the single saṃvara.
Caṇḍālī, the blazing one, as form:
The separate channels and the central one;
From that the wisdom ḍāka is generated.
From the instant that knows all aspects,
In the center of the mind of awakening,
Whatever has the secret form of nectar
Is explained to be Vajraḍāka.
The supreme of those who go in space,
The adventitious, the selfless,
Great magical display, the form of the water-born lotus:
Though the yogin's own mind is without mind,
It is the characteristic of the yoga ḍāka.
The form that embodies the inner wheel,
Through the drops being joined everywhere,
At the vital points and junctions of the characteristics,
Buddhas will be born everywhere.
The assembly embodying outer and inner
Comes from the very nature of the reality of wisdom.
The samaya being, knowing the three,
Is to be known from all the existences
Of birth, of death, and of the intermediate state.
Through abandoning, the samaya ḍāka is born.
Though it is not known by any self,
Beings come to it by the path of the wise.
Passion together with illusion, space:
The lotus ḍāka herself is chief.
The vowels likewise, and yoga,
And the mudrā, and the innate desires:
As they proceed, she is always born.
The blazing jewel empowerment ḍāka:
Likewise the elements, of seven kinds,
Are the characteristic of the seven consciousnesses.
Treasuries of water, mountains and continents too
Have fully entered that great ocean.
That reality embodying the twenty-five
Is the cause of the wisdom of the buddhas.
The encircling range and the rest, emptiness,
Nature, and likewise the person,
Luminosity and all names,
The dharmadhātu and the specific characteristic,
Liberation and buddha likewise,
Wisdom, Vajrasattva, the path, the self,
The source of phenomena and the Great Vehicle,
The way of secret mantra, the all-pervading lord,
The innate and union likewise,
Great compassion itself,
The Selfless Lady and all reality:
Possessed of a nature, empty, again and again.
Therefore, with the name of the ocean,
And by the form of the great ocean:
ā: sentient beings tormented by every fear
Are by nature endowed with the qualities of a buddha.
rṇṇa: they, in their longing for virtue,
Have no weariness and no fatigue.
va: from abandoning all thoughts,
What is attained is the specific characteristic of illusion.
ma: all that embodies the way of secret mantra.
hā: it has the form of generation.
sa: its dharmas are untrue,
mu: because things perish in an instant.
dra: substance, variety, and self-revealed dharmas
Are the characteristic of the great ocean."
The specific characteristic of Lāmā, the grounds,
Just as they are, in their order:
Generated from the intoxication of play,
All the yoginīs are to be known.
The mothers and the rest follow
From the channel paths of the wheel of all.
Or else she will hold illusion;
That is the nature of great illusion.
Through conduct in the sign of the vase,
The number of transferences is the second.
The goddess with Vajravārāhī,
All-enjoying, abides as Lāmā.
The goddess is green in color;
The rest is like Ḍākinī.
All the things of craving and of hatred
Abide upon her.
The color and form of Heruka,
The followers of the tathāgatas,
The remaining yoginīs of the six circles,
The heroes, and all, arise.
Place them in the center of the spokes of the wheel;
Mind and the rest are known as six colors.
The wheel is to be drawn in equal parts;
Likewise the vajra and lotus circles.
The garlands of swords, jewels and curved knives
Are always to be known in their order.
Outside is a garland of crossed vajras,
And garlands of heads all around.
Lāmā is generated from the mind of awakening;
She is the chief of the assembly of yoginīs.
In accord with the times, for the benefit of beings,
She is the characteristic of aspiration.
In the midst of the Fortunate Eon, too,
In the Kṛta age, Vipaśyin and the others
Are to be known at that time;
Likewise Vipaśyin in the northern continent.
In Jambudvīpa, too, in Chudün,
I follow the Śākya clan.
In the remaining five continents
The families of the tathāgatas follow in order.
Therefore Lāmā takes all beings
To the final abode of the supreme buddha.
The mother clearly performs
The deeds of courage of all the yogas.
On the day of the lord of the earth,
She is held to be with the lunar mansion Maghā.
The wind called downward-clearing,
And the channel called Going and Moon,
Until the very time of death,
Even while held, do not decline.
Thus the twelve yoginīs
Are the months, the days and nights, and the winds.
Flowing from channel to channel,
The yoginī generates human beings.
In the entire assembly of the channels
The life breath transfers within an instant.
Having understood that nature,
Through the qualities of hatred and the rest, in order,
She thereby embodies the various emanations,
And through karma the abodes are many.

The single entity of the yogin
Is Lāmā, the characteristic of nirvāṇa.
Even when nirvāṇa and what signifies it
Have both entered in, they are without name.
That which is not known as it is,
I perceive as the supreme.
Buddhas and bodhisattvas would come to be nonexistent;
The Dharma body and the rest are entities.
Through the kindness of the faculties
All who are subject to birth will attain.
Although the reality of the faculties is not true,
At that time one attains through the untrue.
What is the very nature of the two faculties?
It is like the person in a shadow.
Although the shadow as a thing is real,
The form of the person is not real.
Likewise, although the faculties are not true,
Their reality has the characteristic of truth.
The many other kinds of oral instruction
Should be known as supremely praised.
Drink and the like, all one's possessions:
To offer them to the guru is true.
That should be enjoyed as untrue;
One should not hold it in contempt.

Here I shall also explain the mantra.
In this ocean of all the goddesses,
The near-heart mantras
Are adorned with beautiful union.
Body, speech, and mind vajra:
The near-heart is held to be threefold,
By forward order and by its reversal,
And likewise, in the middle, not in forward order.
oṃ oṃ ba hrīḥ jra he lā he mā hūṃ yai hūṃ hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha ṭa pha hūṃ ṭa hūṃ svā hā hā he hrīḥ oṃ oṃ hūṃ he hūṃ he hrīḥ phaṭ oṃ. Thus it is.

By means of the thirty-seven,
In the order of the mistresses of yoga,
The channels and the words are to be known.
Of the nature of the uttered syllables,
Generate the channels of two and of three,
And also of each single syllable.
Or else, by gathering them all together,
This is the time of the Great Self.
Therefore, as the embodiment of all buddhas,
The yoginīs are always born one by one.
If the great being becomes wrathful,
The yoginī is the wrathful one emanated.
Likewise delusion, attachment, and the rest:
The yoginīs are ever emanated.
Accomplishing rites with little effort,
One will attain even Lāmā, the supreme mother.
The Blessed Lord spoke this:
"Each syllable is a yoginī;
Her name is said to be equal to it.
For you it is blended with the supreme vajra."
Thus, constantly relying on that reality,
Together with the yogic consort (mudrā),
One should sport and play expansively.
In the midst of existence and nirvāṇa,
The union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata:
The Blessed Lord spoke this.

This was the fourth chapter of the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, Great King of Yoginī Tantras, on the characteristics of generating Lāmā, the setting out of the mantras, the nature of the six wheel-turning monarchs and the rest, and the presentation of nirvāṇa and the rest.

Next, I shall explain the supreme.
Out of loving concern for beings,
Abiding in the abode of Khaṇḍarohā,
The lord Vajraḍāka:
In the lotuses of the four wheels
The channels are one hundred and twenty.
Their names, as is fitting,
Are explained to one who is a vessel for reality.
She of Madhyadeśa, she of Kaliṅga,
She of Oṭa, she of Karṇāṭa, she of Maru,
She of Saurāṣṭra, she of Malaya, she of Vaṅga,
She of Draviḍa and she of Taliṅgaka,
She of Mālava, she of Mahārāṣṭra,
She of Varendra, she of Kāmarūpa,
She of Ḍāhāla, she of the land of Aṭali,
She of Bhaḍari, she of central Lāḍā,
She of Tirahudati, she of Daddaranaṭa, and she of Nepal,
She of Śrāvastī, she of Rāḍha, and she of Ḍikakari,
She of Bhaṅgala, she of Khaḍa, and she of Harikela,
She of Suvarṇadvīpa and she of Siṅgala,
She of Ḍamaḍa, she of Kantoraka,
She of Sindhu, she of Himālaya, she of Cūṃḍi,
She of Kulūta, she of Jaḍara, she of Patha,
She of Jajjayuta and she of Varuṇa,
She of Oḍiyāna and she of Lampāka,
She of Jālandhara and she of Arbuda,
She of Kashmir, she of Kosala, she of Kāñcī,
She of Jayanta, she of Triśakta, she of Cambha,
She of Luhara and she of Purahorika,
She of Mummunam and she of Kāmboja,
She of Bhaṭṭo, she of Gṛhadevatā,
She of Pretapurī and she of Babbhara,
She of the Pīla and she of the Upapīla,
She of the charnel ground and she of the secondary charnel ground,
She of the shore of the great ocean, she of Khasa,
She of the barbarian lands, and she of all lands:
In order, these are the sixty-four goddesses.
The yoginīs of the navel wheel
Should be known as the channels of the families.
Likewise, in the heart wheel, there are eight,
Messengers who go everywhere:
She of Prayāga, she of Devīkoṭa,
She of Ujjayinī and the Great Glorious One,
She of Jvālāmukhī, she of Siddhaśrimbali,
She of Mahila, Kaumārī, she of Saura.
Thus all of them, in the abode of the heart,
Are field-born ones in the manner of illusion.
And the goddesses of the wheel in the throat,
The supreme chief ladies, I shall explain.
They are the sixteen great portions,
And likewise the sixteen elements:
Blood and semen and marrow,
Sweat and fat and skin,
Flesh and bone and sinew,
Pus and entrails and menstrual blood,
Feces, urine, bile, and saliva.
Rahasya and the rest, the element of fat:
They are the ones who flow with intoxicants.
In the head are the great goddesses,
The thirty-two channels of the wheel.
Throughout the whole sequence of the days,
At all the junctures, they are the great marvelous ones:
Kālī, She Who Bares Her Fangs, the Terrifying One,
Nandā, She Who Has Passed Beyond, Vināyakī,
Cāmuṇḍā and She of Fierce Form,
The goddess Durgā, Sarasvatī,
Bhadrakālī and Mahākālī,
Stout Kālī and Aparājitā,
Jayā and Vijayā,
Ajitā and the Victress,
Raudrī, the Hostile One, Aindrī,
She of the Moon, she of the crossroads,
She who dwells in villages, and the Fierce-Eyed One,
Kāmbojī, Ḍombī,
Caṇḍālī and the Outcaste Woman,
Brāhmaṇī and Śūdrī,
The Princess, She of Great Magic,
And She Who Fills with the Divine Substance, Liquor.
Thus, in the wheel of the channels,
Khaṇḍarohā is the one who moves within.
"Am I the one who moves here?
Let my own lord declare it!"
In all bodies the elements
Become a single entity, like space.
It should be known as with the portions of the earth:
Though divided into regions, the earth is one.
The element that has become piece by piece
Becomes, again, the regions and the days.
Because of this the syllable ḍa is taught.
From the characteristics of egg and womb,
Here consciousness comes to be generated.
In whatever elements, of whatever kind,
It should be known as fourfold.
In action, Khaṇḍarohā herself
Is she who snatches away great evil;
What is slain is all the elements.

Through her, Khaṇḍarohā, the dissolution of beings,
She who performs all actions completely,
The yoginī, the supreme chief lady,
The supreme one, arises from her own transference.
At the time of the rising of the Fish,
With Desire, the lunar mansion, and Supreme Throat,
In the western continent of Godānīya,
She whose nature is great passion,
The goddess who is neither nonentity nor agent,
She who rests on the state of nonduality,
In the center, embodying the thirty-seven,
Is generated, arising from her own mantra:
oṃ ba sa jra rba kha bu ṇḍa ddha ro ba hi jra kā ḍā yai ke hūṃ nī hūṃ ye phaṭ ba phaṭ rṇṇa svā nī hā ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā ha oṃ.
This mantra is to be fully applied.
In all the maṇḍalas one desires,
In the center of each of the goddesses
Is she who is born of the four wheels,
Embodying skillful means and wisdom.
All are in the middle of a source of phenomena,
Properly endowed with four gates,
And these are adorned with archways.
While eating the great flesh,
They proclaim a terrifying sound.
For the wheel of Lāmā, the flesh of cattle;
For Ḍākinī, the flesh of elephants;
For Vārāhī, every kind of flesh:
This is through the application of the pledge and the vow.
Having understood the secret mantra as truth,
It is not the uttering of the syllables of mantra.
Whatever is the speech of all sentient beings,
That is mantra, renowned as the yoginī.
If mantra were a matter of letters,
It would be like the letters of a Goḍi.
In each and every aperture of the channels
The breath is perceived as syllables.
Through the drink of mantra, in the mind,
For as long as there is cessation at the end of the feet,
It moves everywhere, from region to region,
And then, coming also to the central region,
In one's own machine, which is to be satisfied,
The drink flows down as from a vase.
Dwelling on the maṇḍala as one's own seat,
Turn it like the wheel of the life breath.
While it is being turned by the hero,
One should press it into the aperture of the jewel.
In the nine apertures also, the seeds
Should be meditated upon with the vajra and the rest.
Or else, in all the channels,
These syllables of mantra should be set out:
ma ka oṃ kā ma sau ma bi dra ta mā ma bha kā ḍā a bha lā ma ti da ne srā rā ḍi ba khā ha su si ḍā ka si hi cu ku ja pa ja ba au laṃ jā a kā ko kāṃ ja tri ca lu pu muṃ kāṃ bha gri pre ba pi u sma u kha mme: these are at the navel. pri de u ma jva su ma kau: these are at the heart. ra śu ma svā me ca māṃ a snā pū a sva bi mū pi śle: these are of the throat. kṛ ka bhī nā ti bi cā gau o sa bha ma bhru a ja bi a ja go du i ca ca grā rau kāṃ ḍau ca mā brā sū rā: these are at the wheel of the head. Or else they bear the names of Abhedyā and the rest; the first letters of these, too,
All are to be given to the wheel of the head.
This machine, O great-minded one:
The wind of life comes to be held.
In the shape of the petals of four lotuses,
Draw the lines of the wheel of squares in sequence.
All of them in circular form,
The five lines should be drawn.
Eight, sixteen, sixty-four,
And thirty-two squares are held to be.
Having reached the place of the navel,
It turns by the powers of the life force.
As long as there is the wind of the avadhūtī,
So long is this machine declared here.
If one does this by means of speech,
Sing the mantra softly and melodiously.
Having firmly nailed down the life force with sound,
One should block the nine channels.
Free of illness, unaging, free of leprosy,
Always without hunger and thirst:
Limitless benefits such as these,
And one does not end in illness and death.
In the five maṇḍalas, or else
In all that have the machine, one should make it flow down.
The elements, the sense bases, and the rest
Are to be eaten by one endowed with the yoga of nectar.
Red in color, she is perception; for the rest,
She has the arms and the form of Lāmā.
The union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata:
The Blessed Lord spoke this.

This was the fifth chapter of the Glorious Ocean of Ḍākas, Great King of Yoginī Tantras, the rite on the characteristics and generation of Khaṇḍarohā, the brief teaching of the names in the presentation of the channels of the four wheels, the setting out of the mantras, and the rest.

Next, I shall explain another supreme matter,
In brief and not at length.
From the complete union of glorious Heruka,
The virtuous characteristics of Rūpiṇī.
Embodied beings, actions, and the joints
First of all rely on Rūpiṇī.
In a form that embodies outer and inner,
Abiding in bliss, she comes to move.
Here, in all the limbs,
There are twelve joints.
They should be known as five each:
In the twelve there are sixty channels.
She who flows in the maṇḍalas as well
Is held to have twelve transitions.
On the left and on the right as well,
First Aries and the rest, for the knower of mantra;
The remaining ones are the four apertures.
The navel, the groin, and the two flanks
Should likewise be known as the triangle.
Its gates are sixty channels;
The junctures are declared to be fivefold.
Whatever moves along the course of the sun
Moves in the moon and in the months,
In portions of six, five, and thirty;
Likewise it is declared to be renowned in the heart.
This is how the nine channels move:
In the openings of the eyes and the rest,
Relying on the gates, they come to abide.
In the throat, whatever kind of element there is,
Having become that, abides.
Abhedyā and the rest, thirty-two,
Are praised in many tantras.
The goddess abiding in that form:
Conceiving of that Rūpiṇī,
I ask the Blessed One:
"Why is she called by the name Rūpiṇī?"
"She renders all beings formless,
By names such as the bliss of the channels.
Beings, too, on the pathways of the faculties,
Whatever their form, she is Rūpiṇī of that form.
The bliss obtained from the father
Comes from the union of the two organs.
Whatever family belongs to whichever being,
That is explained as that one's destiny.
She leads out from all the truths;
One does not go other than by the way of mantra.
Therefore, through companionship with Rūpiṇī,
Seek the coemergent, perfect awakening."
The great goddess is yellow in color;
For the rest she is like Khaṇḍarohā.
The form of the aggregate of formations,
Name, woman, and the rest: Rūpiṇī.
Peerless, for the benefit of sentient beings,
She comes to be born in this continent.
Having the nature of Aries and the Bird, of Kula,
Of the month of Caitra, and of the signs,
Through the bliss of skillful means and wisdom also,
She dwells in the center of the maṇḍala.

Truly embodying the thirty-seven,
In due order, the yoga of the heroes,
In the application of secret mantra and color,
Should be known as that of Rūpiṇī, the chief lady:
oṃ bajra graṃ rū na pi maḥ nī hi yi oṃ ke svā hūṃ hā hūṃ hu pha oṃ ṭa bau pha ṣa ṭa ṭa he svā hā hūṃ hūṃ hoḥ oṃ phaṭ phaṭ haṃ.
Thus it should be known from its nature.
Through the action of the earlier rite,
Having reached the aperture of the central channel,
The breath, whose essence is consciousness,
Sees in that instant the countless buddha fields,
All of them without exception,
All having the essence of one's own self.
Even what is meditated upon, heard, and seen
Is not realized while one is traversing.
This is the very characteristic of illusion.
The form body, too, is threefold,
The characteristics of body, speech, and mind;
It is the desire, form, and formless realms.
Primordially complete, as in a mirror,
Abiding just as it is in the three realms,
Her own form and other forms: Rūpiṇī.
