She Whose Mind Is on Wealth, She Parted from Union,
Those Whose Minds Are on Children, She Who Grieves,
She of Meditative Concentration, She Who Recites Mantras,
Shame, Pride, She Who Is Tormented,
She Who Strives to Benefit Sentient Beings,
She Whose Mind Is on Kings, She Who Harms Others,
She Who Has Attained Wisdom, the Ascetic,
The Old One, She Who Thinks of Death,
the Happy One, the Suffering One, the Nonvirtuous One,
She Who Exists and She Who Does Not Exist,
She Whose Mind Is on the Guru, She Who Goes,
The Patient One, the Impatient One, the Weary One,
the Rested One, and the Hungry One, supreme:
The goddesses who perform all actions.
This wheel of nature is great.
Its color is known to be that of its own wheel,
And the rest is as before.
The goddesses are of the nature of wisdom and means.
This maṇḍala of nature
Is explained in the Tantra of the Four Seats,
In its twelve thousand,
And they are held to be the offering goddesses
Of the wisdom ḍākinīs and the rest.
The place at the edge of the village is like this,
And it is the ground Universal Light.
Thus, at the end of the bodies of wisdom and the rest,
In what is called the wheel encircled
By water, fire, wind and space,
The supreme vajra lady is to be known.
This is the second of the wheel of speech, the wheel of emanation.

Then the wheel of body is explained,
With the colors of body, speech and mind.
In the center of its thirty-six spokes
Are the wheel-turning ladies of the yoginīs:
She of the realm of the Four Great Kings,
The wheel-turner of the Thirty-Three,
She of Yāma, the wheel-turner of Tuṣita,
She of Delight in Emanation, and likewise
She Who Controls Others' Emanations,
The wheel lady of the Brahmā Assembly,
Likewise She of the Ministers of Brahmā,
The wheel-turner of Great Brahmā,
She of Limited Light, She of Immeasurable Light,
She of Clear Light, She of Limited Virtue,
The wheel-turner of Immeasurable Virtue,
She of Vast Virtue, She of the Cloudless,
The wheel of She Born of Merit,
The wheel-turner of Great Fruit,
The wheel lady of the Not Great and the Untroubled,
She of Excellent Vision and She of Good Appearance,
The wheel-turner of Akaniṣṭha,
She of the sphere of infinite space,
She of the sphere of infinite consciousness,
She of the sphere of nothingness,
She of neither perception
Nor non-perception, the hell woman, the pretī,
The animal, the human woman, the asura woman,
Likewise She Who Moves in Celestial Palaces,
The Moon, the Sun, Yamī,
And Indrāṇī are to be known as the wheel-turners.
They are to be made the color of their own wheel,
Their hands and weapons as before.
They are of the nature of wisdom and means,
And dwell perfectly at the near edge of the village.
The ground is the Conduct of Aspiration.
These are the twelve wheels.

This wheel is square,
And is to be perceived as the emanation body (nirmāṇakāya).
Its five lines are the mirror and the rest,
All marked with the defining characteristics.
Made by the twice eight goddesses,
It holds the four offering maṇḍalas.
Outside are the eight charnel grounds.
The female door guardians, in their due order:
In the east meditate on Gaurī, the Water-Born;
In the north is Gāndhārī;
In the west is Vajra Dancer;
In the south, she with the face of a mare.
In the corners are four goddesses,
In order beginning with Īśāna's quarter:
The goddess Vajra Blazing Face,
Vajra Frowning Face,
Vajra Cleaver, and Caṇḍī;
Their colors and the rest are as before.
They are great fierce women with bared fangs,
Made beautiful by garlands of flame.
The color and form of the heroes
Are just like those of all the yoginīs.
All are adorned with garlands of heads;
The heroes wear garlands of silk.
Those heroes have crowns of matted locks,
And all their limbs are smeared with ash.
The yoginīs are tall and garlanded,
Adorned with bracelets and crest ornaments,
Perfectly complete in every characteristic,
Born from the family of Vārāhī.
The names of all the heroes,
Supreme, beginning with the first wheel, are:
Vajraḍāka, and likewise
Viśvaḍāka and Padmaḍāka,
Ratnaḍāka and Khaṇḍakapāla,
Mahākaṅkāla and Kaṅkāla,
Vikaṭadaṃṣṭra and Suravairin,
Amitābha and Vajraprabha,
Vajradeha and Aṅkurika,
Likewise Vajrajaṭila,
Mahāvīra and Vajrahūṃkāra,
Subhadra and Vajrabhadra,
Mahābhairava and Virūpākṣa,
Mahābala and Ratnavajra,
Hayagrīva and Ākāśagarbha,
Heruka and Padmanarteśvara,
Vairocana and Vajrasattva,
Mahābala and Jñānaḍāka,
The Steadfast-Minded, Liberation, and Wisdom,
Means, and the Vajra of Mind.
The remaining names are like those of the goddesses,
To be given masculine forms.
For the eleven wheels,
The doors and the rest are likewise.
For the goddesses of the central lotus,
The principal is to be made as the lord.
However, in the wheels of the lord and the rest,
Just as the Victors of the Fortunate Eon
Number one thousand less four,
All of them are to be visualized.
Their names are distinguished by the divisions of their qualities;
They are of the nature of emanation bodies.
Whichever yoginī arises first
In whichever wheel,
Through her the twelve are to be known:
They are the women of all conduct, of the sacred sites and the secondary sites.
The rest, of the thirteenth ground,
Are the women dwelling at the doors, the corners, and so on.
At the time of offering, praise, and the like,
One should first know the vajra name;
So too for the heroes.
The outer charnel grounds are to be explained:
The first is known as the Burned,
The second as the Unburned,
The third as the one become Fragments,
The fourth as the Untouched,
The fifth is called the Terrifying,
The sixth the Frightening,
The seventh the Bright and Hollowed,
And the eighth is so named from those hung from above.
In the eight charnel grounds there are always
The eight great hell guardians.
The trees are the śālmali, the aśoka,
The pārijāta, and likewise the udumbara,
The ḍombira and the gambhari,
The juniper and the myrobalan.
There are hosts of various vetālas,
Hosts of yoginīs and of heroes,
Sky-goers, and other earth-goers,
Whoever they may be, of great miraculous power;
Headless ones running, and others,
Headless ones dancing,
Ones lying down, ones without legs,
Ones with heads and bodies cut off;
Through the cause of great meditative absorption
They all dance with joy.
Thus, likewise, in the center
Various constellations are to be made,
Their various colors to be known
According to whatever mounts they have.
As the nature of the cause of perfect awakening,
The complete maṇḍala is to be meditated.
Whatever the causal vajra is like,
Just so it is to be emanated again.
The animal-faced goddesses
Come bearing all the rites.
Having summoned them together with the host of wrathful ones,
First the obstructors are to be dispelled.
Commanded by Sumbha, the vajra-holder,
The one bound by the vows is to drive in the kīlas.
oṃ sumbhani sumbha kāyikāṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ grīhṇa grīhṇa vācikāṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ grīhṇapaya gṛhṇapaya citasikāṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ānaya hoḥ bhagavān vajrajñānikāṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
They are to be placed in the directions beginning with the east.
In the center of the vajra tent,
Below, it becomes the vajra ground;
Above is the net of arrows,
The vajra fence, and the canopy.
Within the source of phenomena (dharmodaya), too,
It is to be meditated as three-, four-, five-,
And six-cornered.
In the order of the four tiers,
All are to be arranged here in sequence.
oṃ gha gha ghātaya ghātaya sarva du phaṭ kīlaya kīlaya sarva pāpaṃ phaṭ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ vajra kīlaya vajradharod ājñāpayati sarva vighnan kāya vāka citta vajra kīlaya hūṃ phaṭ
This is the mantra for driving in the kīla.
oṃ vajra muṅgara vajra kīlā koṭaya hūṃ phaṭ
This is the mantra for striking.
Thus the heroes of all the wheels
Arise from the family of the Blessed Vajra.
They are emanation bodies without exception;
The yoginīs are vessels of awakening.

This is the third chapter: the wheel of body, which is the nature of the three bodies, together with emanation.

Then the stages of the sacred sites, the secondary sites, and the rest
Were taught just as they are.
The sacred sites are Pūrṇagiri,
Jālandhara, Oḍḍiyāna,
And likewise Arbuda.
Godāvarī is a secondary site;
Rāmeśvara, Devīkoṭa,
And Mālava too are secondary sites.
Kāmarūpa, and likewise Oḍra,
Triśakuni, and Kosala are fields.
Kaliṅga and Lampāka,
Kāñcī, and Himālaya are secondary fields.
Pretapurī and Gṛhadevatā,
Saurāṣṭra and Suvarṇadvīpa
Are meeting places. The secondary meeting places
Are Nagara, Sindhu, Maru,
And Kulutā, which too is a secondary meeting place.
The gathering places are the bank of a river,
A garden, what is declared to be the ocean,
And the crossroads. The secondary gathering places
Are the mountain peak, the middle of a village,
The place of gatherings, the mountain of Kaumārī,
And the family field, which too is a secondary gathering place.
The charnel grounds are Mumuniya,
Conduct, Harikela,
And the City of Illusion.
The secondary charnel grounds are the mountain's edge,
The field of mantra, and the city of the gods.
Karṇāṭa and Paṭa
Are likewise declared to be village borders.
The village borders are Koṅkana itself
And the land of the Pervading Lord's deeds;
The Yoginī and the Dense City
Are likewise here the near village borders.

The forest of the fathers, the side of a house,
A pond, and a pool:
Whatever maidens are in these places,
Born from their own birthplace as the innate,
Are to be known in land after land,
Endowed with wisdom and abiding in nonduality.
Through the twelve wheels,
By the divisions of the arrow, the site-born ones,
The twelve sites and the rest, are fully declared,
Caused by the thirty-six elements.
In the elements of each one, too,
The sense sources beginning with the eye,
In the twelvefold meaning of the truths,
Are to be known as the innate yoginīs.

With the following verse
Empowerment is to be received:
"Just as, the moment he was born,
The tathāgatas gave him the bath,
So too I shall be bathed,
And purified with divine water."
Between the vajra and space
All the buddhas have arrived here.
They, arisen as rays of wisdom light,
Confer empowerment with streams of self-arisen nectar,
In order to fill one with the innate water
Of the vajra lord.
oṃ sarva tathāgatābhiṣekata samaya śriye huṃ
This is the mantra of empowerment.

Whatever wheel it may be,
In sealing it with its own,
The yoginīs are the lords;
It is not sealed by another pervading lord.
The wisdom being and the absorption being, too,
Are to be meditated in all the wheels.
With the mass of light arising from them,
The wisdom wheel is to be summoned
With the syllables jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ;
This is to be learned from the Guru's instructions.
The various heroes and yoginīs
Are to be made to offer to the principal.
The two yogas, of the drop
And of the great subtle one, are to be fully visualized.
The drops of the twelve wheels, too,
Are signified in the forms of vajras.
The channels below and above the navel,
A thousand of them, possess drops.
The drops of the ten tips
Are signified in their due order.
The hundred, smoke and the rest,
Are to be made to descend into the place of the drop.
Therefore, whatever the subtle one may be,
Contemplate that it is not the buddhas' sphere of activity.
By gazing at the vajra life winds,
That great seal is the buddha.
As for the recitation of the secret mantra:
With these, the root of more than eight feet,
The forty and the rest,
Complete in all the characteristics,
With great bliss and the rest, the excellent,
Wholly free of all duality,
And the offering, the nature of the three realms,
Is made with all the origins of things.

Gaurī and the rest, the wheel-turning women of the six classes of beings,
The goddesses of both the form and the formless realms,
Through all their own pure natures,
Will worship the wheel without remainder.
Meditate on the maṇḍala of great bliss!
The twelve yogas are merit without evil.
Have faith in all as undefiled by nature!
Know even that lowly maṇḍala to be bliss!
Have faith in the delusion of the senses as great bliss!
In that instant there is no essence of self or other.
With the great form of manifold aspects,
Emanate the maṇḍala wheel throughout the three worlds!

Adorned with mantra, mudrā, and the rest,
The great yogin gives the torma.
Following a recitation of a hundred thousand,
By reciting ten million there is great accomplishment.
Just as was declared,
When one enjoys the various pledge substances,
The yogin becomes accomplished; then
He may dwell wherever he pleases.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the fifteenth chapter of the great king of tantras, the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra, setting out the genuine meditative absorption of the Blessed One and teaching various instructions.

Then I shall explain the extraction
Of the root mantra: listen!
In the center of the source of phenomena,
Then draw this wheel.
In the center, the nature of five segments;
Outside it, draw twelve.
A circle is to be given, and again in it
Doors and corners, four of each.
In the eight-spoked and four-cornered figure
Are the compartments, the vowels, and the consonant classes.
The lord dwelling there is to be extracted,
From whom flow all desires and bliss.
The letters of the five classes and the rest
Are in the center; then the twelve vowels;
The neuters at the four doors;
In the corners, they are in pairs.
The mantrin should know the pervading lord
As at Agni's quarter and the rest.
ka in the center, ca in the east, ṭa in the south, ta in the west,
pa in the north, in their due order.
The last of the west is to be taken,
And likewise the last of the north.
The second letter of Agni's quarter
Is to be taken, and the water seed of Īśāna's.
The third letter of the east,
And likewise the third of the south.
The first of the middle is to be taken,
And the seed of the last of the north.
The second of Īśāna's quarter is to be taken,
And that, likewise, the first again.
The fire seed of Nairṛti's quarter,
And likewise the water of Īśāna's.
The letter ra of the rākṣasa's quarter is to be taken,
And the second letter of Agni's.
Take the fire seed of the spirit's quarter,
And likewise the wind of Agni's.
This is the first word of the mantra;
Its vowels are to be pronounced.
With the strong dot, and fully endowed,
The second letter is adorned.
Pressed by the fourth segment,
The third is adorned with fire.
The fifth letter is adorned
In its lower part with the letter ra.
The learned one adorns the sixth letter
With the second segment.
The ninth is pressed by that same one,
And the tenth too by the fourth.
The twelfth is pressed by that same one,
And the thirteenth too by that same one.
On the fourteenth, water
Is to be arranged in the lower part.
Pressed by the second segment
Is the supreme fifteenth letter.
Thus the mantrin, knowing it well,
Does this for all the feet.
The first of the west is to be taken,
And the fourth of the north.
The water of Īśāna's quarter is to be taken,
And again the last of the south;
The seed of the first of the middle,
And likewise the last of the north.
The first of the middle is to be taken,
The seed of the last of the north;
The water of Īśāna's quarter is to be taken,
The seed of the last of the south,
And the letter of Agni's wind:
This is declared to be the second foot.
It is pressed by the third segment;
The first is marked below with fire.
With the fifth segment the second
Letter is adorned below.
With the first of the north
The sixth is marked below.
The eighth letter too is the same as that,
Set apart by the second segment.
The tenth is pressed by that same one.
It destroys all that is harmful,
Turns the wheel of Dharma,
And brings every untroubled accomplishment.

The third of the west is to be taken,
The seed of the second of the spirit's quarter,
The seed of the first of the west,
And again the first of the south,
The fourth of the north,
The letter of the second of the spirit's quarter;
The last of the south is to be taken,
The fourth of the north;
Take the fire letter of the spirit's quarter,
And likewise the water of Īśāna's,
And the seed of Agni's wind:
This is the third foot of the mantra.
The learned one presses the first letter
With the eleventh segment.
With the ninth segment, too,
The head of the second letter is adorned,
And the very first of the south
Is adorned below with fire.
What is to be given to the third of the middle
Is to be arranged in the lower part.
Pressed by the fourth segment
Is the fifth letter, for the learned one.
The eighth segment is to be given
To the eighth letter.
The tenth is pressed by the second:
It is supreme for cutting down the hostile;
It cuts down the abodes and non-abodes
Of gods, asuras, and humans.
The seed of the second of Agni's quarter,
The letter of the first of the west,
The seed of the second of the wind's quarter,
Likewise the other of Īśāna's,
The letter of the second of the wind's quarter,
The letter of the last of the west,
And again the first of the west,
And Agni's wind are to be taken.
This is declared to be the fourth foot;
It fully reveals all things.
In the lower part of the fifth letter
The letter ra is to be arranged.
On the sixth, the seventh segment,
And again on the seventh, fire
Adorning it below; the second is to be given.
It is the inexhaustible state of omniscience.
The water of Īśāna's quarter is to be taken,
And again the letter dya, the self-arisen;
The seed of the first of the west,
The third of the east as well,
The other of Īśāna's is to be taken,
And again Agni's wind:
This is the fifth foot, with six;
It awakens the yoginīs.
The seed of the first of the middle,
And again that same letter,
The great power of wind is to be taken,
And again the last of the north,
The seed of the wind's ground,
The fourth of the north,
The fire seed of the rākṣasa's quarter,
And again the last of the south;
The third of the middle is to be taken,
And likewise Agni's wind.
To the fifth and the rest vowels are to be given;
It emits and gathers in.
The first letter, the all-going,
Is to be pressed by the third segment.
The fourth seed, too, pressed by that same one,
Is the most supreme in beauty.
The fifth is pressed by the second segment,
And is held to be set on a seat of water.
In that fifth foot
All purposes are joined.
The first letter, being pressed
By the eleventh, is beautiful.
Pressed by the second segment
Is to be pronounced the second letter.
The fourth and fifth letters
Are thoroughly pressed by that same one.
The eighth letter is fully adorned
With the segment of the ninth.
The tenth is pressed by the second segment
And set apart below with fire:
This is to be known as the sixth foot;
It fully accomplishes all desires.
The first of the middle is to be taken,
And likewise the first of the south,
The seed of the wind's ground;
The mantra declared as ka ṣa is to be extracted.
The fourth of the north is to be taken,
Likewise the third of the east,
And having extracted Agni's wind,
This is the seventh foot, the inexhaustible.
The first letter of the pervading lord
Is pressed by the ninth compartment.

The second letter is adorned.

The learned one makes the seat of the fifth.

Is pressed by the second segment.

This is to be known as the eighth foot;
It utterly destroys all evil.
Pressed by the second segment
Is the first letter, with a seat of water.
The learned one's second letter
Is pressed by the second segment.
Pressing with the fourth segment
Is to be perceived on the fourth letter.
Pressing with the second segment
Is to be known on the seventh letter together with
The last of the west.
It brings all enemies to an end.

The fire seed of Nairṛti's quarter,
The seed of its second,
The letter of the first of the north;
The second of Nairṛti's is to be taken,

Gladdens the heart of the ḍākinī.

Is called the tenth; it slays the hostile.

Below the end of the fourth, is joined with fire.

To the eighth, the seat of the fifth;

It terrifies the great terrors.

And again the last of the north,
The seed of the second of the middle,

The eleventh foot of the mantra
Utterly destroys the hosts of māras.
The first letter, pressed by
The third segment, is beautiful.
On the fourth letter, too,
The fifth segment is made its seat.

The wisdom seed is fully extracted,

Delights the minds of all people.

The last of the north is to be taken,
The third of the southern quarter,
And Īśāna's water is to be taken;
From Vāyu's corner, the earth;
And, having extracted that same one again,
On the third of the central row,
That which has the last of the west as its seat,
The second letter of Agni,
The fire seed of Nairṛti,
And again that same one is to be extracted;
Agni's wind is to be taken:
This is the forty-first foot of the mantra.
The first is marked with the seat
Of the fifth compartment and with the bindu;
The second, too, by the second compartment;
On the fourth, by the fourth;
On the eighth, too, by that same one;
On the ninth, by the second segment.
The first of the northern quarter,
The rākṣasa's lord of the sun,
And the first of the central row are taken;
Then that which has Indra's gate as its seat,
The first of the western quarter,
The seed of Agni's wind,
And the first of the west, once taken,
Mounted upon Vajrasattva.
The last of the north is to be taken;
The first letter of the west,
With ra as the seat upon that same one;
Likewise the last of the west,
The seed of Agni's second,
Again the last of the west,
And Agni's wind are to be taken:
This is the forty-second foot.
On the fifth, it is marked by the eleventh;
On the seventh, too, by that same one;
On the ninth, by the eleventh and the second segment.
This secret mantra is declared the great destroyer.
The last of the north is to be taken,
The earth's edge of Īśāna's corner,
And the sun of the spirit's corner;
That known as ka ṣa is itself extracted;
The seed of the first of the central row,
The rākṣasa's son of fire,
And Agni's wind, too, are to be taken:
This is the forty-third foot.
On the second, the fourth and the sixth
The second segment is joined.
As the essence of protecting the yogin
It is to be known from the letters.
The third of the western quarter;
And again, to Nairṛti's second
The first of the south is given as its seat;
Likewise the last of the west;
Īśāna's water is to be taken;
Again the third of the west,
The earth of Vāyu's corner,
And likewise the last of the west:
The forty-fourth foot,
For those who commit the five deeds of immediate retribution.
The Pervading Lord: the first is beautified
By the seat of the fifth segment;
The second is joined with the second segment;
And again on the third,
With the second segment and the bindu;
With the third, on the fourth letter;
Likewise on the fifth and seventh,
With the second and seventh compartments.
The fourth of the western quarter,
The fire seed of Nairṛti
That has the last of the north as its seat,
The fourth of the western quarter,
And Agni's wind are to be taken;
The first letter of the central row,
Nairṛti's son of fire,
And again Agni's wind.
The second letter of the mantra
Is marked by the ninth compartment,
And again the sixth letter
Is joined with the second compartment:
This mantra, the basis of all phenomena,
Is the forty-fifth foot.
Having taken the first of the central row,
It is illuminated by the seat of the sun.
The fourth of the western quarter;
On the letter of Vāyu's second,
That which has the second of the north as its seat;
Nairṛti's Vajrasattva;
Vāyu's second is taken,
Having the second of the north as its seat;
The spirit's son of fire,
Likewise the last of the south,
And Agni's wind are to be taken:
This is the forty-sixth foot.
The first letter is beautified
By being marked with the ninth compartment;
On the third, fifth and seventh
The second segment is joined.
Having extracted the third compartment,
On the third of the western quarter,
Joined with the seat of the sun;
The third of the eastern quarter,
The earth of Vāyu's corner,
The second letter of that same one,
Joined with the letter ra, and Īśāna's water;
The letter of Vāyu's second;
On the very first of the west,
That which has Īśāna's water as its seat;
On the first of the western quarter, too,
The seat is the last of the north;
And the last of the west, once taken,
Is the forty-seventh foot.
On the first, being marked by the eleventh
Is the supreme of beauty;
On the third, with the second;
On the eighth, too, with that same one;
On the tenth, with the seventh itself:
The mantra is the sameness of all grounds.
On the third of the central row
The letter ra is to be joined;
On the seed of Vāyu's second,
Then, with the seat of the first of the west,
Īśāna's water is to be taken;
The third of the western quarter
And the first of the north are taken,
To be illuminated by the seat of the sun;
Īśāna's water is to be taken;
The third of the western quarter
And the last of the north are to be taken;
The earth's edge of Īśāna's corner
And the last of the north are to be taken;
And the first letter of the west.

On the third letter of the mantra
It is joined with the second compartment;
On the sixth, too, that same one is joined;
On the ninth, too, with that same one;
On the eleventh the seventh is joined.
The perfection of wisdom itself,
The forty-eighth foot of the secret mantra,
Was spoken by the Hero.
All the bhūtas on the earth,
And gods, nāgas and the rest, become terrified.
The power of this secret mantra
Has never been, nor will it ever come to be, elsewhere.
Buddhas, bodhisattvas and the rest,
When the secret mantra is spoken with their names,
Are exceedingly pleased, as by a hundred thousand recitations,
And grant accomplishments.
With the syllable oṃ at the beginning of every mantra,
And likewise hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ at the end,
Joined to each of the feet,
They are recited short and long.
Gods and world protectors,
Yakṣas, Indra and universal monarchs,
And the beings born from the four modes of birth,
And the guide, gentle Vajrasattva,
Are accomplished by mere recitation;
They are also accomplished when the mantrin reads it aloud.
The yoginīs, in the order of their families,
Are to be known at the time of conduct.
In order at the beginning and end of the letters,
And on each and every foot as well:
In order to destroy all obstructors
It is to be recited ten thousand times.

haṃ hāṃ hiṃ hīṃ huṃ hūṃ haṃ heṃ haṃ haṃ haṃ hāṃ garbha garbha jāti jāti debānaṃ dāmaya dāmaya sarba śarīrāya hūṃ jaya jaya hūṃ bijaya bijaya hūṃ sara sara hūṃ bisara bisara hūṃ agnaye hūṃ baruṇe hūṃ analāya hūṃ bārāya hūṃ ākāśāya hūṃ sarba rogopaharāya hūṃ biṣabhakṣaṇāya hūṃ yantrabāhanāya hūṃ kālāgnaye hūṃ debāya hūṃ yamāya hūṃ kuberāya hūṃ detyāya hūṃ indrāya hūṃ bhūtāya hūṃ sarba satvāya hūṃ barṅhacakrāya hūṃ dharmadhatave hūṃ mukhāya hūṃ netrāya hūṃ kapālamāline hūṃ bhujāya hūṃ astrāya hūṃ caraṇāya hūṃ jaṭāmukuṭāya masa masa gasa gasa rasa rasa bhara bhara maṭa maṭa traṭa traṭa ghaṭa ghaṭa muñca muñca ghorāndhakārapriyāya phiṃ phiṃ phuda phuda rama rama paramārtharūpiṇe śāntikarāya puṣṭibardhanāya lokapālarūpiṇe sarba śarīrarūpāya bṛkṣagulmolatāya sarbarūpiṇī sarbabastune māraṇāya mārāpaṇarūpāya bhakṣaya bhakṣaya bhakṣanarūpaya niropamāya niropamasukhabe hasa hasa bilasa bilasa nṛtya nṛtya nṛtyāpaya nṛtyāpaya tvindriyayogāya nisvabhāvamahātmane śastradhara nivaraṇāya nānābhicaracchedani cumba cumba hulu hulu culu culu hūṃ ha hūṃ hā hūṃ hi hūṃ hī hūṃ hu hūṃ hū hūṃ he hūṃ hai hūṃ ho hūṃ hau hūṃ haṃ hūṃ haḥ hu huṃ hriṃ hrīṃ

juṃ jūṃ kroṃ krauṃ phi phīṃ phuṃ phūṃ pheṃ phaiṃ phoṃ phauṃ phaṃ phāṃ phaḥ phāḥ kuca kuca traṭa traṭa maḍa maḍa raṭha raṭha daṭha daṭha jaṭha jaṭha madana madana hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā
This is the root mantra.

From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the sixteenth chapter, on the characteristics of the rite of extracting the root mantra, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then something further is to be explained with care:
The armor of embodied beings.
At the heart of Vajrasattva
The seal is to be set through union with the āli.
The wisdom of the vajra essence
And the being that is one with space:
The heart is subtle consciousness;
The āli is Caṇḍī, the consort,
And the union, too, is called unchanging.
In this way all the faculties are sealed.
Therefore the protection, too, is the letters oṃ ha,
The letter that is forever indestructible.
The rest, at the end of uttering Vairocana,
Is the sound that comes from the movement of the vajra.
Therefore, just as it is clearly
Uttered, the form arisen from that utterance
Is in the center of the heart of human beings:
The maṇḍala whose nature is thirty-seven.
Embraced by Vajravārāhī as well,
That protection in the wheel of the navel
Is the form of the mantra oṃ baṃ.
The beautiful central one and the vajra holder:
This union is the great protection,
Arisen perfectly at the very first, without precedent.
The secret mantra of all the wheels, too,
Which grants every accomplishment, I shall explain:
paṃ hā bāṃ hi bi hī bī hu bu hū bū he be hai bai ho bo hau bau haṃ baṃ haḥ baḥ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ svāhā svāhā
By the nature of short and long,
All is of the nature of wisdom and means.
When Vajravārāhī
Abides in the form of the chief goddess,
The letters of the mantra
Beginning with oṃ paṃ are to be bestowed.
The wheel of wisdom is the place of birth,
And the seat, too, is held to be secret.
All turns into the form of bliss:
Stable bliss and moving bliss,
White bliss and black bliss,
Likewise bliss born everywhere,
The bliss of wisdom and the bliss of means;
Vajrasattva is remembered as bliss.
Self-arisen bliss, for human beings,
Is in oneself as the maṇḍala in the Kṛta age.
This protection, in the tantra of the Kṛta age,
Is what brings forth the Kṛta age itself.
Know the various worlds as the Kṛta age,
And likewise the various realms as the Kṛta age;
In the various world realms
All the ages are to be known in this way.
At a single time the Kṛta and the other ages
Do not arise in the world realms.
By having served the Blessed One,
One lives pervaded everywhere by bliss.
Where it is the age of strife, there is the Kṛta age;
Where it is the Kṛta age, there is the age of strife;
There too are the Tretā and the Dvāpara,
Each one paired with a Dvāpara.
The yoga tantras are thirty-six,
And they arose from the gathering of all without remainder.
Through the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the seventeenth chapter, the rite characterizing the generation of the armor of Vajrasattva and Vārāhī, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then, once more, the protection of armor
Belonging to Vairocana:
The seal is set at the head, and its
Secret mantra is namaḥ hi itself.
At the time of going upward, the yogin
Should know, at each one's proper time,
The actions arising from whatever bliss.
The mantra that protects manifestly, with devotion,
Is to be applied to the stainless head:
This is the wheel of great bliss.
It is to be known as the seat of yoga,
Likewise embraced by Yamī.
The self born from the mantra oṃ yaṃ,
Through the cause that is wisdom and means:
yā ro, mī ca, nī ni:
In this way the elements are to be joined.
The body and all the rest dissolve,
And arise as pair upon pair.
Therefore the wisdom of the third
Arises as Yamī.
The means of bliss, which is like transference,
Comes from the single quarter of the Tretā age.
Wherever appearance is desired,
Vairocana himself goes there.
The form in the aspect of the chief
Is again among the thirty-seven.
Arisen from the letters of the mantras,
It is to be known in the center of the forehead.
Here, of the mantras explained before,
Whatever is the first letter of each,
All the mantras of this one, in order,
Are to be known as that one's and that one's.
All the rest are equal:
Meditation and recitation are supreme.
All is neither existent nor yet nonexistent;
It goes everywhere, yet it is not everything.
By illusion all are deluded;
By illusion itself the wise are freed.
Beneficial is illusion, beneficial is wisdom,
And the generation of the mind of awakening is beneficial too.
Such is the meaning of namaḥ hi.
O wise one, the rest is bliss:
Because Vairocana comes to blaze,
That bliss is Vairocana's.
The knowledge-woman who subdues Yama:
She is spoken of as Yamī.
Because, by keeping this protection in mind,
One carries out one's tasks at all times.
The wisdom of the heart and the forehead
Has the characteristic of nondual wisdom.
For the great purpose of the yoginīs
All the tantras were brought forth.
In all the tantras there is
No other yoga that purifies;
The great yoga of this tantra
Is the purifier in all the tantras.
With Yamī, the chief goddess of the maṇḍala,
Then, again, the activity:
Liberation from entering the womb,
And the two, birth and transference:
These are Yamī and Vairocana.
There form and the formless come about;
Wisdom that has both pleasure and pain;
The divine substance is of one taste with the mudrā.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the eighteenth chapter, the rite of the armor protection of Vairocana and the others, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the armor protection
Of Padmanarteśvara was declared:
The fire of desire, as though blazing,
Going to the crown of the head, is Mahābala;
With the mantra letters svāhā hūṃ,
And through the illusion of the deluding goddess, hriṃ moṃ,
The nature of wisdom and means
Is the lord of the wheel of space.

In the lotus of the mouth the deluding goddess, too,
Protects what has taste and what has none.
When tongue and tongue come together,
The yogin, tasting that drop,
When supreme joy has arisen,
Finds no one who has wisdom, and no one joined with it.
Through relying on men and women,
What is called discipline is one's own mind.
The assemblies of joyful speech
Come about through the cause of proclaiming the Dharma.
The aperture of the mind of awakening:
There the breath moves.
Various kinds of apertures are explained;
Through them the breath manifestly moves.
To all the apertures hriṃ is to be given:
Unchanging, supremely peaceful.
moṃ yaṃ is the field, at the head,
Gathering from the supreme unchanging.
Delusion, which binds one to existence,
Leads one fully onto the true path;
Therefore she is known as the Deluding Goddess,
The destroyer of the wheel of existence.
There is no existence, nor any supreme existence:
That is declared to be union.
Through the application of that very mantra,
The maṇḍala whose nature is thirty-seven,
The chief, hero or goddess,
At the crown of the head and in the center of the mouth,
Gives protection everywhere
And destroys mere conceptual thought.
The lotus is ultimate wisdom,
The form of the awakening of the buddhas;
The relative knower and known
Are what is called the Lord of Dance.
The form of the bodhisattvas:
Through awakening itself all is realized.
Because every emanation becomes deluded,
The goddess becomes the Deluding Goddess.
The lotus becomes the source of phenomena;
The Lord of Dance is the vajra;
The Lord is to be known as the innate;
The mind of awakening is movement.
Being without intrinsic nature, and permanent bliss:
That is declared to be union.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the nineteenth chapter, establishing the protective armor mantras of Padmanarteśvara and the others, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the protection of Heruka,
The armor, will be explained once more.
It comes to the throat and the shoulders,
And the yoginī is at the head.
bau ṣa ṭa he, the peerless mantra,
Protects the elements and what arises from the elements.
hreṃ hriṃ is the mantra of the All-Moving Goddess;
Therefore it is of the nature of wisdom and means.
he is the yoga whose nature is mind;
Again, ru is of the nature of speech;
ka is the yoga of the very nature of body.
Beneath the earth, upon the earth, and the higher world:
At the beginning, end and middle of bliss,
bau ṣa ṭa he are the seeds;
hreṃ hriṃ are to be known as below and above.
The life wind is the movement.
Exhaling, growing familiar with joy,
And the time of practicing the vase breath:
At that time the wheel
Is to be known as having the nature of thirty-seven.
Thus, for the four times,
It is held to be in its proper order.
Through the yoga of that very mantra
Worship becomes supremely wondrous.
Thus, of the eight consciousnesses,
One, two, three and five
Are realized in their proper order
Through the yoga of the four chapters.
The conduct tantra arose
In the third chapter,
And the action tantra in the fourth chapter.
The tantra is self-movement,
And the seat of suchness, once arisen,
Is declared again in the third;
In the fourth, the seat of another
Having arisen, wisdom goes beyond.
The protection of the four classes of beings,
Gods, humans, spirits
And nāgas, is to be known by the wise
As belonging to those who range through all that can be known.
Their respective natures and times,
Contained within the Fortunate Eon:
The tathāgatas do not pass away,
And again, not passing away, they are not born.
Having come to know their own nature,
Beings become their own nature.
The seventy-two views
Arise through the action of the All-Moving Goddess:
The five views of the śrāvakas,
The two views of the pratyekabuddhas,
The seven of the Yogācāras,
The eight of the supreme Mādhyamikas,
The sixteen of the Perfections,
And the thirty-six of the Secret Mantra Vehicle,
Less two, those of the seventh and the eighth:
All of them, too, are the Mantra Vehicle.
The conduct of the channels, which has a root,
Is to be known through the kindness of the Guru.
The Heruka goddess serves as the root,
Abiding in the middle between existence and nirvāṇa.
Through the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the twentieth chapter, on the rite of the protective armor of Heruka and the others, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the protection of Vajrasūrya
Is to be explained.
With this mantra, hūṃ hūṃ ho,
In the center of the eye faculty:
Through the application of the two syllables hūṃ
All the views come to be seen.
Through wisdom, consciousness and tears
Dissolving below and above,
The seventy-two views
Come to be seen at a single time.
In the aperture of the throat, five;
In the one who has the secret vajra, two;
Seven are declared to be at the heart;
Eight in the expanse of the brain;
Sixteen come to be at the crown protuberance;
Thirty-six in the maṇḍala of the navel.
All are generated by one's own karma;
Here one comes to see one's own view.
This is my seat;
It is seen as the three worlds.
Through the syllable ho, of all the sattva goddesses,
The infinite buddha fields
Become pervaded by Vajrasūrya;
Therefore it protects the eye.
Embraced by the All-Terrifying Goddess,
Who proclaims the sound of the secret mantra hūṃ hūṃ,
The terror, through the two letters,
Brings all evil deeds to an end.
In the center of the maṇḍala whose nature is thirty-seven
The two are to be meditated upon.
Formerly, through the form of the secret mantra,
All the views come to arise.
Through the view of each one,
Protection is given to it.
Space is filled with yoginīs,
And again there is innate perfect awakening.
The yoginī is the innate form;
The innate letter is the yoginī;
The yoginī devours the innate,
And again the yoginī comes to be devoured.
The form of the buddha is the yoginī,
She who has the form of the vajra holder;
The form of the sacred place is the yoginī;
The deed and the doer are that very one.
It grants the sight of what is to be accomplished
To one who meditates on the letters of the armor.

Whatever the practitioner desires,
That they will grant.
Those sentient beings who do not meditate on this method
Will become deluded.
Merely by arranging the letters,
One will perform all the supreme rites.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This was the twenty-first chapter, on the rite of armor protection of Vajrasūrya and the others, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next, the protection of Paramāśva,
Which accomplishes every task and is auspicious.
Through the yoga of the mantra phaṭ haṃ,
All around, at the limits of head and feet,
And in the center of the ūrṇā, they should be placed.
At the center of the crown is the yoginī;
On the head, knowing her as the All-Terrifying One,
Called "all words" and "space,"
The secret mantra of Caṇḍī is phaṭ phaṭ,
Held to be the sovereign lady of the wisdom seat.
Even in the absorptions of Paramāśva,
What need is there to speak of the accomplishments of yoga?
The yogin tears apart saṃsāra,
And again nirvāṇa too is rent asunder.
Even the concept called "wisdom"
The mantrin rends through nonduality.
If the omniscient syllable haṃ is not present,
What need is there to speak of all people?
There is the one who pays honor,
And there is the one who subdues the senses;
Endowed with the qualities of Caṇḍī's yoga,
That is proclaimed to be nectar.
It is blissful, without doubt,
Because all the senses have been devoured.
In the center of the maṇḍala taught before
Are the Hero and the yoginī.
All the mantras and the rest
Are to be done by all, everywhere.
One devours by oneself the habitual tendencies of karma,
Until even the subtle ones are wholly destroyed.
When all evil has been set ablaze,
It passes away, just like cotton.
She is to be known as the Fierce-Making One,
Arisen from the family of Vārāhī.
Through the stars reckoned by the moon,
The lord of the stars melts with compassion.
Again, to explain it in brief:
The six yoginīs
And the heroes are inseparable, nondual.
I shall explain the characteristics of the purpose.
The twelve yogins and yoginīs
Are to be known, each in its own place;
There one should make them dance.
The characteristic of innate perfect awakening,
The bliss of all embodied beings,
Is to be known from the time of the two empowerments.
By making the six openings into the vase,
One attains a bliss like that.
Three openings below the navel,
Likewise three above it,
Going and coming, two by two:
So too the twelve armors there.
Through the characteristics of the senses and their objects,
The very nature of things dances there.
The yogin comes to drink the nectar itself,
Eating the elements of material things;
By gazing at the sign above and below,
He sports as the innate self.
Through the practice of the six recollections,
This is what the practitioner is to do.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This was the twenty-second chapter, on the rite of armor protection of Paramāśva and the others, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next I shall explain the reality of the torma,
In due order, just as it is.
Within the center of the source of phenomena,
One should give the torma according to the rite.
The lord of yogins, in the form of a ḍāka,
Again protects with the armor and the rest.
Then too one should focus on the absorption.
The lotus vessel should be set down,
And made into the nature of outer and inner.
The place of birth is sought in the avadhūtīr;
The openings below and above the navel
Are to be imagined as the lotus vessel.
The nectars of the five spaces
Should all be made of one taste.
The aggregates are ge ku da ha na;
The sense sources are all the meats.
They are kindled by the mind of bliss
And heated by the fire of desire.
The supreme purification is in oneself;
Again they are made into nectar.
There, continually with the mudrā of meditation,
One should bless them as the three bodies.
Through the application of mantra and mudrā,
One should invite the ḍākinīs.
Knot the fingertips firmly together,
Join the tips of the middle fingers, the thumbs as a vajra;
Having pressed them firmly together,
Place them at the forehead,
And while reciting repeatedly, circle them.
Pressing down with the feet and looking upward:
When the mantrin does this, it is supreme.
With the sound of the syllable phe at the crown,
One should summon the heroes and yoginīs
Dwelling in the world systems of the ten directions.
Through the joining of left and right,
One should display the mudrā at the heart.
Circling with the two hands, left and right,
Is the bliss of the ḍākinīs.
oṃ āralli ho jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ bajraḍākaḍākinyā samayastvaṃ dṛśya heḥ
Open the vajra palms upward;
At midnight one should give the torma.
oṃ kha kha khāhi khāhi sarba yakṣa rākṣasa bhūta preta piśāconmādāpasmara ḍākaḍākinyādaya imaṃ bagrīhṇantu sarba siddhimme prayacchantu yathaibaṃ yatheṣṭaṃ bhuñjatha jighratha pībatha mātikramatha mama sarbakartāya saṅsukhaṃ biśuddhaye mama sāhāyika bhavantu hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svāhā
As long as the mantra is engaged,
Through holding the vase breath one remains unmoving.
One attains it without interruption;
What need even to wish for the bliss born of joy?
The torma feast of the heroes,
And likewise for the yoginīs:
Through the arrangement of the great maṇḍala,
One should make the Guru the principal.
Whatever the names of the yoginīs,
And likewise those of the heroes,
To each of them meat is offered,
And the intoxicant that grants great bliss.
The circle should be formally convened,
Or else in pairs with the mantrin.
The seat is a tiger skin;
The feast is held upon a human skin.
There is drinking from the great vessels,
By the mantrin with loosened hair.
The vessel is to be tended by a yoginī,
Or else by a young maiden.
Likewise the circle of the yoginīs
Is formed by the heroes;
At the places of the heroes,
The vessel is tended by a young maiden.
Silk streamers and crowns,
Likewise scents of many kinds,
Singing, dancing, cymbals,
The six-stringed instrument and the rest, and the lowly ones,
And all the torma vessels:
The mantrin should set them out there, beginning from the east,
Together with all the liquor
And the vessels of offering.
The object of worship, the worship, the cause of worship,
And the worshiper are mutual.
The circle is made of one's own disciples,
Or else of others in meditative equipoise.

The mantrin should exclude all those without good fortune,
And the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) and the like.
All beings are by nature supreme joy;
Dwell in the nature of the circle of the yoginīs!
are re re re, deluded cattle do not know this.
Having taken the innate beautiful one, enjoy great bliss!
śambu. All beings of the three worlds without exception are of the nature of buddha;
Sport together with the maiden of compassion!
are. Whoever has not meditated on the ultimate
Will not attain buddhahood.
are. Know inner and outer to be without division!
Then one is able to liberate the whole world.
are. All these and the rest
Are to be sung here and there as fits.
In accordance with my tantra and realization,
With the mantrin's permission, songs should be sung.
Having paid homage to him,
One should also dance and sing.
The vessel is offered into the lotus hand
By the one who keeps the observance himself.
It is to be held in the lotus hand
And given by that very one.
The first portions of liquor for the yoginīs
Should be collected separately.
The leftovers of what has been eaten
Should be given to all the bhūtas.
According to one's true means,
A fee is offered to them.
This should be done each day,
Or else month by month.
In order to keep the commitment,
It should be done on one day each year.
There the verses of giving should be recited,
And words of auspiciousness spoken as well:

You have worshiped the assembled ḍākinīs,
And served food and drink to the heroes;
May you always have protection and supreme prosperity,
Or else the full achievement of your heart's desires.
In all your births, may you go
Into the midst of the accomplished world of the supreme Sugatas.
By worshiping the nondual heroes, in this very life
May your evil be exhausted and the accomplishment of mudrā arise.

From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This was the twenty-third chapter, on the rite of the torma and the worship of the circle, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next I shall fully explain something further:
Laying the lines of the maṇḍala,
The hearth for the fire offering, and likewise
The worship of the master himself.
On ground that is level,
The maṇḍala is eight cubits,
On a mountaintop or the like,
Or on the surface of pure ground.
There one should dig down to knee depth;
Then, having dug about a span further,
It should be filled with water,
And as long as it has not drained away,
For that long the wise one
Should recite the root mantra here.
It becomes the house of the yogin,
And all births will be burned away.
There the maṇḍala should be drawn,
And the two lines laid down.
On both sides of the brahmā line,
Thirty-two are laid on each.
Then, by laying its center line,
The lines come to sixty-four.
In their middle, equal to them,
They are laid out once again.
In short, for the four circles
There are two hundred fifty-six.
From the sixteen sections on all sides,
The drawing is made with colored pigments.
With what remains, by the measure of the door,
One makes the central lotus and the rest.
The central lotus, however,
Is to be made twelve finger-widths.
For three of the four circles,
Three sections each are made.
The wise should know that each section
Measures two cubits.
The cord of five colors
Is therefore twice the maṇḍala.
In its thickness, measured from the door,
It is twenty less one.
With sweet fragrances and incense,
One should perfume the wisdom cord.
Having given the great torma,
Afterward perform the work of the lines here, with recitation.
Vajra pegs are set in the ground
By the wise one with enthusiasm.
One's own disciple is made to hold the line;
The mantrin holds it
With the left fist at the navel,
And then it is snapped above.
In the same way, the laying of the lines
Should be done counterclockwise.
On entering and on going out,
One gives the torma and steps across.
While reciting the root mantra,
The drawing should be raised into the sky.
The mantrin who knows outer and inner
Should drink the nectar.
The earth is made to depart into the sky,
And then summoned back to that very place.
With flowers of every kind,
One dances with vajra steps.
One brandishes the vajra in the hand
And sports with the mother and the other yoginīs.
All of these, the curved knives and the emblems,
Are to be made by those of the maṇḍala.
Having made offerings of every kind,
The master, bathed in scented water,
Wearing garments stainless,
Washed and well perfumed,
Should loudly sound the bell,
While the yoginīs sing songs,
With cymbals and vessels of many kinds,
And a clamor of sound resounds.
Empowerment is to be bestowed
On one's own disciples, outer and secret,
In a place free of people,
Avoiding kings, thieves and the like.
Here, through the many faults of the rite,
One will fall into Avīci and the other hells.
Now the hearths of the fire offerings
For pacifying, enriching and the rest:
A round hearth is for pacifying;
A square one is for enriching;
A triangular one is for subjugating;
A six-cornered one is for fierce rites;
A seven-cornered one is for expelling;
An eight-cornered one is for causing enmity;
A nine-cornered one is for paralyzing;
A five-cornered one is for killing;
And a ten-cornered one is for all rites.
The color is according to its own lord;
The emblem that belongs to its own lord
Is placed at the center of the hearths:
Wheel, lotus, vajra and curved knife,
Sword, jewel, crossed vajra,
Arrow and bow; and likewise on the hearths
Garlands of these are made.
One cubit and two cubits,
Half a cubit and half of that,
Then again two cubits, three and four,
Five, six, seven, eight and nine,
And ten cubits are rightly taught
For all the hearths, in their proper order.
With the kindling sticks and the substances,
The burnt offerings and all the flowers,
And with the applications of outer, inner and self,
Each performs its own rite.
The rites explained in the Abhidhāna,
With the root mantra and all the rest,
And the rites of their procedures
Are made known throughout all the tantras.

I shall explain just a little;
I shall explain what is taught in all the tantras.
The round hearth arises at the navel;
At the heart is the square one;
At the opening of the jewel, the six-cornered;
At the throat, the seven-cornered;
The eight-cornered is at the head;
The nine-cornered is between the brows;
At the uṣṇīṣa is the five-cornered;
And the ten-cornered is at the forehead.
Having roots that arise from the wind,
All the elements, as the substances to be burned,
Are to be burned in the flowing of the wind.
At that very time the outer rite
Is, for one of intelligence, a lesser rite.
lalanā and rasanā
Are likewise the two ladles, the filling ladle and the pouring ladle;
The vessel of butter is the abadhūtī,
And the hearths lie at intervals along it.
The fire offering for all rites
Is performed in this world;
There all the substances
Should be burned without hesitation.
The filling ladle is one cubit long,
And four finger-widths across;
In it one hollows out two finger-widths.
The groove of the spout is half a finger-width.
In the middle are a vajra and a lotus,
And the groove of the spout bears a vajra.
At the base is a vajra of six finger-widths,
On top of the handle, for the filling ladle and the rest.
The pouring ladle too is of that measure,
And on top it is like a lotus petal.
As for the measure of the hearths,
They are one cubit or two cubits,
Dug downward to half of that;
This is taught for all ten.
At the beginning, in the middle and at the end,
The mantrin offers the full oblation.
The fire is to be summoned,
And it is placed on a bundle of kuśa grass.
Arghya water, water for the feet and water for the mouth,
Having been consecrated in their various ways, are offered.
There three bowls of mother-of-pearl
Are set upon leaves.
A vessel of milk is also set down,
And then a cloth is laid upon it.
On the ground dūrba grass is spread,
First with its tips turned to the front.
In every case on the right side,
There the substances are to be placed.
Three lines are drawn on the ground,
With a single door in the west.
All the things that are to be accomplished,
And people beginning with the king, are to be brought in.
In order to make all of them close,
Offerings are made to the vajra guru.
One should visualize the four-armed sage
With staff, hearth, water pot
And rosary, riding on a ram,
And red in color.
oṃ ehyehi agnaye raṃ sarba siddhimme prayaccha jvala jvala ho mahārīṣi sarba deba mukha pūri asmin sannihito bhava hūṃ
This is the mantra for inviting the fire god.
oṃ āḥ pādyaṃ hutāsanāya grihṇa hūṃ
This is the mantra of the water for the feet.
oṃ baṃ ācamanaṃ ṛṣaye prayaccha hūṃ
This is the mantra of the water for the mouth.
hūṃ ḍākaḍākini samayārghaṃ dadāmi hūṃ hūṃ raṃ raṃ svāhā
This is the mantra of the arghya water.
oṃ debaṛṣi parama saugata kāruṇikā jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ ha ha daha daha paca paca jvala jvala hana hana hūṃ hūṃ
This is the mantra for pleasing the fire.
For all the substances, one should offer them with the syllable oṃ and āḥ, with the name properly joined in the middle, and at the end the syllables hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ and svāhā. This is the mantra of all the substances.
One should meditate on the maṇḍala, together with its principal,
At the heart of the sage.
The commitment fire is completed from raṃ;
The wisdom fire god is equal to it.
Or else one meditates on it blazing,
In the colors for pacifying and the rest.
One imagines the fire dissolving into it.
The mantrin should know the distinctions of light
As the fire of each appearance.
Through the yoga of the nondual aspect,
Of the yogin who is one with the fire god,
The wise one should perform it.
Just as the inner wisdom of oneself is,
So too should the outer be made,
For the sake of the continuum of the accumulation of merit
And to complete the accumulation of wisdom.
There caru should be prepared,
And offered to the fire as food;
One partakes of a little oneself,
And gives some to the patrons as well.
Beautiful maidens dance,
And cymbals of many kinds are played.
With great persons adorned
With ornaments of many kinds, as if at play,
Joyfully with flowers, incense and the like,
It is done in the midst of the gathering.
tambula is given to everyone,
Things that delight them are given.
The remainder, clothing and tooth powder,
And the full oblation are to be given.
A measure of coin, seating in rows, tooth powder,
And water for the mouth come at the end.
The wisdom fire is requested to depart,
And food is offered with recitation of the mantra.
oṃ agnaye sarba santoṣaṇaṃ kuru me yathā kāle siddhi dā gacchatu hūṃ hūṃ svāhā
This is the mantra for requesting the fire to depart.
Whatever little was lacking, in liquor or the rest,
Please forgive all of it.
The grain left over from everything
Should all be burned in the hearth.
Thus the lines of the maṇḍala have been taught in brief,
And the hearth and the supreme rite of the fire offering.
The master who is to be worshiped,
Having been truly regarded as Vajradhara,
Is offered pearls, gold and jewels,
Elephants, horses, land and provinces,
Male and female servants and retinues,
A wife perfect in every way,
Many pairs of garments,
And whatever little pleases him, or land;
Free of the view of a self,
One should offer them and gratify the lord.
With oneself and all one's possessions,
Having prostrated, one should offer.
Flowers, incense and all the rest
Should be offered before the Teacher.
The maṇḍal made in front
Is then of this ninefold nature:
In the center is the vajra guru;
In front is the All-Beautiful Lady;
In the north, the Lady of Supreme Joy;
In the west, the Lady Most Playful;
In the south, the Vajra Lady of Delight;
And Compassion and the others are in the four corners.
oṃ bajrasatva gurupādebhyaḥ sarba satvopakāraṇāya svāhā
For the deities of the maṇḍala, their own names
Are spoken first, with svāhā at the end.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This was the twenty-fourth chapter, called the rite of the maṇḍala, the fire offering and the worship of the master, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next I shall explain the consecration
That delivers from the terrors of saṃsāra.
In this king of yoginī tantras,
All the requisites
Should always be kept by the master,
And likewise the other things fit to be done,
In order to guard against the censure of the world.
Again, the consecration arisen from intrinsic nature
Is to be understood as illusion.
A cast image or a painted scroll:
One projects one's own form outward.
Whatever deity arises at whatever time
As the deity specially abiding there,
Its form is the form of that time.

It is the outflowing of the nature of bliss:
The first letter of whatever seed
Arises in the nature of the moving breath,
From the union of wind and consciousness.
Then, at that time, whatever form it takes
Accomplishes every perfection.
Without nondual wisdom inseparable from it,
A cast image is not consecrated.
From nondual wisdom
The consecration of those in saṃsāra is made.
For those who long for the bliss of liberation,
Here is the great bliss of bliss.
Abiding in the joy of the yoginī,
One should make it arise from that.
On a cast image or a painted image,
The one of true knowledge anoints the essences.
With the substances heated
Together with the five nectars, one bathes it.
The intelligent one sets down the vase.
The deity together with the maṇḍala's retinue,
The sacred Dharma and all the rest,
Are within the source of phenomena;
In whatever places they reside,
They are consecrated at all times.
Having accomplished the place of the syllable e,
The syllable baṃ is Vajrasattva;
There, where the syllable ma abides,
The fourth, without beginning, is known as yā.
"Heard" and "one" are the meaning of the mantra,
Which has the mantra of symbolic letters.
"At a time" is the Blessed One himself,
Dwelling, yet not abiding anywhere.
The words "all the tathāgatas"
Are equal to the expanse of phenomena.
By the words "body, speech and mind,"
He is the lord of the yoginīs and of no others.
"In the bhagas" means the five classes of tantra,
From Kriyā and the rest up to the Unsurpassed at the end.
"Was dwelling": how so?
Consecrated, or not consecrated.
Having delivered sentient beings,
Consecration is found in all the tantras.
When, through special devotion, nonduality
Is consecrated in the yoginī tantra,
Having made the two, the act and its object,
It is nonetheless self-aware:
The great wisdom of self-awareness,
The deity whose nature is self-awareness.
Consecration from the nature of self-awareness
Is highly praised.
Out of loving concern for people,
The rest too should be explained in the same way.
This is the purity of the deities
Of the maṇḍala of true wisdom.
The seventy-two arms
Are declared to be the purity of the tantras.
The four arms are held to be the purity
Of the four, laughing and the rest.
As for the purity of the faces,
Sixteen are innate perfect awakening;
The seventeenth face is explained as mentation.
Existence and nirvāṇa are the two feet.
The purity of all the ornaments
Is the Dharma and those born of the Dharma.
The rest are the purity of the world systems,
Both clear and unclear.
The purity of the root mantra and the rest
Reveals the meaning of all the vehicles.
The vajra, the lotus and all the rest
Here proclaim the womb-born.
All the purities of the maṇḍala
Teach the Dharma in summary.
The purity of the twelve links
Is the twelve wheel-turning kings.
The spaces between the sixteen parts
Should be known as the purity of the charnel grounds.
Whatever tantra has arisen from me
Should all be known as purity.
All who possess omniscient wisdom
Are declared to be its purity.
Substance, perception itself and wisdom:
The purity of naming
Those who speak of the individual wisdoms
Is the tantra of yogins and yoginīs.
All beings are like illusions;
The circle of the maṇḍala is in the manner of illusion;
What is taught is self-aware wisdom,
Declared in each and every case.
The characteristics of the tenet of illusion
Should all be learned from the guru's mouth.
Progressing by means of the oral instructions,
At each juncture there is great bliss.
Whatever purity there is at that time,
These come from the domain of the buddhas.
Therefore the root secret mantra too
Arises for one who desires bliss.
By being assigned to each letter,
They become the forms of things.
The secret mantra of Vārāhī has been explained:
All things are its purity.
The senses and their objects being untrue,
For those who apply the reality of one taste,
It becomes the purity of the dharmakāya.
Speech is the sambhogakāya;
Mind is the nirmāṇakāya;
And body is the innate kāya.
Again there, the four, the Stainless and the rest,
Are held to be pure.
The great purity of the armor mantra
Is the conduct of all yoga.
As the heart mantra and the near-heart mantra,
Yogin and yoginī are in union.
The elements and what has arisen from the elements, gathered together,
Become the purity of the torma mantra.
The purity of the mantra of Sumbha
Is driving the dagger into all concepts.
The purity of the various rites:
Its purity is ultimate.
The deities of all the elements
Become the purity of skin, flesh and the rest.
What need is there for what is not stated here?
All bliss is utterly pure.
Whatever appears in the pathways of the senses,
Each by its own nature,
Through the supremely beneficial yoga,
Experience all as the nature of buddha.
This is to be known as the supreme purity;
The purity of others is not spoken of.
In the views of Īśvara and the rest,
It should be known, in brief, as purity.
It is accomplished in the Buddha's yoginī tantra,
And nowhere else.
Whoever dwells on the path of awakening
While holding to the reality of grasped and grasper,
Through the manifest fault of his ignorance
Sees things as Īśvara and the rest.
Therefore this purity,
Whose nature is self-awareness, has been explained.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, the supreme of bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This was the twenty-fifth chapter, called the purity of "the Blessed One" and the rest, and likewise the characteristics of the rite of consecration, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next I shall explain the characteristics
Of those who make the innate mudrā.
As for the channels, Abhedyā and the rest,
They are taught with four more besides:
Abhedyā and Sūkṣmarūpā,
the Excellent One, Vāmā, Vāminī,
Kūrmajā, Bhāvakī,
the Empowering One, Doṣā, Viṣṭā,
Sāmānyā and Hetudāyikā,
the Real One, and likewise sumana,
lalanā and rasanā,
abadhūtī, the One of Joyful Speech,
Prabandromā, Siddhā,
the Old Woman, the One Agreeable to All People,
the Trembling One and the One of the Essence,
the Goose, Trivṛttā, Kāminī, the Nāga,
Caṇḍī and the Daughter of Māra,
the Moon, the Sun, Agni and rāhu.
Of the thirty-six channels,
Each one has twelve;
When all are divided by their distinctions,
The mudrās are again taught in brief.
One finger should be shown,
Then two of its fingers are shown,
Standing evenly below and above;
And then the thumb should be shown.
For all these channels,
Below and above are equally extended.

Through the omniscient one, the supreme way to proceed
Is to be known in its various stages.
One should show her little finger;
That brings about true abiding with her.
When someone shows the nameless finger,
One gives in answer the pointing finger:
Vajrasūrya, Heruka,
Extended into the lotus of the heart.
When someone shows the threatening forefinger,
One should show the middle finger.
Through the union of vajra and lotus,
Bliss is to be cast upward.
When someone points to her forehead,
One should show her a hanging silk streamer.
In that bliss of self-awareness
One becomes bound together with the mudrā.
When someone shows a wheel,
One should show her the ground.
Going to the crown and to the navel,
It is held stiff with both hands.
When someone points to her eye,
One should show her a mirror.
Through divine substances and the senses as cause,
Luminosity will be seen.
When someone strikes her belly,
One should point to her navel.
In the nature of the expanse of phenomena,
The eight consciousnesses appear.
When someone points to the sky,
One should show her the sun.
Emptiness and compassion are truly seen
Through the very yoga of the channels.
When someone shows a wrathful frown,
One should point to the edge of the space between the brows.
Sporting in true union with oneself,
One knows one's own experience.
When someone shows a river,
One should show her the ocean.
Through applying oneself to the one ground,
All are highly praised as that ground.
When someone points to her knee,
One should show her the foot.
At the time of going to the happy destinies,
That reality of the senses goes.
When someone points to her breast,
One should show her a seat.
Bliss arises as the very nature of enjoyment,
Until existence has dripped away entirely.
When someone points to her shoulder,
One should show her the hand.
Appearing like a blazing staff,
It burns the five nectars.
When someone points to her thigh,
One should point to her secret place.
The life breath, shaped like the staff of a bow,
Dissolves into the central channels.
When someone shows her tongue,
One should point to her palate.
All the fierce fire, gathered
Into the channel, remains there.
When someone points to her lip,
One should point to her throat.
All is the inexpressible nature of reality,
Free from the mindfulness of all the senses.
When someone shows her fingertip,
One should show her a fingernail.
The form of bliss at the peak of existence
Will surely be seen to be cut off.
When someone points to her hair,
One should show her a garland.
Moving sentient beings
Are seen as having the form of wisdom.
When someone points to the crown of her head,
One should show her the big toe.
Like a thread through a jewel, the life breath
Will be seen entering the central channel.
When someone points to her throat,
One should show her birth.
Just as nectar is in a water pot,
All beings are seen within the lotus.
When someone shows the sun,
One should show her the sky.
All the worlds arising from the heart
Come to be known as from the supreme undefiled.
When someone shows the earth,
One should show the sole of the foot.
Through water like a lotus petal,
The sprout of nectar drips down.
When someone points to her jaw,
One should point to her cheek.
Through the power of the channels of all beings,
It is in the abodes of space.
When someone shows the liṅga,
One should show the testicles.
The unchanging goes into change:
That is the mudrā born at the junction of the testicles.
When someone shows a water pot,
One should show her a mountain.
Like a river flowing down from a mountain,
A stream of nectar will drip down.
When a woman touches her ear,
One should point to the line of the back,
By which innate perfect awakening
Is attained within the domain of hearing.
When someone points to her clothing,
One should show her a dye.
As the nature of all phenomena,
Selflessness is seen in an instant.
When a woman holds her lip with her teeth,
One should show the teeth grinding.
All food is nonexistent,
Yet it increases by the power of habitual tendencies.
When someone moves her tongue,
One should show the hand that eats.
Satisfaction with every bliss
Is attained from what is meaningful.
When someone shows the wrist of her hand,
One should show her the hollow of the palm.
The life winds are struck
With the vase breath, the yogic exercises and the rest.
When someone points to her ribs,
One should point to her armpit.
Inwardly one knows the innate
And compassion too.
When someone touches the center of her hand,
One should show the back of the hand.
Self and other and one's own time:
O supreme lady, they are not known!
When someone shows laughter,
One should show her smoke.
From entering supreme nonduality,
Both desire and freedom from desire fade away.
When someone shows a dance,
One should show her a song.
All the three realms are seen as illusion,
Seen from the reality of the two.
When someone shows a skeleton,
One should show her its tip.
Through the blazing of the three channels,
One comes to ransom oneself from death.
Thus there are thirty-six signs,
And likewise the supreme answers to the signs.
Conduct as brothers and sisters
Should be known within the vajra circle.
Pracaṇḍā, as mistress of the family,
Should be known as the chief lady of the place.
The form at the limit of utter clarity
Arises in the ocean of existence.
As long as there is the nondual domain,
The moving phenomena too should be known.
The hosts of ḍākinīs without exception
Gather in the ocean of the innate.
The letters of Pracaṇḍā should be known:
All women are the forms of illusion.
The aspiration of all the mudrās
Is in the nature of the bases of miraculous power;
All that has likewise been taught
Is held to be the bases of miraculous power.
All the signs of the mudrās without exception
Are the characteristics of generating the yoginī.
As taught before in the brief Cakrasaṃvara,
In the center of the circle of the maṇḍala,
In the center, whose nature is thirty-seven,
All these mantras are yoginīs.
The twelve, Ḍākinī and the rest,
Are joined with the times, Makara and the rest,
And the twenty-four, Pracaṇḍā and the rest,
Arise at those times.
Through instants, moments and the rest,
There is one transit of them all.
Through the times of beings,
Very subtle and coarse,
By the measure that makes up a year,
One should always reckon the yoginīs.
Having fixed the times in khara and the rest,
They are marked by the two solstices.
Multiplied by two and by ten,
So are the hours of the year.

A hundred increased by eighty-five
Should be known in five hours.
The measure of a single day, too,
Is sky, eye, and hand, eye.
The measure of a single month, too,
Is sky, sky, taste, and the time taste.
The measure of a single year, too,
Is sky, void, eye, and orifice likewise,
Orifice, and mountain.
So should all the ḍākinīs be known.
The secret mantra has the nature of union;
And of the root mantras, too,
When the feet are equal, two by two,
That is a single foot of the yoginī.
Whatever is in excess of them,
hūṃ phaṭ and the rest, should be left out.
oṃ tri na bhu maḥ b śrī ṇa ba kaṃ jra pa ḍā kaṃ ka pā ma ba hā ṇā bī ya ra hūṃ bī hūṃ rī phaṭ śva phaṭ rā svā ya hā hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā oṃ pra caṇḍi hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
This is the mantra.
Black in color, with four arms,
Her hair let loose, she is terrifying;
The goddess has the nature of wisdom and means;
The rest is as characterized before.
All these goddesses
Are, again, always equal to the ocean.
When the supremely excellent bliss is attained,
One rightly knows many purposes.
In the nature of the moon of gnosis,
This rises and sets.
The form of light of Brahmā's egg
Is the rays, ḍāka and ḍākinī.
What is bound, called the rays,
Is fully able to bring down gnosis.
The lines, too, in the centers of the sky,
The course to be traversed, are born from one's own mother.
Thus the qualities of Pracaṇḍā
Are to be marked in the supremely excellent and the rest.
Moving everywhere at all times,
It should be known as arising from mantra.
Through the cause of the supreme accomplishment,
The gathering is proclaimed to be the yoginī.
She grants accomplishment in dreams;
If she grants it, it should be known through the times.
Whatever the action is like,
Such is its result;
Because of the very connection of cause and result,
The yogin himself comes to be born.
Without a cause there is no result;
So too with the perceptible and the imperceptible.
For those who speak such truth,
It should be learned from the treatises on valid cognition.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This concludes the twenty-sixth chapter, on the domain, called the rite of the characteristics of abiding by the sign language of the mudrās of the yoginīs and heroes in the maṇḍala of the Mistress Pracaṇḍā, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next, the mudrā of Caṇḍākṣī,
Not found in other tantras, I shall explain.
It is to be known by the signs of the eyes;
All of this is union.
Closed and not closed,
Right and left and moving to the right,
Moving upward and to the tip of the nose,
Looking at the ground, the eye fixed on things,
Angry and dejected and agreeable, women,
Sons and kin and brothers,
Delusion, aversion, desire, jealousy,
Fault and pride and great conceit,
Longing and compassion and dullness,
Looking at illusion and looking far,
Going and meditative concentration, half and half,
All open and joy,
Fever and illness and the battlefront,
And with the Dharma: these are the supreme gazes.
If the yoginī gives
These very thirty-six gazes,
The answer to them in mudrā
Is always given by the yogins:
Mirror, water and offering,
Sword, lamp and thumb,
Sky, lotus, white water lily,
Arrow and moon and sun,
Jewel, pearl, coral,
Molten gold and net,
Blazing, shadow, cotton,
Guru, horse, ox, śarabha,
Dance and fright and trembling,
Sexual union, dispute, nearness,
Torma, drawing, maṇḍala,
Pacifying and enriching and subjugating,
Killing and expelling:
These, explained as gazes,
The thirty-six signs,
Are to be given in their proper order.
This is the yogin's connection;
I shall explain their purposes.
The life wind that has entered the course of the moon,
The supreme one that moves on the left,
The thirty-six descents of mind:
These should be known as their essence.
In whatever way the mudrā of the eye is formed,
In that way the life wind is to be made to move.
In all the joints, the head and the rest,
Having established the places of the channels,
Above and below, Abhedyā and the rest,
There one performs the great gaze.
Thus the seventy-two are to be known,
And every excellence will be attained.
Caṇḍākṣī, mistress of yoginīs,
Is she who cuts down the hostile.
In the factors of awakening, too,
All the yoginīs come to know.
Caṇḍākṣī the yoginī is always
Diligence and the two bases of miraculous power.
Toward all the conceptual thoughts of Caṇḍī,
The gaze of the eye is devouring.
The nature of the wavering mind
Is to be bound into one-pointed mind.
Destroying the deceitful antics of the frivolous in the world,
The ḍākinī of mudrā is born.
In the conventional and the ultimate,
Without interval, she makes them nondual.
The nondual eye here is suchness itself,
Highly praised by all the buddhas.
Through miraculous power it makes appearance,
And reveals all the channels.
In the center of the circle of the maṇḍala,
This method is to be meditated upon.
Caṇḍākṣī, endowed with means,
Has the nature of thirty-seven.
By declaring her mode,
All have the nature of awakening.
Born from the syllables of the mantra,
Moving everywhere, having the nature of the channels,
Her color and form are just like
Those of the yoginī Pracaṇḍā.
oṃ śa dāṃ ta ṣṭro sa tka ha ṭa sra bhī ne ṣa trā ṇa ya bhai hūṃ ra hūṃ bā pha ya ṭa hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha svā ṭa hā phaṭ svā hā
oṃ canḍā kṣī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
In the place of Pūrṇagiri, too,
Pracaṇḍā arises at all times.
At the full moon, on the forehead,
The moon rises on the great mountain.
In Jālandhara, Caṇḍākṣī
Was made into food by Rāhu.
On the first lunar day it is rightly taught;
As following the sun it is definitively taught.
Thus each yoginī
Has a vast name in her own field;
For each of them, too, there are twenty-four,
And again the pīṭhas and upapīṭhas.

There is no meditative concentration, nor even mantra recitation;
It is free of what arises from syllables.
The self whose essence is time and non-time:
Make the wish with the syllables hūṃ phaṭ.
From the form that arises from syllables
Comes forth the nectar of Hevajra.
She who is fitting for the conduct of the brothers,
She who makes entering and turning back,
Is to be known in the wheel of the heart;
The heart has the nature of the eye.
Abiding in the heart is one's own yoginī;
Caṇḍī blazes up from above the navel.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This concludes the twenty-seventh chapter, called the rite of the mudrā of the characteristics of Caṇḍākṣī and of the nature of the Mistress, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next is to be rightly explained,
According to the rite, the letters of the signs.
Knowing them, the yogin is swiftly and surely
Freed from the bonds of saṃsāra.
ḍā dā li ka, nā ma daṅ naṃ pi ṣi taṃ, rā ji kā, ba ṭabba ki pi ṭa ka, ḍī, kun tu ru, ka ra pū ra, silla kaṃ, sva yaṃ bhu, ka stu ri kā, ha hau sa ma, bolla, kakko baṃ, kā li ña ja raṃ, śā li śī raṃ a pya kaṃ, pi trī ba naṃ byañja naṃ, mā la tin dha naṃ, ratna sambha baṃ, go, ku, da, ha, na, bi, mū, śu, ra, ma.
Such are the signs of the yoginīs,
And likewise of the heroes, the brothers.
The intention of that yoginī
Comes to be expressed with the mouth:
Man; yoginī; woman; liquor; meat; tongue; pa ḍu ba; ḍamaru drum; of a woman; sexual union; semen; blood; born from flesh; blood; a woman's urine; feces; liṅga; lotus; gathered, or the fortunate one; the flesh of the twice-born; a corpse; a king's man; charnel ground; fish flesh; bird flesh; blood of the battlefield; cow; dog; elephant; horse; human; feces; urine; semen; blood; marrow.
Thus, too, the answers to the signs
Should always be known by the mantrin.
For those who know the reality of yoga,
Generation at all times
Is an emanation, a liberator of beings,
That will be born in field after field.
Bodhicitta is the melting element;
