Its number, too, is thirty-six:
Earth and water and fire and wind,
Space and the element of gnosis;
Their elements in turn become sixfold each,
The form and the rest of the Abhidharma, ending with consciousness.
It should be known in the sign of the nature
Of the one who speaks the definitive meaning.
Between each of them, the goddess
Is Prabhāvatī, whose form is gnosis.
At the end of the time of rising
Of the thirty-six elements, the khecarī,
Having accomplished those rays of gnosis,
Abides in the abode of all bliss.
Taking hold of the edge of the garment,
One should play at vital point after vital point.
Apart from the gnosis that has abandoned the conventional,
Even meditation is of the nature of conceptual thought.
With the nature of the yoga of the vajra underworld,
All the channels are to be demolished.
With the nature of the yoga of fierce burning,
The wise one has the syllables of Prabhāvatī.
This is the basis of miraculous power that is investigation,
And likewise in the place Oḍiyāna.
In the center of the circle of the maṇḍala
One should meditate on the supreme mistress of the deities.
With the nature of the yoga of the truth of secret mantra,
The various syllables reach the center.
The thirty-seven deities
Have the very nature of wisdom and means.
oṃ kaṃ bi kā da la yu mā ta lā ji bha ha ra bā ṇa ya u hūṃ grā hūṃ ya phaṭ hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha svā ṭa hā phaṭ svā hā
oṃ pra bhā va tī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Her color and form are as before.
By her own light the yoga is accomplished.
The syllable oṃ is the lord from whom the mother letters arise;
The life wind moves and moves.
The lords of the stars, the all-knowing ones,
Have abandoned even the senses and the rest.
The forms of the letter ya and the letter a,
Having the end of the foot and the head,
All rest upon the mere nā da;
This is the self-arisen expression of the Dharma.
Thus these four letters
Are also the dharmakāya and the rest.
The human is the gnosis of that portion of mind;
Because it is present, there is investigation.
In the middle of the world and upon the earth,
Through the bases of miraculous power one carries out one's conduct.
In the wheel of all qualities, too,
It comes to bestow as the cause of liberation.
The lord of all qualities is emptiness;
In saṃsāra there is no means other than this.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This concludes the twenty-eighth chapter, called the rite of the mudrā of the characteristics of Prabhāvatī, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next, of the supreme and principal yoginī,
The signs of the weapons are to be explained.
Through the intense yoga of Mahānāsā,
Which has the nature of life wind and exertion,
Here are bell, shield, fang,
Pestle, noose, skull cup,
Bow, khaṭvāṅga, book,
Small shield and threatening forefinger,
Garland of little bells and iron chain,
Rock and dust of the charnel ground,
Spear and drum and wet hide,
A rope of hair hanging down,
The stick for stirring the cremation fire,
Club, shield and head,
Skeleton and the ritual weapon,
Eye and kidney and nape of the neck,
The dagger of Saturn,
Seed, dance and saw,
Needle and flayed skin,
Clouds pouring rain, and the wooden hook.
If the yoginī gives these signs,
What is to be expressed as the answer in mudrā is:
Vajra, sword, long spear,
Trident, axe, curved knife, arrow,
Torch, shield and hammer,
Wheel, ḍamaru drum and dagger,
Staff and single-pointed weapon,
Conch, copper trumpet, triple staff,
And there the peacock's tail,
Crow feather and brush,
Fire hearth and mountain,
Short club, mirror, vīṇā,
The tips of the feet and the tips of the hands, lung,
Intestines, Rāhu, iron chain,
Earthen clay and husk and fishing net,
The headless one, blazing black butter,
And the one called "having the form of Bhairava":
These are the expressions in answer by mudrā.
The entire nature of wind,
Its family, is in the house of the yoginī.

Whatever the shape of whichever mudrā,
That is the body of the yoginī.
One either takes on her nature,
Or else she will cut down the hostile.
The eighteen emptinesses,
And above all compassion,
The unshared qualities of a buddha,
And likewise the eighteen elements:
This is the very ground of the yoginī,
And all people should know it.
In the intense yoga of Mahānāsā
Her own characteristics are taught.
She is the mother of all bhūtas,
Manifestly like a many-colored jewel.
The lord of the nā da set moving by the letter hā
Likewise hangs with its face turned downward.
The thread abiding in the nā da, too,
Bears a garland nondual with the form of bliss.
With the nature of the yoga of the instructions of the sādhana,
It arose before from the vowels and consonants.
Having the nature of Dharma, action, samaya,
And the Great Mudrā,
From the conception of the four letters
The four mudrās are themselves expressed.
The place is called Arbuda;
Likewise the basis of miraculous power that is mind.
The gathering of yoginīs of divine substance
Is renowned as the wheel of space.
Her mistress, whose nature is thirty-seven,
Is Mahānāsā.
The breath at the beginning and end of the nose
Has entering, abiding and rising.
In the center, what is to be marked is free of characteristics;
All the yoginīs come to play.
In the nu baṃ goes Sarasvatī,
She who descends into the center, whatever it may be.
By the yoga of drawing the bow
The life wind and the downward-clearing wind are held.
Having known consciousness as the sign,
That which is marked is also to be destroyed.
Wherever the mind is sent forth,
There one should gauge it by miraculous power.
This is the very womb of the lady of gnosis;
Examine it in every respect.
In the center of the circle of the maṇḍala,
Here one should meditate once again.
The nature of wisdom and means
Arises from the syllables of the secret mantra.
Her color and shape are as before:
Terrifying, black as night.
oṃ ja ko bā ṭi lā la bakṣa lī bhu ḍha jā la ya ga hūṃ nā hūṃ ya phaṭ hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ svā phaṭ hā phaṭ svā hā
oṃ hoḥ m hā nā se hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Perfected through this secret mantra,
She has the nature of all desires.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This concludes the twenty-ninth chapter, called the ascertainment of the rite of the signs of the characteristics of Mahānāsā, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Next, the secret signs of the body,
The conduct of beauty, I shall explain.
Through the yoga of Vīramatī
One will obtain a body born of divine substance.
Head and crown and forehead,
The left and the right ears,
The spine of the back and the two shoulders,
Nose and eyebrows, eyes and mouth,
Palate and heart and hands,
Armpits, breasts, flanks,
Navel, private parts and buttocks, liṅga,
Thighs and knees, calves, feet,
Toes, big toes and ankles,
And everywhere on the soles of the feet,
Testicles, hand, elephant hide,
Wrist and the side of the forehead,
And joints and belly are to be known.
When the yoginī always gives these,
The yogin, as the answer in mudrā,
Should give in their proper order:
Flute, vīṇā, staff drum,
Song and clay drum and music,
Garland, grace, dance, copper trumpet,
Incense and perfume and food offerings,
Fruit, unbroken rice and vessel,
Water for the feet, offering water, lamp, vase,
Cloth and mirror and parasol,
Canopy and yak-tail whisk likewise,
Pennant, flower garland,
Victory banner and perfumed chamber,
Short spear, garland, half garland,
And again small bells and net,
Lion throne and crest ornament,
And likewise various jewels.
Placed in the upapīṭha of Godāvarī,
Having the nature of the faculty of faith,
Through the intense yoga of Vīramatī
All the mudrās are fully explained.
In the language of the innate she is called bī,
Having the nature of thirty-six things:
Aggregates, elements and objects,
Faculties, actions and agents,
And here likewise the sounds of the objects,
Six each for all of them, in due order.
The gnosis that has gone within them
Is the innate, not a thing other than it.
Through the intense yoga of night and day
It has the nature of wisdom and means.
Those innate yoginīs
Are divided by thirty-six divisions.
The gathering of the great samaya
Is to be known in the center of the lotus.
One's own mudrā born from that
Is placed in object after object.
The bliss of the self-aware yoginī:
This is the domain of the buddhas.
That which is disintegrating is greatness itself;
The twelve humans enter within.
Where the vajra wind has fully entered,
There yoga is born everywhere.
Of the bliss that draws forth the rays,
Proceed by means of the non-emissions.
Thus, having known Godāvarī,
Possessing the vajra of the faculty of faith,
One knows the nature of Vīramatī;
She is the chief lady of the center of the maṇḍala.
The vajra whose nature is thirty-seven
Is endowed with the secret mantra of her own name.
She who feeds on various conceptual thoughts
Is so by the name of innate diligence.
oṃ ka pā la ra pāṃ śu ta pā dhū śo mru dya u ta gra śū ba lā pu ya ṣā hūṃ ya hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā phaṭ hā svā hā
oṃ bī ra ma tī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
The color and shape are as before;
This is to be done for all the yoginīs.
Because diligence rightly transfers,
The Abhidhāna rightly arises.
Endowed with the yoga of the garuḍa,
Here one acts without doubt.
The chomaka taught before
Should be known as the supreme tongue.
There is no other answer in mudrā:
The tongue of the answer belongs to the two powers.
In the various tantras and scriptures
I have not explained this to you.
The intense practice of this tantra
Has not occurred before and will not occur again.
Ignorant and hostile-minded,
Deluded people who indulge in objects
Will not attain buddhahood
And will surely be cooked in hell.
Therefore, with a reverent mind,
One should rightly understand the practice with reverence.
Former delusion will cease,
And buddhahood will be attained before long.
By devoting oneself continually with faith,
Life and health increase.
The yoginī of the wheel of wind,
Powerful Vīramatī,
Fully endowed with the divisions of the vajra wind,
Dissolves into the great mind.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This concludes the thirtieth chapter, called the names of the characteristics of the rite of Vīramatī, the mudrās and the answers to the mudrās, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

"Blessed One, please teach
The chomaka of all the elements."
"The signs of stainless things
I shall explain; practitioner, listen!"
Taking the lip fully between the teeth,
With bliss she sucks the bolla.
Having gone, revealing gnosis,
She spoke these words:
"Through the application of the mother letters,
Three tens together with six,
By mudrā and by the answer to the mudrā,
From this the samaya is to be distinguished."
ka kha ga gha ṅa kaṃ ca cha ja jha ña caṃ ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa ṭaṃ ta tha da dha na taṃ pa pha ba bha ma paṃ ya ra la va ha yaṃ
If the yoginī gives these,
The answer in mudrā is, in turn, this:
kā khā gā ghā ṅā kaḥ cā chā jā jhā ñā caḥ ṭā ṭhā ḍā ḍhā ṇā ṭaḥ tā thā dā dhā nā taḥ pā phā bā bhā mā paḥ yā rā lā vā hā yaḥ
Having thus known the distinctions of the mudrās,
This is the door panel of all embodied beings.
In the intense yoga of the two openings,
Everything is like the letters.
Having gathered below and above,
Yogin and yoginī are nondual.
Through the truths of the four moments,
Likewise those four joys,
The four classes and the four wheels,
And the bodies of e baṃ ma yā
Are taught in this tantra in brief;
The detailed explanation should be understood elsewhere.
Through the intense yoga of Kharvarī
The mantrin should know the rite.
A garland of door panels equal to space;
Conduct as a khecarī is one's own.
Speech, too, the self with the nature of language,
Is to be made to approach cessation.
The great secret, mind and mental factors:
Through clearly attaining the last,
The form of joy born of the jewel vajra
Will be seen at the boundary of the gaze.
All have the nature of the yoga of truth,
The four having the nature of four each.
In Rāmeśvara and in the faculty
It should be known as supremely wondrous.
Desire and freedom from desire are blended;
That does not arise from its moment.
Since it has the nature of the five elements,
Fire in the center is the abode of nectar.
The wheel of breath, always blissful:
The Tathāgata is to be eaten.
With the women of the gods and the nāgas
The wise one takes his pleasure.
Through them, at all times,
The transference of all embodied beings, too,
Into space, by way of the nine doors,
Is to be known as fourfold.
Thus the places of the door panels
Always come to be thirty-six.
"Not knowing the division of the doors,
How are the faults and the virtues?"
"Listen to the measure of the time of transference,
And to its right and full application.
By the good path, the beautiful abode;
By the bad path, the fault of existence.
The bindu, the navel and the upper ones,
The eyes and the nose and the ears,
The doors of feces and of urine:
These are the characteristics of the nine doors.
Through the navel, the heaven of desire;
Through the bindu, beings embodied in form;
Through the upper door, the going and coming
Of the higher abodes is renowned.
Existence as a yakṣa comes from the nose;
Through the ears, the gods of accomplishment.
If one does not remain, one proceeds to gnosis;
Humans become rulers of men.
Through the door of existence, the pretas;
Through the urine, animals likewise.
The embodied beings
Of the eight hells come from the feces.
The other doors are three each;
The wise one engages in the channels.
The twenty-seven, too,
Are the lunar mansions, Kṛttikā and the rest.
The three pure gnoses
Belong to each of the door goddesses.
Through the things of the faculty of diligence
Come the characteristics of all enjoyments.
In the center, the one whose nature is thirty-seven
Should be meditated upon with the color given before.
The seeds of the White Vajra Lady and the rest
Should be placed in the places of the door panels.
The goddess with the nature of wisdom and means
Is through the yoga of the wheel of earth.
Become the syllables of the mantra:
oṃ mā bi tri ka ga ṭa ṇa mu pa kha ri hā pū hā ri ra ta bā ma yantrā hūṃ ya hūṃ hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha phaṭ ṭa pha svā ṭa hā svā hā
oṃ kharba rī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Thus she truly arises from mantra,
Born from the yoga of the ultimate.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this."

This concludes the thirty-first chapter, called the gnosis of the nature of the characteristics of the letter signs of Kharvarī, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Again, having made offerings,
She asked again and again:
"The mudrā through the gnosis of things,
Blessed Lord, how is it?"
"I shall explain its characteristics;
Listen with a one-pointed mind.
Innate bliss, and emptiness,
Self-awareness, and great bliss,
Dharmakāya and complete enjoyment,
Emanation, and great wonder,
Luminous mind, and thought,
Peace and nirvāṇa, the union-born,
Perfection, and nonduality,
Liberation, and the ultimate,
Gnosis, consciousness, awakening,
The city of the dharmadhātu likewise,
The lotus and the dharmodaya,
And again selflessness, heaven,
Peace and sound and the guru,
Awareness, the unerring, freedom from concepts,
Elixir, and speech and explanation:
For the wise, these are the synonyms of the chomā.
Just as they are, the first letter
Of each is the chomā of the answer.
As the letter is, so is the meaning;
Aversion to hell should be known.
In the yoga of Laṅkeśvarī
You, yogin, should practice.
In the seed that has come to hang down,
There are all the chomās.
Of the element of gnosis, the anther,
The characteristic lies in its own nature.
One's own being is equal to that, O yogin;
The mountain abides in the heart of the lotus.
The mountain is consciousness in motion,
In all the elements of mind.
In Laṅkā the self is born,
Arising through the gnoses of things.
The lord of all bhūtas:
All things that are not gnosis are born.
That is in the place Devīkoṭa,
And again the faculty of mindfulness of awakening.
Where gnosis is without thought,
There it will be heard through the teaching.
Through freedom from desire, for whomever,
bi is the all-pervading gnosis.

In the spaces of the winds,
And likewise the bliss of the faculties,
ṭi is renowned as the power of the vajra;
It travels to the town of the sense objects.
Mindfulness is innate gnosis.
Where the mind is fully proclaimed,
There, knowing the awakening of the faculties as well,
One has the nature of the vajra.
Having thus ascertained reality,
One should abide in one's own nature.
Abandon phenomena together with the world;
One should draw inward a little, without the eyes.
Either it is the self of all things,
Or else one completely abandons everything.
There is neither thing nor possessor of things;
The absence of things comes to be clearly seen.
Completely freed from thing and non-thing,
Look on all as born from what is untrue.
From the mouth of the Guru, Vajradhara,
Meet with it through the succession of the lineage.
The goddess is in the maṇḍala of fire;
Therefore her body is of that nature.
In the center of the thirty-sevenfold self
One should meditate on her with the former form.
All becomes endowed with skillful means;
It arises from the syllables of the mantra.
oṃ hūṃ sa hūṃ ha kā ja ro su cchanda ra rī ṇa pa mulla khā ba ya ka hūṃ rā hūṃ ya phaṭ hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ svā phaṭ hā phaṭ svā hā
oṃ laṅke śva rī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
When it is known in this way, the great secret mantra
Brings about accomplishment with ease.
It is the very nature of all rites;
Not even once in a hundred is it otherwise.
The accomplishments that bear the names of mountains
Are as the twenty-eight remedies:
Kṛttikā and Rohiṇī,
Mṛgaśiras and Ārdrā likewise,
Punarvasu and Puṣya,
Aśleṣā and Maghā, and further,
Pūrvaphalgunī and Uttaraphalgunī,
Hasta, Citrā, Svāti,
Viśākhā and Anurādhā,
Jyeṣṭhā and Mūla and Pūrvāṣāḍhā,
Uttarāṣāḍhā and Abhijit,
Śravaṇā, and Dhaniṣṭhā likewise,
Śatabhiṣā and Pūrvabhādrapadā,
Uttarabhādrapadā and Revatī,
Aśvinī, and likewise Bharaṇī;
The Moon, the Sun, and Rāhu,
Mars and Saturn:
One should know the proper time of each,
Grasping it without doubt.
All have the nature of the lunar conjunction,
Abiding equally in the sky.
Through the application as it is being explained,
The mantra practitioner should know the rite.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This concludes the thirty-second chapter, the gnosis of the defining characteristics of the signs of the mudrā of Laṅkeśvarī, of the nature of the wheel of the maṇḍala, and of the rite of names, from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Furthermore, something else shall be explained:
The signs of purpose and of action,
By merely recognizing which
The practitioner will obtain accomplishment.
When the Moon and the Sun are seized by the planet,
The defining characteristics of the yoginīs' mantra speech:
po taṃ means speaking in secret;
pra ti po taṃ is the reply.
ga mu is going; lum pa
Means "he comes."
de hi is a city; ca ṭu kaṃ is called
"Hold!"; hrī da ya is a hero.
kau ra ba means "Go!";
karṇṇi ka is a bell; a li is doing.
śi ra is coiled hair;
sra ba is the ear; further,
man tha na is nectar;
na rā is meeting; and tā li ka
Is a ḍākinī; na ra kaṃ is a maṇḍala.
a mu kaṃ is a charnel ground;
kā khi la is a door; sva sa naṃ
Is a brahmin; ba ri is the royal caste;
bir ti is the merchant caste; kru ra
Is a commoner; an ta is an outcaste.
ā li ka is cattle; ḍā ki
Is a sister; mu da ka is fat.
Touching the teeth with the tongue, likewise,
Should be understood as hunger for food.
tre is the nose touched by a scent;
ā ga ma na means "From what place?"
And "such and such"; ki ra ṇa is a flower;
lam pa is the belly; dan ta is laughing;
ni ro dhe is rain; bi jña pa ti is being satisfied.
dhu mra is a cloud, likewise;
dhu mra pri ya is a mountain;
sā nu sā ri ta is a river;
aṅ gu lya is a portion;
ba da na rā ji ka is the tongue;
a dha ra is the teeth; paṃ ka ti
Is a banner, a necklace, and likewise a garland.
ca la is the wind; mrig pa ti is an animal;
maṇḍa la is level;
svā sa should be known as a crossroads.
Birth: phal gu is the lower garment; ma hā kṣa raṃ
Is a great beast; ccha is a goat;
na is a human; ba is an ox;
ma is a buffalo; bha is eating;
si is a lion; byā likewise is a tiger;
sṭhā is abiding. Touching the mouth means "I have eaten";
Touching the teeth means "I am satisfied";
hri ka is shame; touching the hollow
Is sexual union; touching the thigh
Means "Do it thus, now."
So one should recognize the supremely suited consort.
Here the purpose is explained once more;
The wise should churn.
The action should be known as the yoginī;
The hero is the purpose throughout.
Through the nature of various things,
The self whose nature is gnosis and the knowable;
Generation, the union of becoming,
And the free of union, and likewise the conventional;
Illusion, the concordant, and saṃsāra,
Love, and the imagined nature,
The dependent and the thoroughly established,
tu ri yaṃ, and meditation on the wheel,
Delight of mind, deeds, and moral discipline,
Giving, austerity, and heroism likewise,
The astrologer and the physician,
Craft, the elements, and the speaker;
And the rest, the twelve links of dependent origination,
Ignorance and so on, should be known.
In the vicinity of Mālava,
Through the application of Drumacchāyā,
In the aspects of the faculty of concentration,
Not entering and entering are the same.
The faculties, with desire and free from desire,
Are the perfectly accomplished lord.
Māmakī, in the aperture of the vajra,
Should be placed at the crown of the head,
Hanging down, facing downward:
This is the manner of the mudrā of Kamboji.
ba is the continuum of perfect knowledge;
Its nature is illusion, its own defining mark.
Thus all that is made and unmade
Does not appear to the wise in the Middle Way.
Here, in the gnosis of the peaceful state,
The yogin has a body of nectar;
With a form resembling a mirage,
He comes to see himself as embodied.
Fully clothed in gnosis,
One should recollect oneself as the all-pervading deity.
Just as compassion cannot be made into two,
One should recollect arising as a being of that kind.
In the center of the thirty-sevenfold self
One should meditate on the renowned one of former form.
The secret mantra, the very self of the dhāraṇī of truth,
Is planted in the center of the maṇḍala of water.
oṃ sam rba hā bhū śma ta śā saṃ na trā pri sa yā na ya ka hūṃ rā hūṃ ya pha hūṃ ṭa hūṃ pha pha ṭa ṭa svā pha hā ṭa svā hā
oṃ dru ma ccha ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Thus, as the very self of the mantra's form,
She should be known as the one who goes everywhere.
In worship whose nature is the vajra,
The innate beautiful women assemble.

The gnosis Ḍākinī, real in her very self,
Is the yoginī who is chief of space.
Having made the breath hidden,
One again goes everywhere through Oḍḍiyāna.
In the regions of the three levels
One should meditate on the queen of form.
By drawing the mind inward
One should meditate on the accomplishment one desires.
Again, the yogin of Vajravārāhī
Should worship a woman of good family.
Than gathering together divine substances
And human bliss, that is supreme.
In the delightful city of the mātṛkās
One should meditate on the lord of beings.
At such a time as this, the wise
Place gnosis within the avadhūtī.
The yogin meditates on the very self of mind,
Which has emptiness as its form and object.
Having understood everything by that,
It is the very defining mark of the Net of Illusion.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirty-third chapter, called "The Ascertainment of the Rite of the Signs of the Mudrā of Drumacchāyā's Own Defining Characteristics," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then it was asked of the Blessed Lord:
"Great Vajraḍāka, all-pervading lord,
Please teach, in the proper way,
The mudrā, the form of perfect knowledge."
"Through the application of the four mudrās,
Each of them is ninefold:
The action, the Dharma, and the samaya,
And the great mudrā is the fourth.
Whatever woman is white like a lotus root
Gives off the scent of the lotus.
She with the scent of lotus and utpala
Has the color of the campaka.
She with the scent of jāti and campaka
Bears that same form and color.
She with the scent of camphor and sandalwood
Has the skin of a white lotus.
She with a scent like musk
Has the color of a blue utpala.
Blue utpala with green leaves:
Her scent is indicated by that itself.
She with the scent of mallikā and utpala
Resembles a sapphire.
Know her with the scent of ketaka:
She is red like the bandhūka.
She with the scent of pāṭali and mallikā
Is, again, the color of the sky.
Thus it becomes the action mudrā.
I shall explain the Dharma mudrā.
She who delights in the assembly of the sugatas,
Born of the outcastes, is the supreme yoginī.
She who ever delights in meditation and recitation
Is declared to be a brahmin woman.
She who ever delights in bathing and cleanliness
Is a woman of the merchant caste.
She whose very self is free of concepts,
Unyielding, and who sports with heroes,
Has the nature of the royal caste;
She is one who supremely delights in talk.
She who fully sports in work
Is the yoginī of the commoner caste.
She who is devoted to the assembly of the sugatas
Is the yoginī who is the supremely suited consort.
She inclines toward the side of the wise;
In the place of the Dharma she is without anger.
Born into the family of heroes,
She is one who always eats meat.
She guards the path of the Sugata
And holds the intention of a bodhisattva.
Not angry toward those who are angry,
She carries the burden of giving pleasure.
Thus is the Dharma mudrā.
The samaya mudrā shall be described.
She who delights in torma and worship
Is declared to be one who keeps the samaya.
She delights in consecration, the maṇḍala, the wheel,
And the ultimate meaning.
She who confers empowerment on beings
Is renowned as the yoginī who keeps the samaya.
She fully turns the wheel of Dharma
And always guards the samaya.
In the midst of the assembly of yoginīs
She is supremely wondrous and playful.
At all times she is full of joy
And makes a beaming face.
To her, yogins appear as sons;
Know her as Vajrayoginī.
She who is never weary at any time
Bears the name 'She Who Casts Down Weapons.'
A human woman, while ever going about,
Guards the signs of the samaya.
Thus is the samaya mudrā.
The great mudrā shall be fully explained.
The innate and the field-born goddess,
The mantra-born, the protector of the world,
The union-born and likewise the place-born,
The samaya yoginī of divine substance:
The lotus woman, at all times,
Is highly praised as the great mudrā.
Thus is the nature of the mudrās;
The manner in which it is shown is the answering mudrā.
Whoever has whatever color and scent,
One should recollect her manner accordingly.
Through the application of the Earth Guardian,
Kāmarūpa is the power of wisdom.
For the sake of what all sentient beings need,
This mudrā should be accomplished.
The syllable e and the winds of gnosis
Are well seated on the maṇḍala of the Moon.
The self whose nature is the fire of desire
Is the yoginī of the wheel of mind.
The channel arisen from the vajra secret
Is never without the unshifting of all the elements.
The buddhas of the past and those yet to come
Are the portions of sentient beings.
And in each single mind
She has thirty-six actions.
The embodied form, likewise, is by her;
In the field are forms such as kalā.
As the very manner of the great mantra,
She will now sport upon the earth.
Even in the family of all the buddhas,
Form and the formless will not be known.
The goddess who has taken the form of another
Is the one who knows the path of the Buddha.
The faculties are the all-pervading Buddha,
Because they are the perfection of wisdom.
Thus, by ascertaining reality,
One becomes equal to the form of the yoginī.
The goddesses in the wheel of gnosis,
The mistress who grants all desires,
In the center of the maṇḍala wheel
Have arisen in the form of the mantra.
oṃ ja ma ṭā hā ka ka lā la pa pā muntā ku ga ṭā ni ya te hūṃ jā hūṃ ya phaṭ hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ svā phaṭ hā phaṭ svā hā
oṃ ai rā ba tī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
In the seed of the mantra so called
The granter of the supreme has arisen.
Her color and shape are as before,
Just so, in every way and in all things."
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirty-fourth chapter, called "Endowed with the Rite of the Defining Characteristics of the Body Mudrā of the Earth Guardian," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the goddess, having made offerings,
Spoke these words:
"The sign of all truth,
At the sight of which all bhūtas and the rest
Are terrified on every side..."
The Blessed Ḍāka himself spoke:
"Listen to the heart mudrā
That has entered within all mudrās,
Which is taught in no tantra at all.
You have urged it, and so,
Through the application of the vajra fire,
I proclaim all the mudrās.
The goddess who is in the wheel of the navel
Has as her domain the limit of the touch of wood.
The sequence of going and coming
Is through the sequence of the various channels.

The mudrā is the drop planted in the Sun;
Thought and mind are the vowels and consonants,
The thirty-six, long and short;
That mudrā is she who grants manifestly.
The mudrā and the answering mudrā
Have the nature of wisdom and skillful means.
The breath in which the awakening mind is planted:
All this dissolves into sentient beings.
Through the union of Mahābhairavī
The wise should accomplish it.
Having experienced that source,
From whichever and whatever it arises,
All those who laugh with "hā hā"
Should be placed at the root of a tree.
Those who gather there in joy
Are the elements and the sense sources as well.
Free from all habitual tendencies,
It is like holding Bhairava.
In the place of Oḍi, the power of faith
Has as its domain the corresponding number;
In the middle of the house of close measuring,
Therefore the stable and the moving,
Dissolving too, are the self of all things.
The wise moisten with the mudrās;
As though flying into the sky,
Gnosis is supreme above all beings.
Having known it through the force of faith,
One has the strength of all embodied beings.
As though eating the great flesh,
One drinks blood with the mind.
The consort who acts as the mother of Heruka
Properly enters at the foot of a tree.
In the center of the maṇḍala circle
One should meditate on Mahābhairavī.
In the center of the wheel of mind
The mantras arise from the syllables.
oṃ ma kha hā ṭa sa bāṃ mu ga dra ka me pā kha la lā śū ya la hūṃ pri hūṃ nna pha dhā ṭa ri phaṭ ṇe svā hūṃ hā hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
oṃ ma hā bhai ra bī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
From the great union of the secret mantra so called,
She who has the body and mind of the yoginī,
By drawing the mind inward,
Grants the accomplishment one desires.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirty-fifth chapter, called "The Rite of the Defining Characteristics That Explain the Rite of the Inner Mudrā of Mahābhairavī," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the yoginī of Kāminī:
The tantra of the signs of the mudrā
Shall be explained out of compassion;
By the yogin's seeing it, accomplishment comes.
When things are among the appearances,
They are seen as the domain of the faculties.
They appear black and white and red,
Blue and orange and green,
Yellow and pale blue and multicolored;
Variegated, and the color of smoke;
Blue mixed with orange;
And again the color white and black;
Orange and pale green-blue;
Blue and the color red;
White-blue and red-green;
Cloud-colored and variegated;
Variegated and the color yellow;
Smoke and golden-colored;
Yellow-green and green-yellow;
Red and conch-colored;
Smoky, white, variegated, blue;
Mixed yellow and red;
Smoky and the color pale blue;
White and variegated in color;
Grey and the color of smoke;
Pale white and pale red;
Cloud-colored, grey, and pale green;
Variegated and the color grey.
In the hues of all the colors
Are the colors of the twelve elements:
Gold and silver, copper and iron,
Bell metal and brass.
By colors mixed with one another,
These are the twelve of great love.
All the elements of the yogins:
There appearances arise.
At the time when appearances arise through that,
Great magical display will come forth.
By fully knowing the two mudrās,
It is accomplished through seeing the colors.
From the gathering of the former and the later,
If it is the very self of innate union,
Through the application of Vāyuvegā,
In the place of Triśakuni,
The goddess is in the power of diligence.
So too the meanings of the syllables:
ba descends into the central channel;
By the power of habitual tendencies one dies.
In all the junctures of time, among phenomena,
Again it is the very self of the path of seeing.
be is endowed with objects:
It is the path of meditation of selves.
That very one, closely embraced by the mother,
Is the path of liberation of the protectors.
The heavens, the human world, and beneath the earth
Are called the three kinds of beings.
One who comes to see the bliss of sameness
Abides at the boundary of the blazing vajra.
Because the vase breath and the rest are practiced,
It is Caṇḍī with the subtle channel.
In the lotus are all the channels;
There is no gnosis like it.
Therefore diligence arises;
In power, gnosis possesses gnosis.
Knowing this, the constant yogin,
Enjoying desire with the woman of desire,
And the mistress of the maṇḍala
Has arisen perfectly in the syllables of the secret mantra.
In the supreme innate perfect awakening
The great goddess will be born.
oṃ be byā tā ghra la cā saṃ rmā ghamba ma ra rdha dhā ṇa ri ka ṇe rā hūṃ ya hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā phaṭ hā svā hā
oṃ bā yu be ge hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Her color and form are just as before.
In the center of the thirty-seven,
Through the heart mudrā of the three realms,
Whatever that is, is renowned as perfect power.
In the great secret wheel of speech,
Whatever I teach in sequence,
All should be known as true.
Therefore Vajradhara is uplifted.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirty-sixth chapter, called "The Defining Characteristics of the Rite of the Color Mudrā of Vāyuvegā," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then, when Vajravārāhī
Had worshiped with the proper rite:
"Listen, goddess, I shall explain.
The deity mudrā of the Guru himself:
The Kisser, and likewise Lāmā,
Ḍākinī and Rūpiṇī,
She Who Transforms and She Who Follows;
The yoga, and likewise the six perfections.
The signs of the left hand
Are shown by the divisions of yoga;
Each one becomes threefold.
The three kinds of heroes, namely
The Tathāgata and the Vajra,
Ārolik and Paramāśva,
And the Herukas, are on the left hand.

The wise know the yoga of union in pairs:
Locanā and Māmakī,
Tārā, Pāṇḍaravāsinī, Nairātmyā,
And likewise Vajradhātvīśvarī
Should be known as the six perfections.
Thus the three kinds should be known.
The hero is the lord of all beings;
Kṣitigarbha and the others are there;
Likewise the ḍākas and the others are chief.
The goddess is placed on the back of the hand;
On the fingers are the tathāgatas;
Likewise the goddess is at the middle joints;
On the palm of the hand is the tathāgata;
The hero likewise is on the nails;
At the place of the bracelet is the yoginī.
One makes the sign with the tip of the hand;
The wise should perform the dismissal behind.
Whoever is the lord of whatever,
It should be indicated by that very one.
Through the application of Surābhakṣī,
Kosala, and the tip of the nose,
Through the very self of the power of mindfulness,
One should eat and hold a feast.
At the time of sleep, born from all,
Hold the great mudrā well.
At the time of Rāhu, fire in the Sun;
What is eaten is likewise at the tip of the nose.
From the subtle, nondual form,
Having made the objects too into food,
From the seed of the flower of mind,
kṣi drips a thread of nectar.
Thus the yogin who ascertains it
Proceeds from the liquor of the navel that has gone before.
The mind planted in the shaft of the bow
Is one's own form, mere perfect knowledge;
The experience of equal taste, too,
Will be brought about here without doubt.
Through the power of the bliss of lalanā,
The seed is planted without outflow.
At that very time the constant mudrā
Is obtained through the sign of the hero.
Into the wheel of awakening, with joy,
She of the supreme face will enter.
If one is separated from the signs,
There is no other means of liberation in saṃsāra.
The mistress of the assembly of yoginīs
Is Surābhakṣī, the greatly renowned.
In the great wheel of the thirty-seven
Is she of the supreme face, arisen from the secret mantra.
oṃ śa ma trū hā ni bi su gha mbha na na ni ka bā rā ri ya ṇe hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ svā svā hā hā
oṃ su rā bha kṣī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Thus the king of mantras, manifestly,
Has not arisen and will not arise.
Through emitting and withdrawing,
It is spoken in lines by pairs.
The goddess is the very self of wisdom and skillful means;
Her color and shape are as before.
The answering mudrā, the way of the omniscient:
Having recognized it, one should not give it away.
If one who does not know
Accomplishes this great mudrā of the yoginī,
Being killed by snakes, thieves, and the like,
He goes to the hells and other such places.
To heroes who hold the various scriptures
It is given so that they may have true confidence.
Where it exists through the kindness of the Guru,
The wise one gives the sign by that condition.
Accomplishment is brought about
By the very self of things, and not otherwise.
Through his kindness there is liberation;
The wise should dwell in the field.
The instruction of the Guru is the field;
That is the place and the side of awakening.
The abiding ones are the cchomakas;
Absence of intrinsic nature is the reply to the cchomā.
Or else all this is the Guru,
Or all gurus are all things.
With instruction, the great gnosis
Has a domain beyond the path of speech.
In the wheel of body is great accomplishment;
It abides in the bliss of all the vehicles.
There is no absence of bliss, and no absence of non-bliss;
There all is the great nondual.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirty-seventh chapter, called "The Rite of the Defining Characteristics of the Nature of the Signs of the Full Application of Surābhakṣī," from the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the goddess, the lotus-holding Vārāhī,
Having made offerings, said:
"The great Vajra, lord of action and of the yoginīs,
Spoke to me:
'I shall explain the rite of divine substance
That accomplishes what all sentient beings need,
By which, in subjugation and the other rites,
The gods and all others likewise come.
First, the action of the root secret mantra
Of Vajravārāhī:
In Kaliṅga, of Śyāmadevī,
One makes the manifest command.
Those born into her families
Are granted as self and one's own.
In the center of the thirty-sevenfold self
One should meditate on the supreme mistress.
In the root mantra of two lines,
She is born from the seed syllables.
In this world and in the next,
Without her there is no accomplishment.'
oṃ śa tri ta sā sa ha ha sra sra ma mu hā khā sa ya ha hūṃ sre hūṃ ka phaṭ ro svā mā hā ba lyā nta barti ṇe hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svā hā
oṃ śyā ma de bī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
This, the supreme of the maṇḍala,
Is to be meditated upon, recited, and contemplated.
Vajravārāhī is not to be made two;
Strive in the root mantras.
A cloth moistened with the flower of menstruation
Is to be dried well;
With the dung of the gargaṭa,
One who desires accomplishment offers incense.
With this incense the mantra practitioner
Should draw near to the Blessed Lady.
Endowed with the union of Vārāhī,
She spoke these words:
'From the gathering of the concordant channels,
All beings arise from the left.
One's own continuum, as though beautiful,
Is the cloth of menstrual dust.
The dung too, mixed with the three worlds
And with gnosis, becomes incense.
The mind set down like a cat:
This word is in the syllable hyā.
The green, too, is the union with compassion;
The goddess likewise is the central channel.
What is bliss? What is knowledge?
What is the domain of the ultimate?
The one who is like a garment, in the great yoga,
Consciousness is held to be the goddess.
She is the very self of all people, the greatest,
Traveling in all directions.
Having bound skull cups mouth to mouth,
One declares the fire offering with hair.
This is renowned as the flower;
It guards each of the samayas.
One should always worship the yoginī,
With the peaceful drink every day.

One who knows the mantra that acts in this way
Is the one who bestows all accomplishments.
With beef pounded with liquor
One should perform the fire offering with the left hand.
If all the gods together with the lunar mansions,
The nāgas and others, those who dwell in the Thirty-Three,
And even the buddhas come under control,
What need is there to speak of petty humans?
Phlegm and a tooth stick,
And likewise the rubbings of one's own body,
Pounded with liquor, with the name:
By this fire offering one brings the target, together with the world, under control.
Menstrual blood
And the vomit of food that has been eaten,
Together with human hair,
Is supreme for summoning this very moment.
The vomit of butter from one's own body
And one's own hair the wise should burn;
By a blazing fire of neem wood,
It is supreme for causing enmity at once.
One performs the fire offering with crow feathers;
In equipoise, in a fire of datura,
With black butter and salt together,
It brings killing and expulsion at once.
Through nine thousand oblations
One will before long obtain a kingdom.
Of the oblations to be burned,
The wise offer a hundred with flesh;
Three months after that,
Poverty is destroyed in an instant.
Whoever constantly recollects this
Becomes agreeable to people.
In twenty-one nights
It will be accomplished; there is no doubt of this.
Women, the supreme host of the Bodiless One,
With minds intoxicated, will be won.
The worlds intoxicated by desire
Grant fearlessness.
Surrounded by ten million women,
The accomplished one sports upon the earth.
Endowed with the union of Vajravārāhī,
Equal to the yoga of glorious Heruka,
Here the other vajra masters
And all the heroes pay homage to him;
To one gentle in the conduct of samaya
She grants accomplishment in the three worlds.
Through the application of yoga explained before,
The fire offering done in this way is accomplished.
There is no action other than yoga;
Without action there is no union.
The action of the twelve transitions:
The flowing of the winds plants them.
From the meeting at the junction of the two,
Look within the hearth of the fire offering.
The two navels are in meeting,
And again at all the junctions.
At that time it should be known:
The mantra too, a hundred thousand for each syllable;
Recited, urged on, and done in secret,
It brings accomplishment on every side.
Having properly made the vajra yantra,
Then one should set out on the ground
A grid of fifty less one squares.
The mantra practitioner places the mantra of Sumbharāja.
In it, first, is Vairocana;
In the seventh, again, that same one.
In the ninth one places the syllable su;
In the thirteenth, the syllable gri;
The seventeenth is the syllable bha;
Again, in the nineteenth, hṇa;
In the thirty-first one places the syllable gri;
In the thirty-third, the syllable ni;
What is to be placed in the thirty-seventh,
The wise apply to the syllable hṇa.
Here in the forty-first
Likewise one places the syllable su;
In the forty-third likewise
One should apply the seed of gnosis.
What is placed in the forty-ninth,
By the all-knowing, is the syllable sambha.
What is to be placed in the second section
Is the first vowel of the Vedas;
Likewise here in the sixth section,
The wind, at all times.
In the tenth one places the syllable gri;
In the twelfth, the syllable ba;
In the eighteenth, the syllable hṇa;
In the thirty-second, the syllable ya;
In the thirty-eighth, the syllable hṇa;
In the fortieth, the seed ya;
Likewise here in the forty-fourth
One should again place the syllable gri.
In the forty-eighth, the mantra practitioner
Always places the syllable hūṃ, the indestructible.
Likewise here in the eighth,
Again the lord of all syllables.
In the fortieth is the syllable ja;
In the sixteenth, the syllable bha;
In the twentieth, the syllable ā;
In the twenty-fourth, the syllable ga;
In the twenty-sixth one again places na;
In the thirtieth one should apply vān;
In the thirty-fourth one places ya;
The syllable rā likewise in the thirty-sixth;
In the forty-second one should place hūṃ.
This completes the vajra yantra.
In the twelve sections
One should insert the name of the one to be accomplished.
Write the syllable hūṃ in the center
Of the names of the targets in all the sections.
Surround it entirely with the root mantra,
And make a garland of vajras.
A pair of wheels should be drawn;
For subjugation, on the base of an earthen bowl.
The yantra arisen from the vajra
Should be properly drawn with the substances of subjugation.
Even the Buddha Vajradhara
Will then quickly come.
From the descent of the five nectars,
The samaya is to be performed there.
Through the fire offering of the inner self
One should know the chief lady of the mantra.
Through the union of the hero in nonduality,
I shall explain the supreme action that follows.
The secret worship of the yogins
Through constant embrace brings about the supreme.
Throughout all the nights the practitioner
Should then worship the messenger.
Having placed the root of the piśāca plant in the mouth,
For as long as one wishes,
For that long the liṅga will be fit for action;
I have not explained it otherwise.
Earth creeper cooked in sesame oil
Should be used to anoint the feet.
The sign of that, O wise one,
Is that it is rigid at all times, O wise one.
Pātha, kaṭuka, rohiṇī,
And horse scent: these
Should be made in equal parts;
The remainder of the rite of great love,
Pounded with the milk of arka:
With this one should rub the liṅga.
To whatever size one wishes,
It will become that size, it is said.
Quickly, if one washes it
With warm water, it returns to its former size.
The filaments of a white lotus,
Pounded with honey, and what comes from it:
If given, the liṅga becomes firm,
And one enjoys Kāminī.
The filaments of a white lotus,
White sesame, and likewise others,
Fresh butter, and the root of the sole-hero plant,
Pounded together with honey:
If one smears it on the navel,
For that long the liṅga will not fall.
The blood of a menstruating woman and the two kinds of turmeric,
With the buttermilk of one who has been taken as wife,
Two measures should be boiled down
Until it has become a single measure.
By drinking it thus for seven days,
One completes the elixir.
Thus the supreme and principal application
Was spoken by the true hero.
Having taken the liver of a monkey,
One should make it into a fine powder.
Fully endowed with one's own faculties,
It is the unsurpassed means of subjugation.

Wherever one may be staying,
Having recited Vajravārāhī,
With the threatening forefinger over which one has recited once,
One should circle to the left and to the right.
Whatever one wishes, all of it,
She bestows it all completely.
Grain, clothing, and gold,
Feasts, food, and drink:
All of these one obtains,
And the mantrin, having taken them, brings them to completion.
The unchanging substance and blood
The practitioner should mix into one;
By reciting the vidyā seven times over it,
And then by smearing it on a person,
At its touch men become crippled;
The word is not otherwise.
By water over which this vidyā
Has been recited once, they are released.
Likewise, by tying on a thread
Anointed with the blood of any being whatever,
Through recitation one seizes the form;
All of these are released by water,
And of this there is no doubt.
Women of beautiful form, kings,
And lords of the earth: if one looks toward them,
Even those the practitioner has not seen,
All of them are summoned.
If one consecrates it seven times,
She comes that very night;
Within seven nights, with the force of an arrow,
She comes, and of this there is no doubt.
Thus, for all rites, those to be done and not to be done,
Arriving at the vajra treasure,
The one whose nature is the application of self-known awareness
Accomplishes gnosis gone to the far shore.
In the grid of the yantra of the elements,
The channel is Rūpiṇī of subtle form;
The syllable is the great wind,
And the deity is the one who goes everywhere.
The green one, the great nondual Self,
The goddess, is the ocean of gnosis.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirty-eighth chapter of the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra, called "The Characteristics of the Rites of the Fire Offering for Magnetizing, the Yantra Wheel, and the Vajra Maṇḍala of Śyāmadevī, Nondual with Vajravārāhī."

Then the Lord, the Blessed Ḍāka,
Spoke once again:
Through being fully joined with Lampaka,
The application of the yoginī Subhadrā
Should be known as the power of wisdom.
Likewise the yantra, like the gait of a horse,
Abides through the application of supreme joy,
By the yogin of the root mantra.
Endowed with gnosis, lord of the moving breath,
He fully holds the conventional truth.
Through the all-pervading course of the channels,
Power is present in all the rites.
Entered fully through sleep,
All the life breath is within it.
As if eating at the great rite,
Having worked it with the root mantra,
And having melted the warm nectar,
In its center is the wheel made of the channels.
It has the nature of thirty-seven;
As it hangs down, awakening is born.
The goddess, ever in the form of fire,
Is by nature the heart of the chief goddesses.
Having reached the skeleton, the wind, and consciousness,
Those whose nature is the charnel grounds,
Having known their seed syllables,
The messengers ever generate them.
At the end of the seed syllable of each one
Is the root secret mantra:
oṃ ma jñā hā na pi sā śig ta ra ru pra dhi bhā ra ya ba hūṃ svā hūṃ hā pha ri ṭa ṇe pha hūṃ ṭa hūṃ svā phaṭ hā phaṭ svā hā
oṃ su bha dre hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Thus, having arisen fully from the mantra,
With color and form according to the rite,
The yogin, whose nature is nectar,
Should meditate in the center of the secret place.
One should offer torma and make fire offerings;
Afterward one should undertake these rites.
The mantrin, through the yoga of the deity,
Having done a hundred thousand recitations:
The yantra wheel that brings happiness
To all beings, I shall explain.
Having made the wheel in the form of the previous one,
One should set the secret mantra here.
Vairocana comes first;
Then in the second one should place it.
In the third one should give the syllable hūṃ;
In the fifth, the syllable oṃ.
In the sixth give the syllable gri;
The syllable hūṃ is in the seventh.
In the eighth give the syllable mbha;
In the tenth, the syllable su.
In the twelfth give the syllable hūṃ;
In the fourteenth, the syllable gri.
In the fifteenth, the syllable mbha;
In the sixteenth, likewise, the syllable hūṃ.
In the seventeenth, the syllable su;
In the nineteenth one should join hṇa.
In the twentieth one should give the syllable phaṭ;
The syllable hṇa in the twenty-first.
In the twenty-ninth, Vairocana;
In the thirtieth, two syllables ya;
In the thirty-first, that again.
The first Veda in the thirty-third;
In the thirty-fourth, the syllable ya;
The syllables rā ja in the thirty-fifth;
In the thirty-sixth give the syllable hṇa;
In the thirty-eighth, the syllable gri.
In the fortieth one should give van;
In the forty-second, likewise, ā;
In the forty-third, hṇa;
In the forty-fourth, hūṃ hūṃ;
In the forty-fifth, gri;
In the forty-seventh, na;
In the forty-eighth one should give
The two syllables hūṃ hūṃ.
The sound bha ga is given once more,
In the forty-ninth.
In the rest one should write the root mantra.
It should be accomplished in four wheels.
All around, encircling it,
The mantrin should draw Hayagrīva.
With the second turned to the left,
One should make the yantra, a yantra joined face to face.
As born from the secret syllables
It should be known by the yogin of the innate.
It serves for summoning nāgas, for subjugation,
And for slaying gods and the like.
The yogin whose nature is love
Should always slay his own mind.
Whatever hostility there is toward it,
By that, all are slain.
Otherwise, if one with a hateful mind
Slays, one will not obtain the accomplishments.
Whatever rites belong to the Later Saṃvara,
All of them will certainly be performed.
A little of the rites of each one
I shall explain here in brief.
Likewise the eight nāga kings
Should be meditated on in order, beginning with Ananta.
Vāsuki should be known as the life wind;
The downward-clearing wind is Śaṅkhapāla.
The evenly abiding wind is the nāga Padma;
Meditative concentration is Mahāpadma.
The pervading wind is the nāga Takṣaka;
The nāga wind, again, is Hulu.
The tortoise wind should be known as Ananta;
The lizard wind is the nāga Jaya;
Devadatta is Vijaya;
Dhanañjaya is Ananta.
By drawing the breath in, one summons;
By sending it out, one requests departure.
By the vase breath one brings down rain;
By the even wind it is dispelled.
Likewise the eight consciousnesses
Should here be known as the nāginīs.
Gnosis and space, the other two,
Are the cause of the ten consciousnesses.
One should give eight syllables phuḥ,
And likewise the various samādhis.

Entwined through being born from one another,
In the armpits of those nāginīs.
In the great form of Vajraḍāka
One should accomplish the maṇḍala of the nāgas,
With the colors in their due order,
And the eight watches with their two junctures.
The very utterance of the root mantra
Should be known as the nāgas and nāginīs.
Having made the senses dissolve,
One recites a hundred thousand for each syllable.
Whatever outer rites there are,
One should perform each in the manner of that rite.
One should make the form whatever one wishes,
Whether wrathful or peaceful forms.
Mind is rightly proclaimed to be form;
Therefore she bears the name Subhadrā.
The lord of yogins, the master of all,
By him the deity is made.
Moreover, no deity other
Than the self of outer and inner has been taught.
By his giving expression to all,
The yogin goes wherever he wishes.
Through the fault of the habitual tendencies of beings,
Clouds are seen outside as well;
At the time the tendencies are exhausted,
Clouds and nāgas are not seen.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the thirty-ninth chapter of the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra, called "The Characteristics of the Rites of the Nāga Activities of the Families: A Brief Teaching of the Root Mantra of the Blessed One and of the Horse Mantra, Whose Nature Is the Union Nondual with Subhadrā."

Then, once more, for that reason,
The goddess made offering here,
Endowed with the passion of Vārāhī.
Pleased, the deity gave this teaching:
Having fully joined with Aśvakarṇā,
At all times, in Kāñcī,
With samādhi and the limbs of awakening,
She truly abides in the heart.
The syllable ha and the nāda, throughout the body,
Arise from the very center of the heart.
The syllable yi is the life force of movement;
It is moved by the vajra wind.
However many there are, together with bliss,
Again there are three on each petal:
The qualities of body, speech, and mind,
Twenty-four on the eight petals,
And twelve in the lotus of the navel:
The yoginīs are always thirty-six.
Then, of each one of these here,
At the junctures are the horse and the rest.
The syllable ṇa is not that goddess;
Her nature is both manifest and unmanifest.
Though born in the desire realm, she is a woman
Whose activity takes the form of liberation.
The syllable cyā is the principle of transference;
All are freed from the bounds of awareness.
In the center of the self of thirty-seven,
One should apply oneself to the rite of slaying.
Whatever rites of slaying there are
Are to be performed against one who kills the Guru,
Or one who does harm to the teaching,
Or likewise one who commits the five acts of immediate retribution.
With poison, mustard seed, and salt,
Datura, charcoal from the charnel ground,
And blood from one's own forefinger,
One should carefully draw the wheel.
The yantra of Yama always
Does its work; it is not otherwise.
Having understood the grid explained before,
One should set out this garland of secret mantra.
First is Vairocana;
In the second, su is pronounced;
In the third, the syllable mbha is pronounced;
The first Veda, likewise, in the fifth;
In the sixth, likewise, the syllable gri;
And in the seventh, the syllable hṇa.
In the eighth, two syllables hūṃ;
In the ninth, likewise, the syllable phaṭ;
In the tenth, likewise, that syllable;
And again in the twelfth, the syllable hūṃ.
In the thirteenth, the syllable phaṭ;
The syllable gri is in the fourteenth.
The syllable hūṃ in the fifteenth;
The syllable mbha in the sixteenth;
In the seventeenth, the syllable su;
The syllable hūṃ in the nineteenth.
In the twentieth give the syllable hūṃ;
The syllable hṇa in the twenty-first;
Vairocana in the twenty-ninth;
In the thirtieth, again, the syllable gri.
In the thirty-first, the syllable hṇa;
In the thirty-third, Vairocana;
In the thirty-fourth, the syllable ā,
And there one should join the syllable na.
In the thirty-fifth, the seed ya,
And there one should give the syllable ho.
In the thirty-sixth give two hūṃ;
In the thirty-seventh, the syllable phaṭ;
In the thirty-eighth, two pa ya;
In the fortieth, likewise, two hūṃ;
In the forty-first, phaṭ;
In the forty-second, two bha ga;
In the forty-third,
The two syllables pa ya;
In the forty-fourth, hṇa;
In the forty-fifth, the syllable gri;
In the forty-seventh
One should join the sound ra ja.
The sound bidyā, again, the mantrin places
In the forty-eighth segment.
The syllable vān is to be given once more,
In the forty-ninth.
In the rest is the armor mantra,
With the name of the target inserted within.
The faces of Yama in all the corners,
And the hands and feet are placed in the directions.
The heart is the ground of Yama;
One should meditate in its center.
With the nature of the union of gnosis and consciousness,
The wise one should engage in the conduct of mantra.
The one who despises the Guru and the sacred commitments
Is consciousness and the inferior intellect.
The one who does harm to the teaching
Is the sense sources, the eye and the rest.
The one who commits the five acts of immediate retribution
Is the aggregates, the elements, and likewise the rest.
The family that is one for all beings
Is the aggregates and so forth, and the elements.
For the lords of yogins,
Slaying is the slaying of the element of beings.
Having looked upon the face of whoever it may be,
The form having the nature of that one
Is slain by gnosis in its habitual tendencies;
It is not that other beings are slain.
Or else, by performing the rite of slaying them,
It is like that slaying.
The fire offering with poison and other substances
Should be performed by one in the form of true awareness.
One should call to mind the maṇḍala wheel there;
The burning is in Caṇḍī.
Born from the seed syllables
Is the maṇḍala of Aśvakarṇā,
With color and shape as before.
Great is the offering of wisdom and means.
oṃ śrī saṣṭi rba saṃ mā hā ra ra ba kā la ra ṅhāṃ kā si ya ne hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ svā svā hā hā
oṃ ha yā ka hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
The assembly, filled with amazement
And terrified, fell to the ground.
Alas! The worlds that do not know this way
Are bewildered indeed.
Abiding in every bliss,
One subdues them here by their life breath.
As the beings fell to the ground in amazement,
Vajrapāṇi and the others rose,
Set their knees upon the ground,
And said, "Great Being, I ask:

By what are the self and whose very being,
Their slaying, or else the rite,
And the nature of result and cause,
O Blessed One, where are these established?
If you have said that, by its own results,
It is like that slaying,
Or else that the cause lies with the Blessed One
And the result is the slaying of a person,
My lord, please speak!
Please dispel this doubt."
He declared:
"With one-pointed mind, to these words
Listen, O Vajra!
The result accords with the action.
I am the abode of all bliss;
By the power of that action
You are buddhas and bodhisattvas.
The nature of cause and result,
Because it is nondual, has become one.
That is the cause of all things;
For the wise, action is the condition.
Armies and the like on the great earth:
By the seeing of the wise,
One should make all of them nondual in nature;
Therefore it is endowed with slaying.
By slaying the self there is buddhahood itself,
Not by slaying all other people.
By the power of time, within a kingdom,
It is accomplished with little effort.
Therefore, having examined former karma,
The wise should designate the elements,
In this place and that,
As having the nature of the heroes.
By slaying the self in that way,
The elements of beings are just that.
Having carefully generated love,
One must certainly slay.
In this world and in the world beyond,
One lacking in love will not succeed."
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the fortieth chapter of the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra, called "Slaying: The Characteristics of the Rite of the Armor Mantra and the Root Mantra through the Union Nondual of Aśvakarṇā and the Hero."

Next, the application for causing madness,
Hard to find anywhere,
Through full union with Khagānanā,
I shall explain in brief.
Diligence, the quality that is a limb of awakening,
Has truly entered Himālaya.
In the center of the maṇḍala of the navel
One should meditate on the chief lady of the wheel.
The messenger whose very nature is the form of space,
In the palate, and in the center of the vajra and the lotus:
Consciousness, together with the wind,
Is shot upward with the force of an arrow.
The body should be made to tremble;
This is done by applying fierce force.
The yogin who practices this constantly
Will accomplish the supreme undefiled state.
As long as the yogin does not wish it,
Wind and consciousness will not move.
The life wind that has reached the nostrils
Should be moved into the center of the navel.
By joining the navel and the two nostrils,
And holding it there, it will not move.
Wind and consciousness together:
That is wandering about in madness.
At that time, for one whose nature is this union,
Whatever things are seen
Turn into things resembling that.
With a fire offering in a fire of datura,
One should burn hair hung on a tree
And piśāca root.
Through the full application of this rite,
A buddha goes mad in an instant.
Therefore diligence, the limb of awakening,
Should be actualized from these.
Through diligence, by seeing perfect awakening,
One will always attain it swiftly.
The syllable hi, as the gnosis that hangs down,
Was explained by the Hero himself.
The syllable mā, too, is self-awareness;
Abiding in the state of the aspects of the self,
In dissolution and enjoyment, the form of the aspects,
One endowed with the body of a buddha will be born.
Those who are aware, whose nature is self-awareness,
Having worked for the benefit of beings, will pass on.
In the center of the self of thirty-seven
One should meditate on the yoginī of the rite,
With color and form as before,
Arisen from the application of secret mantra:
oṃ a sa ntra rba mā nā lā gā dhā nāṃ ri śo ṇe ṣa hūṃ ṇa hūṃ sta phaṭ mbha phaṭ na svā bi hā dhrā ba ṇā ya hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
oṃ kha gā na ne hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
The one whose nature is the application of this mantra
Has the nature of wisdom and means.
Just as before,
I shall explain the yantra wheel.
On the wheel of the pretas, always,
One should set out this garland of mantra.
First is Vairocana;
In the second one should join mbha;
In the third, give that same one likewise;
In the fifth, the first of the Vedas;
The syllable hṇa should be given in the sixth;
The syllable hūṃ is set in the seventh;
In the eighth, the syllable śu;
In the ninth, one should give that same one.
In the tenth one should give two hūṃ;
The syllable gri, again, in the twelfth;
In the thirteenth, the syllable hṇa;
In the fourteenth, the syllable hūṃ;
In the fifteenth, the syllable ni;
The syllable hūṃ is set in the sixteenth;
In the seventeenth, the syllable phaṭ;
The syllable gri in the nineteenth;
Give the syllable hūṃ in the twentieth;
The syllable phaṭ in the twenty-first;
Vairocana in the twenty-ninth;
In the thirtieth, set the syllable hṇa again;
In the thirty-first, give that same one;
In the thirty-third, Vairocana;
In the thirty-fourth, the two ya ho;
In the thirty-fifth, the two rā ja;
Give the syllable gri in the thirty-sixth;
In the thirty-seventh, that same one;
In the thirty-eighth, the two hūṃ hūṃ;
In the fortieth, the word ā na ya;
In the forty-first, van;
Two hūṃ in the forty-second;
In the forty-third, pa ya;
In the forty-fourth, that same one;
In the forty-fifth, hūṃ and phaṭ;
bha ga in the forty-seventh;
In the forty-eighth, hūṃ;
In the forty-ninth, phaṭ.
In the empty spaces, the heart mantra,
With the name of the target inserted within.
Everywhere around, one should draw pretas
Seizing the yantra with their mouths.
On cloth from the charnel ground
It is to be made face to face.
This wheel, in the place of the navel,
The wise one should meditate on.
The preta is the essential nature of the dharmadhātu;
Because it devours conceptual thought, it is Yama.
The horse, too, is in what is a place and what is not a place.
The vajra, through the awakening of the essence;
The gait of the nāga is the nature
Of all the people of the Great Vehicle.
Virtue and nonvirtue are the vetāla;
It proceeds by deceiving beings.
The others, the three vehicles,
Are taught to those with former karma.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-first chapter of the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra, called "The Rite, together with the Heart Mantra, of the Ritual of the Characteristics of the Nature of the Yantra Wheel for Causing Madness, Nondual of Khagānanā and the Hero."

Then, through union with Cakravegā,
I shall explain the wheel of the serpents.
In Pretapurī, the limb of awakening
Called joy, in brief:
It paralyzes all forces,
Its activity reaching wheel-turning monarchs and the rest.
With turmeric mixed with lac
One should draw the yantra on a skull cup.
One practices it at all times,
At the navel and the other places, three with a half.
With the form of a vetāla as one's nature,
It paralyzes going and coming.
Together with a woman, an excellent mudrā,
One should gradually let out the breath.
Consciousness, the chief, the lord of all,
Dissolves into the blisses.
It does not move,
O wise one, at any time.
For it, all acts are absent;
The nature of what is done fully arises.
Through the rite, the buddha field
And its place should be measured.
Whatever thing the yogin calls to mind,
That very thing he paralyzes at once.
Engaging in it through that rite,
One should not again call to mind other rites.
In this place and that, all people
Will bring about great awakening.
Moreover, having passed into complete nirvāṇa,
They enter from great bliss.
rī here is the nature of passion;
It acts upon all people.
Born from joy, the limb of awakening,
The nature has arisen there.
Abiding in the state of Cakravegā,
One should thus perform the rite of paralysis.
Color and shape are as before;
One should meditate in the center of the wheel.
Mantra, shape, and the nature of the Dharma
Will arise in that place.
oṃ kro su dha me bi ru gra ka ha ma dhā pi ra ta ṇā nā ya dā hūṃ ya hūṃ hūṃ pha hūṃ ṭa pha pha ṭa ṭa pha svā ṭa hā svā hā
oṃ cakra be ge hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
This secret mantra of the great goddess,
Supreme, has the nature of wisdom and means.
The wheel, too, is as explained before,
Made of fifty less one.
First is Vairocana;
The syllable su, again, in the second;
In the third segment one should give mbha;
The syllable ni likewise in the fourth.
In the fifth one should give the syllable su;
Again, in the sixth, the syllable mbha.
The syllable hūṃ should be given in the seventh;
The syllable of wind in the eighth.
The syllable gri should be given in the ninth;
In the tenth, likewise, the syllable hṇa.
In the eleventh give pa;
Then in the twelfth, the syllable ya.
The syllable hūṃ in the thirteenth;
In the fourteenth, the syllable hūṃ.
The syllable pa in the fifteenth;
The syllable baṃ should be set in the sixteenth.
The syllable bi in the seventeenth;
Again, the syllable dyā in the eighteenth.
In the twentieth less one, rā;
In the twentieth, set hūṃ again;
In the twenty-first, give that same one;
Set the syllable hṇa in the twenty-second.
The syllable ga in the twenty-third;
In the twenty-fourth, the syllable phaṭ.
In the twenty-sixth give the syllable ja;
The syllable hūṃ in the twenty-seventh.
In the twenty-eighth, the syllable phaṭ;
The syllable gri in the twenty-ninth.
In the thirtieth, the syllable bha;
In the thirty-first, the syllable hūṃ.
In the thirty-second, give that same one;
The syllable hūṃ in the thirty-third.
The syllable phaṭ in the thirty-fourth;
In the thirty-fifth one should set oṃ.
In the thirty-sixth, give that same one;
In the thirty-seventh, likewise, ho.
The ya in the thirty-eighth,
And in the thirty-ninth.
In the fortieth one should give ā;
In the forty-first, oṃ.
The syllable gri in the forty-second;
In the forty-third one should set phaṭ.
The syllable hūṃ in the forty-fourth;
In the forty-fifth, hūṃ;
In the forty-sixth one should give hūṃ;
In the forty-seventh, hṇa.
The syllable gri in the forty-eighth;
In the fiftieth less one, hṇa.
Thus the yantra wheel,
Born from the throat, should always be set out.
All paralysis comes from the great body;
The life force has the great nature of the vajra.
Whatever is applied in that way
Paralyzes beings who do harm.
Release, for all beings,
Is again by the mind of the yogin.
From practicing it for one year
It is accomplished; of this there is no doubt.
The near-heart mantra
Should be written properly in the central square.
Having inserted the name of the target within,
One should make wind and consciousness dissolve.
It is renowned as the coiled serpent;
The yantra, too, belongs to embodied beings.
The mountain goes to the heap;
The mind of awakening is the skull cup.
Upon it is the very giving,
By whichever yantra the wise one uses.
All the syllables are its nature,
And it should be known from the days and nights as well.
The waves of the great servant of the mind
Are what is to be paralyzed.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-second chapter of the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra, the chapter of ritual called "The Nature of the Characteristics of the Union Nondual with the Hero: The Rite of Paralysis of Cakravegā."

Then, through union with Khaṇḍarohā,
I shall clearly explain the rite as it is.
Having made the hearth for all the rites,
And the fire offering according to the ritual,
By seeing the rituals of the rites,
One should accomplish the rite of expulsion.
In the place Gṛhadevatā,
As pliancy, the limb of awakening:
The mind of awakening at the aperture of space, too,
The great holder, is shot upward.
What is to be dried up, through the aspect of aḥ,
Should be held firmly in the head.
By the syllable ṇḍa it is made to go,
Circling all about with the force of an arrow.
Having meditated on it with the maṇḍala of wind,
A victory banner in the center of the head,
That consciousness will be generated.
Across hundreds of thousands and thousands of leagues,
One attains buddhahood without remainder.
That yogin, in the three worlds,
With hā hā he he in eight ways,
Laughs through the yoga of the ḍākas.
Through the act of going to Khecara,
The yoginī does so there.
From one's own small, constant going upward,
One should know that one dies at home.
It steals away all evil,
Having the ritual of the lord of hatred.
The whole of the Great Vehicle should be given,
Always without concealment.
The consolation of the vajra holder, too,
Should be extended to all beings.
For as long as one does not act toward the target,
For that long one is a great vajra yogin.
Pliancy, in all phenomena,
As the nature of gnosis and the knowable,
Should be viewed as the very limb of awakening;
That mind is everything that bears phenomena.

Thus, knowing the particular phenomena,
The awakening of the buddhas is fully taught.
It is expulsion into the Great Vehicle;
It should be completed with this fire offering.
Crow flesh, wings, and sinews,
And the perch of a vulture,
One should burn with one's mind fixed on that one.
If it drives out even the buddhas,
What need is there to speak of all the beings
Of the small world of the earth?
In the center of the self of thirty-seven
One should meditate on the one explained before,
Arisen from the syllables of the mantra,
Having the nature of wisdom and means:
oṃ bu ma dha hā ko kā ṭi śa sa ti ha ya sra kā kṣa tma ṇā ne ni hūṃ ṣpā hūṃ da phaṭ nā phaṭ ya svā hūṃ hā hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
oṃ khaṇḍa ro hi hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
Arisen fully from this mantra,
The yantra has the very nature of the previous one.
In the grid made of fifty less one,
In the places of the mantra beautiful in harming:
First is Vairocana;
In the second one should give that same one.
The syllable ā should be given in the third;
In the fourth, the syllable na;
In the fifth, the syllable ya;
In the sixth one should give the syllable ya;
In the seventh, the syllable su;
In the eighth, the syllable phaṭ;
In the ninth, likewise, the syllable su;
In the tenth, Vairocana;
The syllable gri should be set in the eleventh;
In the twelfth, the syllable hṇā;
In the thirteenth, the syllable mbha;
In the fourteenth, the seed na;
In the fifteenth, likewise, two hūṃ;
In the sixteenth, hūṃ phaṭ;
In the seventeenth, Vairocana;
In the eighteenth, the syllable hṇā;
In the nineteenth one should set gri;
In the twentieth give the syllable pa;
In the twenty-first one should join ya;
In the twenty-third, the syllable hūṃ;
In the twenty-fourth set hūṃ phaṭ;
Give the syllable hūṃ in the twenty-sixth;
In the twenty-seventh one should join ya;
The syllable hūṃ in the twenty-ninth;
The syllable ja should be given first;
In the thirtieth, give that same one;
The syllable gri in the thirty-first;
In the thirty-second, the syllable hūṃ;
The syllable hṇa in the thirty-third;
In the thirty-fourth one should give gri;
In the thirty-fifth one should set ho;
In the thirty-sixth, the syllable rā;
In the thirty-seventh, the two hūṃ phaṭ;
In the thirty-eighth, the seed ya;
In the fortieth less one, ya;
In the fortieth one should give hṇa;
In the forty-first, hūṃ;
The syllable bha in the forty-second;
In the forty-third, ni;
In the forty-fourth, dyā;
In the forty-fifth, bi;
In the forty-seventh, vān;
In the forty-eighth, ga;
The syllable mbha in the forty-ninth.
The yantra should be placed in a vetāla.
The target, in five sections,
Together with the armor of the six yoginīs,
Should be enclosed within the secret mantra,
And it should be placed in the mouth of the vetāla.
With the name of whoever it may be,
One should draw the yantra with great love.
It should be known as belonging to that one,
And the wise one expels them.
The great secret is not to be shown;
It is accomplished in this very life,
Even by a yogin who has not recited the secret mantra
And is without meditation.
By the permission of the Guru and the yoginīs,
The all-pervading lord acts as he wishes.
Therefore one should attend upon the Guru,
And those of great nature should please him.
Through his delight one attains it;
Through the rite, awakening arises.
At the time the yantra dawns,
One should meditate on it in the head.
By that it should be known as fully accomplished;
At other times it is not accomplished.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the forty-third chapter of the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra, called "The Characteristics of the Ritual of the Armor Mantra of the Six Yoginīs: The Rite of Expulsion with the Yantra Wheel, Meditating on the Full Union of Khaṇḍarohā."

Then, the yantra for causing enmity
Between a pair of vetālas, likewise,
Through full union with Śauṇḍinī,
I shall explain in brief.
Having accomplished the place Saurāṣṭra,
The limb that is the full discrimination of phenomena,
So that awakening is attained without doubt,
One should know the supreme Kāminī.
As joining with the great intoxicant,
It is explained by the syllable śau.
The sublime substance of the elixir, too,
Is held in the supreme lotus.
Whatever thread is in the center of the brows,
Having gone inside the vajra jewel,
As it is born from union,
The syllable ṇḍi is not to be drunk by touching it oneself.
There, without touching, through consciousness,
One should drink the supreme water.
It is within selflessness;
That is explained by the syllable nī.
By drinking gnosis such as this,
All the senses become fully intoxicated,
And then, being divided among themselves,
They are made to quarrel by the mind.
Sporting in great bliss:
I have explained it as the syllables sau yiṃ.
Through the application of binding the rasanā,
One should suck the lower lip.
In the yantra of the horse and the buffalo,
The division is into the life wind and the downward-clearing wind.
The syllable ṣṭa, in all the joints,
Brings knowledge, without doubt.
Through that limb of full discrimination
One realizes the awakening of the selflessness of phenomena.
The yogin endowed with this
Divides and slays
All the senses and their objects,
And enjoys the world of Akṣobhya.
By slaying them with love,
He himself possesses the cause of liberation.
In the center of the maṇḍala wheel
One meditates on the supreme chief lady.
In the region between the brows, forms,
White or red, and the weapons,
Arise fully at that very time.
In the thirty-seven syllables,
The form whose nature is wisdom and means
Should be known as the supreme chief.
oṃ sa jñā rba nā ka mṛ rma ta pra ba barṣā rta pa nā ṇā ya ya ne hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ svā svā hā hā
oṃ śauṇḍi nī ye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svā hā
The great yogin whose nature is meditation
Should perform the rite as his mind desires.
With ink made from charcoal of the charnel ground,
One should write with a human bone.
The lunar mansion for all medicines
Is Puṣya and the rest, through the equality of the elements.
The wheel is as has been described;
Its segments, too, are fifty less one.
In the secret mantra of the mutual pair
The wise one should set out the syllables.
In the four corners is Vairocana;
The four doors are empty.

As for the rest, in the first row,
Filling the four segments in order,
One should set out hṇa sumbha gri.
Then in the seven segments of the second,
gri ya hoḥ gri bha ga pa ya
bā na bi phaṭ: write these in order.
In the third, on the sides of the middle,
One should place three on each side:
