Yama and the vināyaka,
Nairṛti and Vemacitrin,
Indra, Gaṇapati, Kārttikeya,
Rāhu, the moon, and the sun,
Bṛhaspati, the Protector, and likewise the Lofty One,
Vāsuki and Puruṣa, Agni:
Intoxicated, he presses them down with his feet.
Each is properly joined with his own consort;
Their colors and weapons are also to be made.
He is embraced by Vārāhī,
Who has her own form or is two-armed,
Very swift, holding various forms,
And again having a form like his own.

One should meditate on a thousand petals,
Which are by nature all the yoginīs.
Whatever the color of the Blessed One,
In that same way they are to be depicted.
Short and long, their qualities increased,
The eight groups beginning with the letter ā,
Divided by the twelve mother letters,
Are again the thousands of yoginīs.
They are to be known as ka-vajra and the rest,
Through the mind that is the condition of the elements.
Possessing the yoga of the lord of the six wheels,
All of them dance;
All of them press down upon a corpse.
Two-armed, or else the lord of the ḍākas,
She holds a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
Having a form in the aspect of great bliss.
Emanating from the heart of the buddha,
The three wheels send forth garlands of light rays.
With the life breath looking below and above,
One should summon the Saṃvara Ocean.
Again, sending it out with the breath,
The Ḍāka Ocean is the nondual self.
In all the means of accomplishment of Saṃvara,
One should perform the other yogas as they are.
Simply by acting in this way,
The accomplishment of the mudrā will arise.
All the treatises should be studied,
But one should not focus on other yogas.
One who keeps the conduct of the samaya
Is fully taught in the Laghusaṃvara.
Cast away everything like chaff,
And take up everything as the vow.
Through the thorough application of Bared Fangs,
Let it come by the stages of drawing the bow.
Its maṇḍala and wheel too
Should be meditated on through the former application.
Below and above, the two fangs
Are Saṃvara and the Ḍāka Ocean.
To the secret mantra that speaks one's own name,
Join the syllables hūṃ phaṭ at the end.
For all the secret mantras
Of the vajra of perfect samādhi, this is to be done.
All the ornaments that were fully explained
Are, for the knower of mantra, as before.

"Since my Lord has taught this tantra,
I am truly delighted.
Having done the deed together with you,
How is the lower mudrā?"
"I shall fully explain to you
The lower and the upper mudrā: listen, goddess!
The Ḍāka Ocean is great wisdom;
Skillful means is the Saṃvara Ocean.
Through their union below and above,
That is what is called mudrā.
The stalk that surely emerges from the mouth
Is the self commanded by speech.
That is the conduct of the ḍāka;
Consciousness and all things come.
The form of melting at the heart:
With its light, bind everything.
That is the binding in the Great Vehicle,
The great self, the form of wisdom.
Thus the form in which there is no duality
Is what is called mudrā."
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the great ocean,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This was the rite of Bared Fangs, the fourth chapter.

Then the goddess, having made offerings
And bowed, said this:
"The arising of the vow of beings,
Blessed Lord, please tell of it yourself."
"Through the thorough application against the enemy of the gods,
I shall fully explain it: listen, goddess!
The channel through which bliss comes,
Possessing a portion of the awakening mind,
In that yoga of the vow that ripens beings,
It emanates outward.
Desire and freedom from desire intermingled:
The vow has the form of beings.
Because it binds by that very thing,
It is the vow of former conduct.
In the space of vai, in space,
The three bodies perfectly abide.
The Dharma body, the enjoyment body, and the emanation body
Will be attained in this, without doubt.
When the mind is moving,
Dissolve it into the center of form.
Self-aware in nature, endowed with awakening,
One will attain the coemergent body.
An instant of samādhi without cognition,
In which one dissolves into the three existences:
That is to be known as the arising;
It is not the vow of other embodied beings.
The secret vow of the yogis:
Thus that reality is to be known with certainty.
The inner self, of the days and nights too,
Will by that be explained as the vow.

Possessing all the characteristics,
The groups of the vowels and consonants and the rest,
The portion of the first letter and so on:
Wherever the name is surely seen,
There the family is to be known;
The numerals too are fully endowed with beings.
Ananta and whatever other nāgas there are,
And likewise the sun and the other planets:
Placing the names of the ḍākinīs first,
They are renowned as those mātṛkās.
Generated by all the gods,
Of the mantras and of the tantras themselves,
She is the mother of the whole world;
Therefore one relies fully on the mother.
From the stopping of the nine channels,
They are to be known as the courses of the life breath.
a ā ka ca ṭa ta pa and
The ya śa group are nine letters.
i ī kha cha ṭha tha pha
And ra ṣa are declared the second.
u ū ga ja ḍa da ba
And la sa are the third group.
e ai gha jha ḍha dha bha
And va ha are the fourth group.
o au aṃ aḥ ṅ ña ṇa
na ma: thus they are ninefold in nature.
The four are renowned throughout,
And likewise the five solar ones.
Thus for the whole world
They abide in gathering in and sending forth.
Through those twelve great illusions
One should call to mind the host of seed forms.
They are renowned as the zodiac signs,
Fully endowed with all the lunar mansions.
By the portions of the moon and the sun,
Likewise the faces below and above,
The two, the stable and the moving,
Are explained as lunar mansions and signs.
The pair of life breaths is likewise renowned.
Long and short, victory and defeat:
Above is explained as its victory;
Below is death, equal and unequal.
All that ripens in the sun's course,
The lunar mansions, the signs, and likewise the planets:
Because the signs move in three ways,
They rise while abiding in the sun.
Fully endowed with the rising of the life breath,
It is to be known through all the signs.
The Crab, the Lion, the Maiden,
And the Pot are fully endowed with six families.
Their rising and their having risen
Are in due order through all the planets.
The Scorpion, the Horse,
The Deer, and the Pot are fully endowed;
By the measure of five portions
They rise with the rising of the planets.
The Hare, and likewise the Ram,
And likewise ripening in Skanda,
Their portion is the fourth:
The rising comes, and they rise.
All the risings of the day as well
Are fully endowed with the names of the results.
From Kālī to the Suckling One,
Twenty-four in all, in due order:
Whatever they resemble, such is their name;
They are to be known as the results.
Doubled enjoyment is written,
Endowed with the portions of the senses;
What is attained is to be known as the day.
My day goddess is constant;
By oneself, all that is needed,
And likewise apply it to others.
Therefore, in the outer and the inner,
Virtuous and nonvirtuous actions are to be known.
At the time when the vow arises,
It is to be indicated by nonconceptuality.
Whatever are the planets, know them as the vowels;
Whatever are the vowels, they are the signs."

The vowels and the signs, and likewise the stars:
Their own forms too are to be observed.
The one who bestows the fruit of accomplishment
Does not exist apart from perfect union.
Through a moving rising there is moving action;
Through being firmly stable it becomes unmoving.
Through the guide one becomes a guide.
Because of the merit of beings,
And because it is the very cause of the liberation of the gods,
One should meditate on the Enemy of the Gods.
The wheel whose nature is thirty-seven
Is the chief that brings about the accomplishments of yoga.
Its colors, characteristics, and shapes
Should be meditated on in accordance with the action.
The Enemy of the gods, the asuras, and the bestowers
Is Cakrasaṃvara.
Through the union of all the heroes,
Vajraḍāka, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Enemy of the Gods,
The Blessed One, spoke this.

This was the rite of the Enemy of the Gods, the fifth chapter.

Then all the yoginīs
Assembled and asked:
"How is it that the gods and the bestowers
Are givers of suffering to beings?
If the Teacher shows that all is its own nature,
Then, as we ask about virtue and nonvirtue,
Blessed One, what is it that gives
To all beings, the gods and the rest?
In this I have doubt;
O Lord, please make it clear!"
"Listen, goddess, great Māyā,
Of the many, Vārāhī and the rest!
Following each one's own karma,
Gods, bestowers, and beings
Do not exist in my own nature;
They exist from melting into great awakening.
One's own nature, the qualities themselves,
Arise wholly through one's own karma.
Therefore look at virtue and nonvirtue;
It brings about all manner of conceptions.
Therefore the gods and the bestowers,
With the planets, the signs, and the lunar mansions,
Partaking of the fruits of their own actions,
Give; apart from that there is nothing at all."
How does the Teacher become liberated?
He brings delight with this song:
a mi tā bha ja ni tā ma sa mā dhya mā ga mā ga mā dhā ña ma pā ña rī sā mā ña ri ga mā sā ña mā rī gi ma bhū ya te sa rī re sā rī re sa rī rī sā nī sā ña śru ta ni sā mā ña sā nī śī rī bha ga mā dhā mmā dha mmā sā ña pā yā ma pā pā ma da mi te

Through the thorough application of Amitābha
One should accomplish such a liberation.
In the center of the Avadhūtī
One should mark the liberation of consciousness.
All the winds, the life wind and the rest,
Should be dissolved into the interval where they are transformed.
Consciousness, once mingled,
Is the elixir of all the elements;
By drinking it one will attain accomplishment.
From the characteristic of the passage of the senses,
The tongue resting toward the palate:
That is explained as the Avadhūtī.
From the last of the one with digits, the moon,
A stream of nectar will trickle down.
Filling all the channels,
It dissolves into the form of perfect awareness.
In brief, there are five elements;
Other things are not stated here.
The great Indra disk, earth, is burned by fire;
It dissolves fully into the midst of water.
Swiftly melting, it moves on
And abides in the space of wind.
When the mass of water has been dried up,
It dissolves into that very thing and moves on.
The mind should be brought into emptiness,
Emptiness into the element of space.
Space should be made to enter wisdom;
Wisdom's own characteristic is illusion.
The wise one is wisdom, the self of the knowable:
Thus one should meditate on Amitābha.

One should meditate on this wheel of the knowable,
Well adorned with eight spokes.
The Childless One: bri ba mi u ku;
dha si in the fire quarter; ro ma ga in the demon quarter;
In the wind quarter tu ka bi mi;
And in Īśāna's quarter are the signs.
These, in the order of the wheel,
Are likewise to be set out upon it.
The seeds a i u e o,
The five planets beginning with Maṅgala,
Are to be set in the east and the other directions;
The long ones are to be set there too.
aṃ aḥ are to be known as the sun and the moon;
Here the sun and the moon are liberated.
The sun is in Kulīra, the Crab;
Mercury abides here in the Lion.
Finally, whatever remains
Is consumed by its respective rite.
That wheel, adorned with the signs,
Is of benefit to all beings;
Thus it becomes the wheel of the dola.

The lunar mansions, the signs, the times, and the days,
The Rams, Rohiṇī, and the rest,
And the moons and the suns:
The indications of the signs of death
I shall explain in brief.
Fully endowed with the lunar mansions and the hours,
The planets, and likewise the diseases,
By the measure of the dola and the signs,
They are declared to be the workings of birth.
In Kārttika, at the time of a Bhadrā day,
With Mars, and likewise Kṛttikā,
Know that death will come then,
If there is no yogi.
Mṛgaśirṣa and the Head,
With Mercury and the Sun, bring madness;
Through a disease of the throat, on the fourth day,
In Taurus one surely dies.
With the lunar mansions Puṣya and Mṛga,
And Mercury in Gemini, one dies.
On the eighth day of Maghā in Kulīra,
On Venus at dawn, one dies.
With the lunar mansions Pūrvaphalgunī and Maghā,
On the tenth day, with Saturn in Leo,
Know that one dies in the evening.
Even if one were equal to Śakra,
With the Sun in Virgo, one dies of a tumor.
With Viśākhā and the fourteenth day,
With Sagittarius and Jupiter's turn,
Even if one were equal to a buddha,
Know that one dies in Libra.
With Anurādhā, the eleventh day, Citrā,
And Jupiter in Scorpio, one dies.
With Pūrvāṣāḍhā, Uttarāṣāḍhā, and the ninth day,
With Śravaṇa, Sagittarius, and Venus, one dies.
With Śravaṇa, the seventh day, and the Moon,
With Puṣya and Makara, wealth in hand is lost.
With Pūrvabhādrapadā, Uttarabhādrapadā, and the thirteenth day,
With the Moon, Uttaraphalgunī, and Aquarius, one is exhausted.
With Aśvinī, the eighth day, Revatī,
And Saturn in Pisces, one dies.

The reckoning of the births of all beings
Is to be known by the one who speaks of time.
The qualities born from a single seed,
And the second, by the time of birth:
Having understood them as two kinds,
One should undertake the reckoning of its time of birth.
Through the many kinds of former karma,
The wise one is born into that family.
Its result is to be known,
For it follows from the characteristics.
That circle and connection of the signs and the rest
Is the characteristic of all consciousness.
Through the thorough application of Amitābha
One should vividly meditate on the maṇḍala.
In the center is the protector Amitābha;
For the rest, the ḍākinīs and the others.
As consciousness arises clearly,
One should recall that result of karma.
From the union of all bliss,
Then, relying on nonduality,
Endowed with the union of Amitābha,
The Blessed One, the Teacher, spoke this.

This was the rite of Amitābha, the sixth chapter.

Through the thorough application of Vajra Light,
The reality of wisdom shall be explained.
The goddess, having once again made offerings,
Asked about the wisdom of reality.
"To bring sentient beings to certainty,
Whatever the characteristics of death are like,
Just so shall I explain them;
Listen with a one-pointed mind.
The signs of the channels in the body:
How they are clearly distinguished.
There are thirty-two great channels,
And of them the chief is supreme.
Likewise, O goddess, twelve
Have the characteristic of the passage of the vowels;
By the white and the dark fortnights
They are said to be twenty-four.
There the nine essences are supreme;
They are the characteristic of the body's signs.
By merely knowing them,
The time of death stands as if present.
Merely by the spreading forth of the breath,
When the embodied one's transits are twice six,
In the Rohita portion of the morning,
Its sign is to be marked.
At the nose and the region of the eyes,
At the cheeks and the root of the ears,
At the juncture of the tongue and the canine teeth,
At the throat, and likewise the flanks,
At the tent of the heart and the navel,
At the thighs and the calves, and likewise others,
At the private parts and the penis likewise,
At the head and the region of the crown:
At all these junctures
The breath is cut off, cut off.
These are the nine signs of the body;
They show the time of death.
The counts that measure the days,
O joyful one, are of the nature of the wheel of time.
When it rides upon six days,
The one that is declared is adorned;
There it has the course of three years,
And likewise three hundred and sixty.
The measure of the portions in this way
I shall explain in brief.
When the wind flows,
Having fully reached seven days,
Two years are declared,
With months to the number of the seasons,
And eighteen days:
That is declared to be the measure.
When the wind flows,
Fully emitted for eight days,
The count of his life
Is fully explained as two years.
Then death is certain:
His months are to the number of the Vedas,
And the days are declared to be twenty-four;
That is the count of the time of his life.
Goddess, if it flows for nine days,
He lives for two years,
Two months, and days to the measure of the suns:
So it is for the embodied one in this application.
Goddess, with ten days,
He lives for two years.
Likewise, when the channel
Operates for eleven days,
With the path of one year,
Months to the number of nine,
And eighteen days are declared;
This is declared without doubt.
When it flows for twelve days,
It is known as one year,
With months bearing the number of the years,
And days adorned by the seasons.
When for thirteen the channel
Flows, moving on the right,
It is declared to be a year,
With months to the number of the Vedas,
And days to the number of nine:
Thus the characteristic of death is declared.
Goddess, when for twice seven
The wind constantly flows,
The life is declared to be a year;
Its span in months is likewise proclaimed,
And the days are twelve:
That is the measure of his life.
When for fifteen days
The wind likewise flows,
Then one should know that death comes
In one year, without doubt.
When for twice eight
Of the supreme and beneficial days it flows,
Life is ten months,
And days of the supreme number:
As the measure of twenty-four days
I declare it.
When for seventeen days
The wind surely flows,
Then, by that measure,
The months are nine,
And likewise eighteen days;
Of the time of death there is no doubt.
By its moving for eighteen days,
The months are eight,
And the days, too, are to the number of the suns:
This count of time is declared.
When for twenty less one
The rider on scent, the wind, moves,
The days shall be declared:
Life is seven months,
And days to the number of the seasons, it is declared.
By these times, O fair-faced one,
When it moves for twenty,
At six months one parts from life.
When for twenty-one days
The solar channel flows,
One lives fully for five months,
And days to the number of the suns.
When it flows for twenty-two,
Life departs at the Vedas of months;
The days are twenty-four,
And the months are declared to be the Vedas.
Goddess, when for twenty-three
The wind flows continuously,
He lives for four months,
And the days should be recalled as the seasons.
When it flows for twenty-four,
Three months are declared,
And likewise eleven days
Are proclaimed as the time of death.
Because the count of time
Was explained above, earlier, in brief,
Whatever intervening days were not explained,
By the number twelve,
Once worked out through the sequence of the portions,
The intervals are made clear.
So that those of lesser intellect may understand,
These times are fully taught.
The very subtle and concordant times
Of the yoginīs I shall explain.
Through those goddesses one gains conviction,
And the elements come into balance.
When the flesh of the nose is cut off,
And the Upward-Gazing One likewise flows,
His life is to be known
As seven days, without doubt.
When the covering of the eye is cut off,
And likewise the supreme Upward-Facing One,
Mounted fully upon nine portions,
Likewise is cut off when exceeded by five.
Godāvarī, she who goes upward
Along the surface of the cheek,
Is to be known as ten days,
Likewise exceeded by one night.

Resting fully upon the right ear,
Bhadrā rests truly upward;
Mounted upon the eleventh portion,
Likewise it is cut off at eighteen.
Likewise the twelfth, O goddess:
When the tongue bears black lines,
The death of every embodied one
Is to be known within the nights of a lunar month.
The thirteenth, between the teeth:
Likewise the Black-Faced One touches;
By days to the number of a lunar month
Is the passing of creatures.
The channel through which tears constantly flow
Is renowned as the Upward-Gazing One;
Mounted upon the fourteenth vowel,
By half of ten days, without doubt.
When the cage of the heart is crushed,
And that supreme Upward-Looking One,
At three and five, O lovely goddess,
In half a month, without doubt.
Having reached the center of the navel, O goddess,
The sixteenth, lord of the portions, is complete;
When the nails are likewise without blood,
For ten days one is in doubt.
When the seventeenth channel
Dries upon the thigh,
It is declared to be twenty-four;
On the day after that day, without doubt.
The channel of the pair of calves,
Likewise the supreme Upward-Looking One,
Is to be known as the eighteenth;
The days, too, by the count of the suns,
Are taught as twenty less one.
It is death: when the supreme Upward-Facing One
Comes to be cut off evenly,
By this there is no doubt about the span of life.
She renowned in the signs as the Playful One,
Mistress of the supreme upward passage,
Is declared at twelve;
In the days and the hours one is in doubt.
Whatever intervening days are explained,
At twelve itself there is no doubt.
Having worked them out by the sequence of the year,
By the sequence of seventy-two:
Six and thirty, eighteen, and ten.
Just as this proceeds in sequence by months,
So should it be known, O great goddess:
It is divided step by step.
The remainder, when it is summed,
Is to be known as the span of life.
Through these true rites
One should meditate on Vajra Light.
When the life-breath is filled through thorough application,
What need is there to speak of death for a buddha body?
Relying on this thorough application,
It will be explained in brief.
The life-breath that has come by the vajra path
Extends out to the end of twelve threads.
Through the application of recollecting the Buddha,
One comes to partake of the five desirables.
Abiding in the light of the great secret mantra,
One should travel everywhere.
One whose nature is illusion becomes deathless,
Having the form of the gandharva class.
Like grass that fills everything,
It abides in all things.
The elements of all people
It causes to attain wisdom.
The selflessness of phenomena comes to flow,
And it comes to be established in persons.
Both are great Māyā,
Who abides through the power of compassion.
Through the application of the ultimate, the mind
Dies at all the junctures.
The great death, the form of the Buddha,
Bestows bliss upon yogins.
Having taken hold of all the maṇḍalas,
It becomes the nature of all bliss.
Through thorough application of the stages of the cakras,
One truly experiences supreme bliss.
One should meditate on Vajra Light,
Equal to the form of the Blessed One.
The deity has the form of full awakening,
Having the nature of wisdom and skillful means.
From the true union of all the life-breaths,
Abiding supremely undefiled,
The Tathāgata renowned as Kālacakra,
The Blessed One, spoke this.

This was the rite of Vajra Light, the seventh chapter.

Then Vajrapāṇi and the other bodhisattvas,
And the tathāgatas, made offerings,
And, having paid homage,
Addressed him in these words:
"Lord, tell me the means of accomplishing the deities,
And their own mantras:
By which all come under control,
By which thorough application it is done."
"Listen, O king of the vajra body,
To the means of accomplishing the deities and the rest,
By whose true rite
Accomplishment swiftly comes.
Having drawn the maṇḍala,
The meditation on the body is explained here.
There the mātṛkās and the gods,
The messengers, the bhūtas, and likewise the planets,
The great kaṭapūtanas,
And the secret mantras of knowledge shall be stated here.
Abiding within the central channel,
They are accomplished through the purity of existence.
Generated at all times,
They flow down into the maṇḍala.
The mātṛkās are to be accomplished in the great Indra disk,
The gods in the maṇḍala of water,
The bhūtas are to be accomplished in fire,
The planets in the maṇḍala of wind,
The kaṭapūtanas in space.
In the thorough application of the vajra body,
By the portion of the wisdom maṇḍala,
It flows into Avadhūtī.
The last of the five maṇḍalas
Is to be known at its own time.
Having observed that, outwardly
One should lay out the maṇḍala on cloth or the like.
On the eighth and fourteenth days of the dark fortnight
One should accomplish the maṇḍala of the bhūtas.
Then, through reciting a hundred thousand times,
They will grant it, directly present before one.
Brāhmī, and likewise Śivā,
Kaumārī and Vārāhī,
Aindrī herself and Cāmuṇḍī,
Gaṇeśvarī and She of the Fire Seat.
In the second section, Indra,
Yama, Varuṇa, Kubera,
The lord of the pretas and the one with a human seat,
Nairṛti, and likewise Vāyu.
To be placed in the third section:
The sun, the moon, Mars,
Mercury, Jupiter, Venus,
Saturn, and Rāhu.
In the fourth, Brahmā, likewise Viṣṇu,
Rudra and Kumāra are arrayed all around.
Earth and water, fire and wind,
The elements of the great elements,
The Ḍākinī, the Lamp, the Suckling One,
And Kamboji, likewise the all-pervading lord.
The bhūtas, in the form of bhūtas,
Are always to be accomplished according to the rite.
Through the application of the great maṇḍala,
One should draw the outer maṇḍala all around.
With three encircling lines and a center set within,
The one who keeps the observance should make it beautiful.
An eight-petaled lotus should be drawn,
Its hub the domain of the profound.
In its center, the vajra body,
With one face and four arms,
A face slightly baring its fangs,
A laughing face, yet wrathful,
Blue in color, greatly fierce,
Adorned with a garland of skulls,
Dancing upon a sun, abiding upon a corpse,
Terrifying the Thirty gods,
Wearing a garment of tiger skin,
Of excellent form, with three eyes,
Holding a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
Adorned with a ḍamaru and a curved knife.
In the east, the south, the west,
And the north, in due order,
Are the Ḍākinī, Kamboji herself,
The Suckling One, and the Lamp Lady.

Thus the meaning of the vajra body
I shall state in brief.
At the navel it takes the form of the vajra.
Of the messenger who travels on six feet,
At the heart, the secret place, and the throat,
That very one is to be set out again.
Then, while holding the life-breath,
The mind is in meditative concentration, and not by any other means.
Divine substances and rays of light come to be generated;
The deities of the maṇḍala are born from one's own body.
Through the application of the beginner,
They come to be emanated into the ten directions.
The goddess arisen from the syllable hrīḥ
Is Vārāhī, with four arms,
Holding a bow and likewise an arrow,
A victory banner and a skull cup;
Blue in color, greatly fierce,
Boar-faced and terrifying.
Drawn with the syllable pheṃ is Śivā,
Red in color, of great radiance,
Holding a skull cup and a hook,
A khaṭvāṅga and likewise a ḍamaru.
By the syllable hrīḥ is Kaumārī,
Yellow in color, with four arms,
Holding a trident and a triple banner,
And likewise a bow and an arrow.
By the syllable hoḥ is Cāmuṇḍī,
Black in color, with four arms,
Holding a khaṭvāṅga and a skull cup,
A ḍamaru and likewise a vessel of liquor.
From the syllable oṃ is Brāhmī,
White in color, with four arms,
Holding a lotus and a skull cup,
And likewise, supreme, a bow and an arrow.
From the syllable gaṃ is Gaṇeśvarī,
Green in color, with four arms,
Holding a trident and a skull cup,
A rosary and a sweetmeat.
From the syllable bi is Vaiṣṇavī,
Blue in color, with four arms,
Holding a wheel and a skull cup,
And likewise, supreme, a noose and a sword.
Arisen from the syllable ī is Aindrī,
Yellow in color, with four arms,
Holding a vajra, a short spear and a skull cup,
And likewise a noose with the threatening gesture.
All of them are blazing, with hair standing upright,
Clad in various ornaments,
Riding upon corpses, with three eyes,
A tiger skin wound about the waist.
At the eastern gate, Brahmā should be drawn,
White in color, with four arms,
Holding a skull cup and a rosary,
And likewise a water flask and a lotus.
In the north, likewise, Viṣṇu,
Black in color, with four arms,
Holding a sword and likewise a wheel,
And likewise a skull cup and a club.
In the south, Rudra should be drawn,
Smoke-colored, with four arms,
Holding a trident and likewise a skull cup,
And likewise holding a noose and a garland.
In the west, Kumāra should be drawn,
Yellow in color, with four arms,
A hero holding a short spear and a club,
And likewise holding a skull cup and a rosary.
Īśāna, riding upon a bull,
White in color, with four arms,
Holds a skull cup and a trident;
His other hands are joined in homage.
Indra, riding upon an elephant,
Yellow in color, with four arms,
Holds a vajra and a skull cup,
And, supreme, makes the gesture of joined palms.
Agni, riding upon a ram,
Red in color, with four arms,
Holds a fire hearth and a skull cup,
And, terrified, likewise joins his palms.
Yama, riding upon a buffalo,
Black in color, with four arms,
Holds a club and likewise a skull cup,
And, utterly terrified, holds his palms joined.
Nairṛti, riding upon a man,
Blue-gray in color, with four arms,
Holds a sword and a skull cup;
Utterly terrified, supreme, his palms are joined.
Varuṇa, riding upon a makara,
White in color, with four arms,
Holds a noose and likewise a skull cup,
And likewise his other hands are joined.
Vāyu, riding upon a deer,
Smoke-colored, with four arms,
Holds a banner and a skull cup,
And likewise, supreme, holds his palms joined.
Kubera rides upon a treasure,
Yellow in color, with four arms,
Holding a jewel and a skull cup,
And likewise his other hands are joined.
All the constellations and planets
Should be meditated on as abiding in their own maṇḍalas.
Arisen from their own pledges,
They have miraculous powers and great strength.
The mātṛkās, the mothers of all,
The bhūtas, and likewise the vidyās and the others:
Through the performance of such a rite,
Each one should therefore be meditated on.
In places such as charnel grounds,
Together with the eaters of the various pledge substances,
For the enjoyment of the yogin, ever joyful,
One should truly undertake the activity of the messengers.
By the armor of the moon vajra,
It is as when Vajrasattva arises.
Urged on by the king of mantras,
All of them act like servants.
In the twenty-four sacred sites and the rest,
They should therefore be worshiped in sequence.
Then scents and the rest should be offered,
And the recitation of the mantras should also be done:
In the secret mantra that states one's own name,
oṃ hūṃ phaṭ and the rest are inserted.
Having exchanged the Blessed One in the center,
There one should accomplish everything.
Then, within the other exchange,
The Blessed One has another form:
A blazing triple banner,
A vajra, and a skull cup, supreme, with dust,
Holding a bow and an arrow,
Brandished by the right and left hands.
The rest has the earlier form;
The body, faces, and so forth are as before.
Surely through the yoga of the central channel
One will succeed; of this there is no doubt.
The lord of the vajra of body, speech, and mind,
Having the nature of every body,
Abiding within every form,
The Blessed One in form spoke this.

This was the rite of the vajra body, the eighth chapter.

Then here the yoginī, having awakened the King of Bliss,
Asked with these words:
"O great intelligence, what is time?"
"O yoginīs, you yourselves are time,
Born through the sprouting one.
The great mind, the seed of the manifold,
Is explained through the application of the sprouting one.
The planets, the constellations, and the lunar dates
Are declared to be the time of the mind.
The formless count is its time,
The cause of the bliss of yogins.
As merely the time of the world
It was spoken of by the sages, the supreme among beings.
Then, meditating on the sprouting one,
The seed is the characteristic of the seed.
The sprout has the form of the mind;
The sprouting one arises from the mind.
In whichever constellation, sign of the zodiac, planet, and so forth
The sprout is born,
By that the sprouting one is to be determined.
Nature is to be purified as nature.
All that is virtuous or nonvirtuous,
The lunar dates, the constellations, the rising of the planets:
In all these times,
Meditate on all as born with the nature of bliss.
ro kṛ ma brings gain.
ma bha ma is to be avoided, for it destroys through disease.
ku sva ā is in the form of the maker of time;
That lunar date is to be wholly avoided.
mo bu mo is excellent and auspicious.
ra bu ā brings destruction.
bhu bu bha is in the form of time.
bha pū gu brings gain.
mi gu dha exhausts wealth and the like.
tra gu a has the form of time.
bṛ śu pū brings about virtuous things.
bhi śu pū is disease and destruction.
bhu bha śu has the fangs of Yama.
kū ma kri attains fame and merit.
byā ma ā brings torment.
pū ra ma has the time of death.

In the one who has the seat of Agni, Mikira;
Again, in Yama, Raṇḍā is to be avoided;
In the rākṣasa, the fifteenth;
And Taurus is to be avoided in Varuṇa.
In Prabhañjana (the wind), Distraction;
In the Treasure, likewise, the Dreaming One;
In the Three-Eyed One (Śiva), Bhadrā;
And likewise in the east, the day of the bhūtas.
The lunar dates of the moon, the sun, and the rest:
Their turns shall be fully explained.
Bhadrā, and likewise the Widow,
And Distraction abide on the day of the bhūtas;
They are to be known as the movement
Of the portion of the sun for all people.
Taurus and Mikira,
The Dreaming One, and likewise the fifteenth,
Are renowned as the movement of the moon;
These sequences are thus proclaimed.
In the sun it abides truly as fire;
In emptiness, in the place of the wind.
Thus, abiding in the center,
The tormentor abides truly in the directions.
The line arising from the moon and the sun,
By looking evenly, moves evenly.
Having observed the peaceful and the harmful,
The wise one therefore avoids it.
By failing to watch for its meeting with the fang,
One is truly brought to death.
The speaking of time, in brief,
Is renowned as the movement of Rāhu.
Through the rite of the white fortnight and the other,
One of consummate intelligence comes to know it.
Whatever deluded one speaks
In discord with the constellations and the rest
Has not come to understand this karma
As what people have done themselves.
By that karma the times
Come to arise without doubt.
As long as the aggregation of the body lasts,
So long does time operate.
Yogins experience it,
And come to attain the state of perfect awakening.
That is renowned as the sprouting one,
Red in color, of great light.
The sprouting one, in the form of a sprout,
Should be meditated on as abiding at the root of the navel.
Having entered within a blazing wheel,
That form is the form of divine substance.
Through meditating on the sprout of wisdom,
All karmas will no longer be seen.
From the great union whose nature is all,
Destroying former karma,
Abiding always within the causes,
The Vajra Cause spoke this.

This was the rite of the vajra sprouting one, the ninth chapter.

Then, having performed the vajra dance,
The yoginī asked this:
"How, O Teacher, great being,
Does one come forth bound up with karma?
When former karma has deteriorated,
When the continuum has deteriorated, there is no coming.
About this I have doubt."
With compassion, pleased and gentle, he declared:
"The arising of the great being as well,
As the former buddhas taught it,
I shall explain in brief;
Listen with a single mind.
Through the application of Vajrajaṭila,
It will be accomplished within karma.
Through the application of each one,
It abides as self-arisen.
Abiding in exceeding faith,
One should gather all the faculties.
By its locks the world is always
Bound by the name of locks.
It is what distinguishes beyond all joys;
You will attain the vajra body.
Therefore all habitual tendencies
And virtuous karma come to be exhausted.
The arising of that great being
Is attained by the single path.
From the nonconceptual comes conception;
By conception it becomes nonconceptual.
Having obtained the sound of the bell,
I shall explain their characteristics.
Nine-pronged and single-pointed,
Five-pronged and three-pointed:
Thus and in other ways are the great bells,
Up to a thousand points.
By the thing that gives voice to sound,
Free of elaboration and without letters,
The yogin is the ocean of ḍākinīs;
From its sound true conception arose.
By that very sound, the nature of nonconceptuality
Surely enters into whatever places there are.
The pronged one explained before is the vajra;
Wherever the mind radiates,
Having a body of the class of Dharma and emanation,
There it should be restored.
The characteristic of the outer bell,
As the Hero taught it, I shall explain.
Its round body, always in three parts,
Should be truly adorned with deities.
The lord of the deities of each one,
Just as in the maṇḍala of meditative absorption,
Through the yogins' surpassing devotion,
Likewise all are to be made as faces.
The seats of those goddesses:
On that bell one should make lotuses and the like.
Just as they are adorned,
So is the girdle of the bell.
The deities at the gates and the corners
Are to be made in the eight directions.
Just as the emblems of the yogin are to be shown,
From their mouths the points emerge.
On the vase and the rest, and on the two faces as well,
Above are the marks of their own families.
Such is the great bell;
Its measure is explained elsewhere.
That has an inner form;
Therefore the sound is to be indicated.
The form of the bindu is the great all-pervading lord;
The vajra, too, should be made equal to it.
Thus, through the application of the locks,
All sentient beings should be accomplished.
The one with locks is renowned as the bell;
The vajra is called the vajra.
With oneself as Vajrajaṭila,
One should meditate in the maṇḍala as well.
The yoginīs and all the ḍākinīs
Should then be made to enter within.
From the full union of all dualities
Vajrajaṭila arose;
Relying truly on vajra and bell,
The Blessed One, the Hero, spoke this.

This was the rite of Vajrajaṭila, the tenth chapter.

Then the great application of another,
Through the thorough application of the great hero,
I shall explain directly;
All yoginīs, listen!
In the body of every embodied one
There abide a thousand vital points.
The placing of letters upon them:
From ka and the rest, likewise up to ha at the end,
The vowel letters, the guṇa vowels and the rest,
The letters upon each element.
Again, by the twelve mother letters,
The learned should divide them.
Caṇḍālī, whose nature is a thousand,
Abides blazing at all the junctures.
From the abode of bliss of all the elements,
One comes to attain the enjoyment of the hero.
Drawn in by those rays of light,
The great family of the bodhisattvas
Is set by selflessness as the self,
Within the nondual source of phenomena.
Thus the thousand tongues of flame
Abide in the state of the great hero.
It is close to all medicines;
This is taught as the hidden elixir.

The lord of the letters of the outer substances as well
Is to be known as its very name.
The first names of the groups,
Flowers, fruits, and the rest, number in the thousands.
With them one should accomplish the elixir;
Otherwise it will be without fruit.
In due order of class and the rest,
All of them, ka and the rest, are great medicines.
In another tantra that speaks of action,
There it should be clearly known for certain.
Having meditated on the Primordial Protector,
One is to obtain the signs.
Or else, whoever sees
The elixir of accomplished yogins,
By merely seeing it
Will attain the unsurpassed accomplishment.
From full union with the groups in their kinds,
In the form of the junctures of nectar,
Having the nature of accomplished medicines,
The Blessed One spoke this.

This was the rite of the Great Hero, the eleventh chapter.

Then the yoginīs and heroines,
Having made offerings together, said:
"O Teacher, great vajra,
Arisen within the three cities, what are you?
In what form have you come?
Where does the emanation go?
And at the time of listening to the Dharma,
By what is it made firm?
Gods, asuras, and humans:
By what speech do they go and come?
The characteristic of the manifest juncture,
Please explain it as before."
"The characteristic of the manifest juncture
I shall explain in due order; listen.
The great meaning, exceedingly profound,
Unknown to the buddhas and the world,
Which is the self-appearance of sentient beings,
I shall surely explain in brief.
The light of the vajra mind, as something other,
Not abiding in the elements and the signs,
Arises in the three cities
Of pure body, speech, and mind.
Carried as a former entity, it comes;
Surely it goes to where karma is slight.
Or else, at all times,
It is seen like a reflection in a mirror.
Having first obtained the hearing,
The mind too is in its abode.
By meditative absorptions such as the vajra,
The gods and the others are bewildered.
By that, through the thorough application
Of Vajrahūṃkāra, it always emanates.
At the tip of the vajra it emanates the world,
Radiating hooks of light rays;
By the sound of the syllable hūṃ
The Teacher made the supreme one who holds the wheel.
In the many-storied house of the source of phenomena,
On the seat of the twenty coemergent ones,
He causes them to hear through the conduct of speech;
Having gone there, they dissolve into it.
Thus the master, the lord of yogins,
The all-pervading lord, should be well known.
Vajra and the like are his very names;
I shall explain his characteristics.
His nature is benefit to all sentient beings;
He bestows fearlessness;
He knows the application of mantra and tantra,
The applications, the crafts, the arts, and the rest;
His speech is harmonious, and he is in harmony with all
As with an only son;
He always delights in giving and the rest;
He is intent on supreme meditative concentration and absorption;
He does just as he has said;
His speech is harmless and true;
He has loving care for all sentient beings,
And has perfected leading them onto the path;
He is equal to the qualities of the omniscient;
He has gone beyond all treatises;
He is the father and mother of the protectorless;
He is the maker of the signs of the mudrās.
Filled with these and other qualities,
In this very birth he is a tathāgata.
In the center of the wheel of yoginīs,
Vajrahūṃkāra is meditated upon.
The maṇḍala is that of the awakening mind;
The King of Bliss is the yoginī.
In sounds is the wisdom of sound;
Vajrahūṃkāra is the all-pervading lord.
The remainder of what has transferred becomes wisdom;
It is the cause of the Primordial Protector.
The experience of the mind
Therefore has a subtle nature;
It is renowned as the element of touch.
By that very thing it is carried and comes.
In the ocean of ḍākinīs, always and surely,
The three worlds are made its nature.
Thus the application is supreme and foremost;
Others are like woodworms in straw.
Not knowing its course,
The childish and ignorant are bound.
The speaker of the ultimate bliss,
The one to be taught and the Teacher, Vajradhara,
The teacher of the coemergent form,
The Blessed One, Wisdom, spoke this."

This was the rite of Vajrahūṃkāra, the twelfth chapter.

"Then I shall explain the application without thought of accomplishment
By which the practitioner is accomplished.
Through the thorough application of the Exceedingly Excellent One,
I shall explain the conduct and the observances.
His conduct becomes spontaneously accomplished;
Those who desire accomplishments should know it.
The nature of outer nondual union:
That conduct is highly praised.
For the good mind that is the nature of all,
One should rely truly on the city of the senses.
One should practice in those towns,
And display the observance with the mind.
In the forms of a sister and a peaceful one,
One should go oneself to wherever that is.
Oneself, having the nature of form,
Will be truly seen in a mirror.
Relying on the melting mind,
Dissolve the body into the body.
Having bound oneself by oneself in the vow,
It is renowned as the Great Seal.
The five mudrās and the rest abide on the body;
One holds a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga;
One eats the five nectars and the rest;
Together with a playful yoginī,
One meditates with a mind of passion;
Even if unwilling, one therefore goes.
By the entities of grasped and grasper
This mind is not stained.
With a mind of fearless aspect,
One goes at night to charnel grounds and the like.
Where one practices on the four grounds,
They are to be known as the eight consciousnesses.
The fivefold conduct and observance:
He sports in Sukhāvatī.
Relying fully on the first application,
Afterward it is fully explained by the mind.
Through the equal union of all the life-breaths,
Holding the observance that purifies the wisdom
Of the bliss of the city of the senses,
The Blessed One spoke this."

This was the rite of the Exceedingly Excellent One, the thirteenth chapter.

"Then I shall explain the distinctions
Of the classes of people; listen.
Through the thorough application of Vajrabhadra,
That class here comes to be experienced.
There, first, the fifth and the seventh,
The eighth, the thirteenth, the fifteenth,
The seventeenth and the twenty-second,
And likewise, supreme, the twenty-seventh:
These, beginning with Aśvinī, are to be known;
They are to be remembered as the class of gods.
The fourth and the three eastern ones,
The second and likewise the sixth,
And likewise the three northern ones:
They are to be remembered as the class of humans.
The third and the tenth,
The ninth and the twenty-third,
The fourteenth and the sixteenth,
The eighteenth and the nineteenth,
And the renowned twenty-fourth:
They are to be remembered as the class of rākṣasas.
Of the class of the goat, the third and the eighth;
The nāgas are the fourth and the fifth.

The sixth and the twenty-ninth
Are said to be of the nature of the dog.
The tenth and the eleventh are the rat,
And likewise the cat is the ninth;
The fourteenth and the sixteenth
Are always declared to be the same.
The twelfth and the twenty-fifth
Obtain the class of the bull.
The first and the last are the donkey;
These are renowned as classes.
The thirteenth and the fifteenth
Are named the buffalo.
The first and the twenty-sixth
Are declared to be of the class of the horse.
The twentieth and the twenty-second
Are declared to be what is called the monkey.
The second, and likewise the last,
Go to the class of the elephant.
The twenty-third and the twenty-fourth
Are the human, which is great in strength.
The twenty-first is the mongoose.
This has been taught in brief.

The lunar mansions that bring about strength and the self
I shall explain: listen now!
For humans, life is the lunar mansion;
It is not obtained in any other way.
When the channel of time reaches the star,
The one who holds it will surely die.
Aries, Libra, Karkaṭa (Cancer),
And Makara are to be remembered as the lofty ones.
Kīṭa (Scorpio), Aquarius, Leo and Taurus
Are taught to be for stable activities.
Pisces and Gemini, and likewise here
Sagittarius and Virgo, belong to the ones that lead.
The Guru (Jupiter), the Sun, and the Earth-born (Mars) are male;
The Abandoner of Evil, Rāhu and the Moon are female;
Mercury and the Auspicious One are neuter:
Such are whatever planets there are in the circle of the sky.
Thus are the classes; but otherwise
The sons of awakening do not change.
That is taught to be Vajrabhadra.
Through the mind in the form of perfect union,
The mind that has reached the left channel
Is joined in union at the end of the central channel.
The seeds of awakening descend;
This is the family of the awakening minds.
Through the mind that has reached compassion,
The sun of karma trickles down.
Ever endowed with perfect giving,
There is no class, nor anyone who has a class.
Merely as play within existence,
A body in the form of meditative concentration appears.
Just as whatever form is seen
In the domain of a mirror does not exist,
So the bodhisattvas
Have forms of the union of meditative concentration.
The mind that melts within it:
There is nothing else equal to it.
Surely, in the center of the maṇḍalas,
One should meditate on Vajrabhadra.
By the rite of wisdom and skillful means,
Preceded by the worship of secret mantra,
He whose nature is the portions of time and non-time,
Who is not a non-entity, abides as the agent.
The great one whose nature is the form of awakening,
The Blessed One, the Being, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Vajrabhadra, the fourteenth chapter.

Then I shall explain the secret union
By which one is born as the embodiment of all bliss.
Through the union of the ultimate,
One should fully enter into suchness.
Through the yoga of Mahābhairava (the Great Terrifier),
One comes forth oneself as the garland of mantras.
The forty-eight
Are taught upon the grounds.
A wheel-shaped diagram drawn in a hexagonal shape
Is to be made in the center of the ground.
Everywhere one should write
The forty-eight words of secret mantra.
Those forms of the mantra
Have life in the form of letters.
Having abandoned the fine portions of the letters,
They abide in the central channel.
Having made them dissolve completely into emptiness,
One should look below and above.
From the tips of the feet up to the head,
So far does the lord of yogins act.
When one has fully entered all the elements,
What need is there to speak of one whose conduct is of the mind?
Thus pressing the secret upward
Has the purpose of the Great Hero.
One who has fully entered the central channel
Abandons striking, accomplishing and the rest.
By holding the scattering wind,
The first sun becomes inexhaustible.
The mind that has risen on the vajra path:
This is because of the sequence of transmission.
Thus in this illusory mantra recitation,
The root mantra and the others, set in squares,
The squares of all the heroes,
Are to be laid out on the mantra wheel.
The heroes of the lords of samādhi,
The twelve as well, arrive in the wheel.
Whichever it is, preceded by its name,
One should write it in its respective place.
All the mantras of others
Will arise in these, here in this maṇḍala.
All the recitations of body and the rest,
Likewise those with recitations of speech and the rest,
All the recitations of mind and the rest,
And likewise those with recitations of wisdom and the rest:
Urged on by the king of mantras,
The heroes then arise.
In the centers, one should meditate on Mahābhairava
As having the form of fire.
By the power of the wisdom family,
One should put to use all those hosts of bhūtas.
The letters for pacifying, enriching, subjugating,
Wrathful rites and paralyzing:
Meditating on forms that accord with the rite,
One should set out the blazing forms and the rest.
Cheating death, by that very means
One comes to be endowed with yoga.
Nāda is to be united with nāda,
And bindu is to be dissolved into bindu.
Thus, by the action of the rite,
Having recited the secret mantra, one should drive in the ritual dagger.
These, through the chief of mantras,
Are the forty-eight lines,
With the syllables oṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ at the end,
In due order, for all of these.

namaḥ śrī vajraḍāka mahāvīra vīrīśvarāya tribhuvaṇakampakampāvaṇāya daṃṣṭrotkaṭabhīṣaṇabherebāya śatasahasranetrāya biṅyutajihvāya kaṃkālamālābharaṇogrāya koṭilakṣabūjāya jvālābalīḍhalagnāya parśupāśodyataśūlāya kalpāṃtaṅhūmrūgrapuṣāya bikaṭamukhahāhārabāya mātṛgaṇaparipūritamantrāya sahajasundharivallabhakarāya hūṃ hūṃ kāroccharaṇamukhāya mahāśmaśānapriyāya sarvabhūtasaṃtrāsanakarāya mahākalpāntāgnitejāya jaṭākalāpamukuṭāya khaṭvāṃgakapālaśūlabhinnadhāriṇe mahāsamudramekhalāya byāghracarmāṃbharadhāriṇe betālasaṃghamardhaṇakarāya mahābighnanibāriṇe śatrunisuṃbhanakarāya trisāhāsramahāsahasrekaromābalāntabartiṇe śatasahasramukhāya jñānasāgaraprabhāya mahāpiśitarudhirabasāhāriṇe sarvamārabaladhvaṃsine śriṣṭisaṃhārakārakāya sarvanāgānāṃ ṣoṣaṇastaṃbhanabidhrābaṇāya antramālādhārine sumerukaṃpitanādāya krodhabighrahadhāraṇāya mahākāśatilakātmane buddhakoṭisahasrakṣaṇāniṣbādanāya jñānāmṛtabarṣapaṇāya sarvakarmaprabarttanāyane baṃdanakarāya sarvabikalpabhaṃjaṇakarāya muṃḍābalilagnaśarīrāya parakṛtayaṃtramaṃtranāśanāya mahārakṣākarāya duṣṭānāṃ bidālane dharmodhayakarāya krodhasphārasphāraṇāya iṃdrajālasarvasatvātmane grastabādaprabādamahāmate

Whatever secret mantras there are, such as these,
Are exceedingly many and vast.
Their names, of many and various kinds,
Are to be known through the names of beings.
Through the union of all the mantras,
The chief of all the tathāgatas
Who dwell throughout the three realms,
The Blessed One, the Mantra Holder, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Mahābhairava, the fifteenth chapter.

Then I shall explain the rite of the higher knowledges
Of all the faculties, the eye and the rest.
Through the perfect union of Virūpākṣa,
One should fully accomplish the three vajras.
Where the awakening mind abides,
There one should surely look with the eye.
The supreme undefiled state of the yogins,
The mind, is to be known as the eye.
Through the perfect union of the channels,
One should bring down the wind in each.
At the time when it arises in any place,
At that very time it dissolves and departs.
That belongs to the eyes of higher knowledge;
Through this mind one will go.
Forms that have entered among those who are born and move about,
And sounds, one will hear with the mind.
The rite of going and coming
Is to be indicated through the divine substance as smell.
Through the perfect union of the divine substance as taste,
The tongue, together with joy,
Relying on the higher knowledge of the tongue,
Gives birth to the messenger of the central channel.
Through the aspect of bodily touch,
One should accomplish that supreme undefiled state.
For the recollection of former habitual tendencies,
That very one comes to be endowed with yoga.
Having made day and night equal,
With sixty water-hours,
And a full sixty water-weights,
One should also apply it to the ghaṭikā.
Twelve finger-widths in breadth
And eight finger-widths in height,
With a measure of sixty guñjā,
Is the probe of twelve finger-widths.
The instant, the moment, the brief while,
The kāṣṭhā, the closing and opening of the eyes,
And the snap of the fingers are to be known as included,
Through the higher knowledge of the movement of the breath.
By way of the six perfections,
The sphere of reality (dharmadhātu) is to be indicated.
One should meditate on Virūpākṣa,
The dispeller of time and non-time.
What is to be indicated and its characteristic
Are made within the maṇḍala of the yoginī.
This mind has various forms;
It is the dance traversed by living beings.
It will attain the nature of selflessness.
It abides through the meeting of the knots.
Its very being comes to act as form,
Beginning with the supreme and ending with separation.
That which abides in the source of phenomena
Is to be known as Vajraḍāka.
Always relying on the path of the downward-clearing wind,
And again within each one,
Through the five winds, the life wind and the rest,
The eyes are to be known as five.
The seed that does not drip drips,
Endowed with union with the Wisdom Ḍākinī.
The five seeds of the bindu
Are the five, the agent of higher knowledge and the rest.
Such a supreme yoga, fully complete,
Is the supreme even among all yogas.
Every gnosis of entities without exception
Has been abandoned completely and far away.
From the union of all within the body,
The coemergent eye enters.
He who makes all entities free of entity,
The Blessed One, the Eye, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Virūpākṣa, the sixteenth chapter.

Then the Goddess paid homage,
And likewise made great offerings,
And requested, "May the Great Lord please teach
The fire offering of Mahābala."
"I shall explain the inner fire offering of the self
That the wise perform continually: listen!
Through the perfect union of Mahābala,
With all the elements as the filling and the pouring,
Bliss is truly attained as the hearth.
Sun and moon are the filling ladle and the pouring ladle.
The master is nonduality; into that
One burns, in a fitting form.
The various ḍākinīs are this fire,
Whose defining mark is the nature of secret mantra.
The kindling sticks are truly renowned as the channels.
Because the rice is born from śāli grain,
The fat is always the curd-food.
Here the butter is the awakening mind.
The blood is to be known as honey.
The winds, the life wind and the rest, are the dūrvā grass.
Urine and feces, likewise,
Are sandalwood and musk.
Flowers are always renowned as blood.
Spittle and phlegm are the rice soup.
The firewood is the teeth, nails and bones.
In brief, the skin is the cloth.
In the center of the fire hearth,
The hair of the head and body become the deities.
The various other subtle elements
Are the various grains: burn them in it.
The ḍākinīs brought gradually to completion
Are to be known as the full oblation.
All the senses dance,
And their habitual tendencies sing songs.

The various great sounds are the music;
Compassion and the rest are the feast.
One should meditate on Mahābala,
Lord of all the life breaths.
The fire offering for all rites:
Through the delight it brings, accomplishment arises.
In all the elements of the supreme one of the world,
The mind truly sports there.
At whatever time bliss has arisen,
At that time it firmly abides.
By seeing the fire offering as one's own self,
One censures and one favors.
Worldly and transcendent wisdoms:
These are to be attained in the central channel.
Anger throughout all the quarters
The great mind will slay.
The buddhas emanated by conceptual thought,
Arising from that, are to be eaten.
By destroying the indicated and the indicator,
The two are fit to be known.
The defining mark whose nature is illusion
Will be made to dissolve into it.
From the perfect union of all the elements
Comes wisdom whose mark is that the two cannot be made.
The Blessed One, the Hearth,
Who always dwells in the form of fire, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Mahābala, the seventeenth chapter.

Then I shall explain, just as it is,
The union of vajra and lotus: listen!
Through the perfect union of Ratnavajra
Comes the accomplishment that possesses Avadhūtī.
The yoginī truly sports;
She abides in the experience of the ultimate.
Liberation through union with her
Comes about through the causes of nonduality.
While it is being experienced in the body,
It is the light of awakening even for the childish.
The seed, the letter ha joined with the sixth,
Is to be set upon the vajra jewel.
It descends into the channel of Avadhūtī,
And the Upward-Facing One reaches the forehead.
At the time of guarding, the intelligent one
Should hold the vajra with his own hand.
For one who plays with the awakening mind,
Old age and death are far away.
By stepping beyond the faults of existence,
The three realms of existence become his very nature.
Through the perfect union of the action seal,
One should practice each and every day.
Whatever one sees at that time,
One should descend upon all things.
The very nature of the way of great passion
Will always arise in oneself.
For the sake of the cause of the yoga tantras,
The one to be taught becomes the teacher.
Through the yoginī tantras,
The benefit of beings is surely accomplished.
By the yoga of the four bodies,
I shall not speak what is untrue.
Having the nature of Ratnavajra,
The wisdom born of union travels upward.
One should make her the chief of the messengers,
And delight her with the fire of passion.
The awakening mind is always to be taken
With the tongue, not with the hand.
The mark of the coemergent having been transferred,
One should hold it with the garuḍa mudrā itself.
It is given into the very mouth of the consort,
And then it is to be drunk.
With all one's strength one should eat,
With diligence, what is grown from śāli and the rest.
All fire offerings, too, through that very thing:
Whatever one performs, subjugation and the rest,
Is to be done in its respective skull cup,
And then the seed is to be set.
With those substances one should perform the averting,
The torma offering, and the protection.
Merely by binding with it,
A dhāraṇī like that will enter.
A pill or a thread:
This the Hero himself proclaimed.
Meditated upon in the center of the bhaga,
That which has Ratnavajra is medicine.
It belongs to all the elements of the maṇḍala;
The averting has been fully taught.
For whatever rite it may be,
The mantra bears its respective name.
From the complete union of all the seeds,
These are always medicines.
He who always binds
The mudrā of the Blessed One proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Ratnavajra, the eighteenth chapter.

Then I shall explain the characteristics of the ḍākinīs
Who abide at the end of saṃsāra.
Through the perfect union of Hayagrīva,
One should accomplish the characteristics of the messenger.
The letter ha is in the center of the womb;
The letter ya abides on the head;
The letter bhyi is in the lotus of the central channel;
The letter ba is at the heart.
Holding to the great yoga,
One becomes like Sarasvatī.
It steals away all delusion,
Because the scriptures will follow after.
In the throat, the verses always
Abide purely in the throat.
Abiding in this living form of the horse
Is the remaining yoga.
Going through its union,
First it is upon the seat of the sun.
That sun is Vajrasattva;
It will circle through the three realms.
Having adorned the sky with the sun,
One will go with the mind of awakening.
One realizes the whole circle of the directions,
The supreme lotus of the yoginī.
For the delusion of all sentient beings,
And for all who see the path,
That very sun is ever illuminating,
And steals away the fierce darkness of night.
Arrived at the center of the yoginīs' circle,
The Lord of Horses is meditated upon.
That by which one goes to buddhahood,
The limbs of the sādhana, will dawn.
The obstructors will be utterly destroyed;
Clouds and the like will be dispersed.
It also steals away the evil of all.
Its defining mark is the dawning of one's own joy.
At the time of the sacrificial offering, in the world,
Everything will be accomplished in an instant.
By holding the seven elements,
Accomplishment will come after that day.
These selfless ones are those goddesses;
They sport as the limbs of a single one.
One should not perform mantra recitation,
Nor likewise the fire offering, the āga, and the rest.
One should realize it in whatever way is blissful,
And one will attain the state of Vajradhara.
By passion, the door of passion;
One burned by fire is to be treated with heat.
Likewise, relying on passion through passion,
One is not bound.
That itself is the means; the action of the mind
Is to be made to bear the mark of virtue.
It is not something done inwardly:
With a devoted mind one abides in the central channel.
The wisdom of those who have outer wisdom:
That itself is the buddhas, Vajradhara and the rest,
From which beings, the world and the rest arise.
By abandoning these, there is no buddha.
Between the tīrthikas (non-Buddhists) and the Buddhists
There is no distinction at all.
All the scriptures contradict one another;
The logicians are full of scorn.
Since all words are consciousness,
For that reason those who have wisdom know.
From the union of all the ḍākinīs,
He dwells throughout the skies.
The Blessed One, who always dwells
As worldly within the world, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Hayagrīva, the nineteenth chapter.

Then I shall explain the qualities of Prabhañjana:
Listen, you practitioners!
Through the union of Ākāśagarbha,
This is the defining mark of the vajra wind.
In the formations and destructions of the world,
Whatever is empty, that is the living being.
The elements are to be known to be like that;
It abides truly in gentle union.
Renowned as being in the form of the moon,
It drips, born from its own nature.
The wisdom of the remaining yoga as well
Is spoken of as Prabhañjana.
At all times it is without movement;
Movement is not seen here.
That is the wind, the self of all,
That fully enters all thoughts.
Through applying dolāhāra and the rest,
It is the giver of liberation to yogins.
That is to be known as sound and bindu,
As luminosity and the three appearances.
Therefore Ākāśagarbha too
Is to be known through the unions of the ḍākinīs.
Through the union of the inner central channel,
That life is highly praised.
It is also to be known as the basis of all,
And it is also taught as the mirror.
The dharma body and the rest are also that,
And so too are the destinies, hell and the rest.
The abode of the buddhas and the awakening minds
Is the vajra maṇḍala.
When, on the path of the moon, the space
Of the yogins transfers without remainder,
Its time becomes beyond all designation;
It cannot be traversed by childish yogins.
Of all the elements of space,
It is taught to be their essence.
Those are the two, wisdom and skillful means;
The states, too, are just two.
The very radiating forth of existence
Is to be joined in the center of the faculties.
The infinite buddhas and worlds
Will play within the earth.
Thus suchness with aspects
And the state without aspects abide.
The continua of mind and consciousness,
The two themselves, become nondual.
It is accomplished by those whose view is the Middle Way;
In that wisdom this is complete.
If there is attachment to the Buddha,
And likewise to the supreme Dharma and Saṅgha;
If one desires the great way, that dharma
Of the learned tīrthikas (non-Buddhists);
If it is the yoga of wisdom and means,
Whose nature is emptiness and compassion;
If all is impermanent, or a self:
This way is to be meditated upon.
What use is there in saying much here?
Even if one desires the dharmadhātu,
One should meditate on the emanation of the Buddha
Who has the form of Ākāśagarbha.
Butchers, bamboo workers and the rest,
Wholly engaged in the business of killing,
All of them, in that delightful place,
Obtain liberation through the protectors who follow the path.
Surely, in the centers of the maṇḍalas,
One should meditate on Saṃvara in bodily form.
In this world and in the next,
Even one whose nature is delusion obtains accomplishment.
He whose nature is the union of all consciousnesses,
Whose sole essence is emptiness,
The Blessed One, dwelling in the yoga
Of the form of Prabhañjana, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Ākāśagarbha, the twentieth chapter.

Then the Goddess made offerings,
And having paid homage, made this request:
"O Blessed Vajra, please speak
Of the defining marks of self-awareness."
What arises from self-aware wisdom
I shall explain in due order.
Through the perfect union of glorious Heruka,
The yogic course of the moon:
From the first day of the bright fortnight onward,
The ocean of wisdom will increase.
On the first, wisdom will arise.
On the second, it is the form going in the sky.
On the third, it resembles the shape of an arrow.
On the fourth, it has the form of a target.
On the fifth, its nature is that of five peaks.
On the sixth, the six elements arise.
On the seventh, it is the form of the ocean.
On the eighth, it is declared to be a lotus.
On the ninth, it is the elixir.
On the tenth, it is through the coemergent form.
On the eleventh, it is the peaceful form.
On the twelfth, it is the form of a wheel.
On the thirteenth, it is the sun;
On the fourteenth, likewise, the moon.
The fifteenth is Rāhu,
And the sixteenth is called the bindu.
The defining marks of the sixteen aspects
Are to be known as self-awareness.
Through the union of glorious Heruka,
One should accomplish it in the four wheels.
There is no aging and there is no death;
Illness, leprosy and the rest are left behind.
At the navel it becomes the letter śrī;
The letter he is to be set at the heart;
The letter ru is in the wheel of the throat;
And at the crown of the head is the letter ka.
Through the perfect union of the four bindus,
It will be accomplished as self-awareness.
That suchness of sixteen aspects
Is the supreme undefiled state of the yogins.
Transferring from place to place,
It is to be led fully into the central channel.
Having reached the petals of the four wheels,
It is self-awareness, infinite and supreme.
That family is to be known
As having arisen from the yoginīs.
The sentient being, while awake,
Will attain a vision that is meaningful.
It always becomes a dream,
And surely abides at all times.
As the defining mark of enjoyment and emanation,
One who has fallen asleep sees it then.
The fourth is selflessness, abundant;
Therefore those are the ultimate enjoyment.
Thus, abiding in the four wheels,
One will surely go upon the path.
Having realized the suchness of wisdom,
With the sun, which has the suchness of means,
The union of the menstrual blood arises;
And whatever here is in the dark fortnight,
Of the portions of the sixteen,
The sun itself is in between.
From the union of emptiness and compassion,
The vajra blazes, blazes and blazes.
In the centers of the wheel of the maṇḍala,
One should imagine glorious Heruka.
Fully endowed with all the defining marks,
One will go to vajra joy.
He who truly unites all states,
The chief, whose nature is self-awareness,
The Blessed One, ever dwelling
At the limit of the fourth, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of glorious Heruka, the twenty-first chapter.

Then I shall fully explain
The feast of the deities of the body and the rest.
It arose from the perfect union
Of Padmanarteśvara himself.
The channels are truly renowned as the lotus;
They dance with the life wind.

The lord, through the awakening mind,
Dwells within them.
Self-awareness is to be eaten;
With the mouth of compassion it is to be drunk.
Sport with the nondual yoginī;
Those who follow illusion are accomplished.
With everything as its very nature,
One should eat without eating.
One should meditate on Padmanarteśvara,
And indicate him in due order.
The letter pa goes to the town of others;
All that is stable is the letter tma;
The letter nā is the yoga of what moves;
The letter rte has the deity of bliss;
The letter śva is the strength of ta and the rest;
The letter ra has the cause of joyful play.
In all of existence and nirvāṇa,
One should apply its perfect union.
All these words of the junctures
Are to be known in the Later Saṃvara.
Relying upon these courses,
Padmanarteśvara himself,
Heruka and Saṃvara himself,
Vajrasattva and the other chiefs:
That Padmanarteśvara too
Is to be known in all the unions.
The lotus is truly renowned as wisdom;
The Lord of Dance possesses consciousness;
The two arise as truly nondual;
All the deities arise here.
All the knowers abide in him;
In the three realms he is the Lord of Dance.
Upon the great body that dances,
The signs are then to be indicated.
Through union devoted to the sky,
One goes to what is supremely marvelous.
In the center of the wheel of yoginīs,
Sport in the coemergent form!
Through this, the rites of subjugation always,
And through them, delight is brought about.
For whatever rites belong to whichever ones,
One should meditate on him accordingly.
At the root of awakening of the practitioners,
All the buddhas come under their power.
Then, having vanquished Māra,
Dancing, he holds the lotus.
Utterly destroying all evil,
He establishes those who have attained greatness.
All desires will be accomplished,
And he purifies within the existence of the yogins.
Through one's own mind one obtains accomplishment;
It is not to be done as conceptual thought.
Otherwise, he whose nature is union with existence,
Who fully enters all minds,
Should fully enter the teachings
Of the deluded and wasted tīrthikas;
Having completely abandoned bad habitual tendencies,
He should afterward teach the true nature.
From the union of all eating,
He ever abides without eating.
The Blessed One, the Tathāgata
Who performs the lotus dance, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Padmanarteśvara, the twenty-second chapter.

Then I shall explain the defining marks
Of Vairocana: Goddess, listen!
Whatever wisdom transcends form,
That is called the great wisdom.
Likewise, that which is illuminating
Bears the name of the great being.
From all the tips of the pores
They emerge with great strength.
Having fully entered their openings,
They reach the lake-born lotus of the four wheels.
Just as butter poured into butter,
In the same way they enter the elements.
By stopping movement,
One will see everything to be like that.
What is seen is that very seeing;
Look at the face with the face!
The forms of men, women, gods and the rest
Are like water on a lotus petal.
That is to be known as the great life;
It surely emerges from the dead body.
Bring the letter oṃ to mind,
And indicate it through the movement of the breath.
By oneself, emanate the letter hūṃ,
Endowed with the yoga of reviving.
In the mind it becomes the letter oṃ;
Indicate it as abiding in the body.
Through the letter haṃ, the first of the supreme,
Emanate into the entities of wisdom.
In the center of the spheres of reality
Is the meditation of Vairocana.
Through the dripping streams of nectar,
The mantra practitioner is to be set ablaze.
Numbering thousands of koṭis,
The enjoyment of desires with the yoginī will come about.
Through the desires there is no emission;
If one wishes, one radiates bliss.
Then the yoginī, pleased,
Bestows all accomplishments.
Discriminating between the attractive and the unattractive
Is not to be done toward a yoginī.
Swiftly, the forms of thousands
Are to be accomplished as Vairocana.
The Dharma, in many various languages,
Is heard by those embodied beings.
Vairocana and Akṣobhya,
Ratneśa and Ārolik,
Amogha, Vajrasattva,
Vajradhara of the cause and the rest,
And the Perfection of Wisdom and the rest as well:
These are known from the yoginī.
He who liberates the six classes of beings
And leads them into the higher destinies,
The intelligent one, will bring about the benefit of each being
As the nondual entity.
For the sake of destroying ignorance and the afflictions,
This method too is to be meditated upon.
The sesame oil of all the elements
Is to be used without hesitation.
Through union with Vairocana it is to be drunk,
Or anointing is done with that very thing.
With it one should administer through the nose,
Always upon the vajra path.
One becomes the form of Vajradhara,
Delightful to the yoginīs.
From the complete union of all the consorts,
The yoginī grants the liberation one desires.
The Blessed One, the body purified of all,
Who fully liberates, proclaimed this.

This is the rite of Vairocana, the twenty-third chapter.

Then the great secret is to be explained,
In brief, not in detail.
Through the perfect union of Vajrasattva,
He truly abides in all places.
The indestructible vajra wisdom
Reveals the heart of the being.
Emptiness and compassion, inseparable,
Are renowned as Vajrasattva.
Through the perfect union of the moon vajra,
The course through the sacred sites is to be taught.
From Pullīra it descends to the navel.
At Jālandhara it truly abides.
At Oḍyana all conduct is accomplished.
At Arbuda there is eating and drinking.
At the site of Godāvarī there is play;
At Rāmeśvarī there are victory and defeat.
At Devīkoṭi there is favor;
At Mālava is the chief of the yoginīs.
At Kāmarūpa speech is accomplished.
At Oḍi direct accomplishment is bestowed;
At Triśakuna, for the faculties,
Accomplishment swiftly enters.
At Kosala is the accomplishment of yoga;
At Kaliṅga subtle accomplishment arises.
At Lampāka one will always be accomplished.
At Kāñcī is the accomplishment of killing.
At Himālaya there is accomplishment in harmony;
At Pretapurī, one's own view.
At Gṛhadevatā, permission is granted.
At Saurāṣṭra, the coemergent without emission.
At Suvarṇadvīpa one's own aim is accomplished.
At Nāgara the accomplishment of the site is bestowed.
At Sindhura, likewise, strength is accomplished;
At Maru, the accomplishment of deathlessness.
At Kulutā, as the accomplishment of the true nature,
That very thing arises in the yogins.

Having meditated constantly on Vajrasattva,
Bring forth all the accomplishments!
In the midst of the yoginīs of the sites and so on,
The great flavor that has become the elements
Should be known in the secret words.
The supreme is to be remembered as the coemergent;
Joy is to be known as wisdom,
The self of all, the abode of qualities,
Ever abiding, nondual in form.
For the work of sentient beings and the world,
And of those endowed with yoga as well,
Through the very nature of Vajrasattva
It arises truly from the midst of nāda,
And, dissolving into that very thing, it goes.
This is declared to be its abiding.
Thus all of this, according to the rite,
Should be known by the supreme heroes.
The nature of the twenty-four principles
Is explained to be power (śakti).
Through the full entry of all the powers
That have arisen and are arising,
In a form that is not nonexistent,
Free of the vowels and the three mothers,
The form of the bindu, the great being,
Is renowned as Vajrasattva.
From the union of all the heroes,
The lord of all the yoginīs,
The Blessed One who brings about the entry
Spoke this, the accomplishment of the site of illusion.

This is the twenty-fourth section, the one embodying all the sites.

Thus the hosts of rites of the chief hero
Should be known.
The delightful nature of the twenty-four,
In the site of the all-encompassing great secret,
Having the great secret explained at length:
I shall explain the rite of conduct.
The perception of the elements explained before
Is explained in sets of three.
The twenty-four, which have their triads,
Should be known as eights.
Of the twenty-four heroes,
Each one has twenty-four;
In the yoga of the circular form
The bindus themselves should be placed.
By circling them outside the maṇḍala,
Concentration becomes vajra.
In order, starting from the east,
They radiate to the vajra wheel and the rest.
In the maṇḍala all the deities
Should be known as all the yoginīs.
Five hundred and seventy-six
Transitions are to be made over the day and night.
The shift of breath for each one
Is thirty-seven and a half.
Twenty-one thousand six hundred
Should be known for a day and night.
The very thoughts of all embodied beings:
Their times proceed in this way.
For those who meditate on that yoga in truth,
The thought of the buddhas fully revolves.
All of these, the heroes,
In the twenty-four sites and so on,
Dissolving and enjoying as themselves,
Devour time and the hours.
Through performing such a rite,
What abides everywhere is not a self.
The hosts of yoginīs and the heroes
Are, in that order, "I" and "mine."
In all the buddha worlds
There is no domain of "I" and "mine."
Nirvāṇa as a mere matter of belief
Is taught to those who speak from treatises.
Through ignorance of suchness the world is deluded;
By abandoning suchness there is no accomplishment.
For yogins, in this king of tantras,
That, and its supreme wisdom,
Luminosity and illusion, I
Have explained to the world of illusion.

O! The coemergent nature is taught as one alone;
Homage to the one who conquers gods, asuras, and the three worlds!
Indra and worldly people do not know it;
Worship with the song of supreme great bliss!
Meditate on it in the form of all actions!
Why teach existence and nirvāṇa?
Through delusion, illusion abides like a painting.
Why do sentient beings still let the vehicle decline?

Thus the song of complete delight
Was sung by the Blessed One himself.
That very thing, together with the maṇḍalas,
Take up at the time of awakening.
From the union of all the heroes,
Vajrasattva, supreme in bliss,
Vajraḍāka, the Tathāgata,
The Blessed Lord, spoke this.

This is the fiftieth chapter, called "Embodying the Twenty-Five Principles, Teacher of the Meaning of All Tantras, All the Great Secrets," from the Great King of Tantras, the Glorious Ḍākārṇava Yoginī Tantra.

Then the yoginīs and heroes,
The retinue of many kinds,
Praised Vajraḍāka, the all-pervading lord,
The Blessed Lord:
"You are the way of great passion,
Of the nature of entities and nonentities,
A form of pure nature;
By teaching the dharmas you bring satisfaction.
Form of Dharma and non-Dharma,
In you, Dharma and its bearer are not two.
Through the things you yourself emanate
You bring benefit to all the worlds as well.
Great being, bestower,
Who leads the world into buddhahood,
You have taught the pledge (samaya);
It arises from the pledge itself and from nothing else.
By my cultivating merit,
I have set my mind on supreme awakening.
The Teacher abides in supreme bliss,
And by that he works for the benefit of sentient beings.
